# Open questions

The register. One row per question, ids monotonic, **never reused and never renumbered**
(`doc/workflow.md` section 3). A work item reserves a block before it starts and fills rows from it.

**Status values:** `open` · `deferred` (real, not now, with a reason) · `resolved` (with what
resolved it - an ADR, a spec section, or a plan record).

**Owner** is the work item or plan that will answer it, or `no owner` - which is honest and common
before the first plan exists.

**A leaning is not a decision.** Several rows below carry one, because starting a refinement pass
from a blank page wastes it. Every leaning states the argument against itself. Nothing here is
settled until an ADR says so.

---

*(Re-derived at [W85](work-items/w85-what-a-nested-region-carries-and-how-it-ends.md), **P22's

predecessor built closes no row, and says so rather than reaching for one. **278 / 217 / 2 / 59**,
by a parser written from scratch and wrong in TWO directions before it was right, both of them new:
it tracked which TABLE a row is in by SEARCHING for the row's own text, and a row's text can occur
above the heading it lives under, so forty-odd live rows classified as resolved and it ABORTED on
every one — correctly, and for a reason that was entirely its own. Then, reading the live table's
STATUS CELL to bucket a resolved question, it read `**resolved by ADR 0003**` as **open**, because
the cell begins with emphasis rather than with the word: **a classifier that is wrong without being
unclassifiable**, which is [Q204](#live)'s shape at the cell it reads rather than at the count it
prints. What decides is MEMBERSHIP of the Resolved table, and the live table keeps a stub row that
is a pointer. The nine wide rows [W84](work-items/w84-what-a-regions-identity-is.md) declared are
carried as declared exemptions and printed by id. **Q1, Q2, Q3, Q31 and Q32 have a resolved row and
no live stub**, which is established practice for the five oldest and not a finding. The headline
was CORRECT on arrival at 272 / 211 / 2 / 59 — the second time running, after W84's.)*

That is this line's own failure mode one column across, and it is why the count now names the table rather than a number of clusters - the old wording said *five* beside six rows. **Verification and fixtures** is the new row and takes Q44, Q45, Q98 and W17's two; Q3 and Q97 are filed into existing ones. **The two halves are checked separately** - every id has a row, and every row's id appears in exactly one cluster cell. **W18 re-derived it from the predicate and found the predicate itself needed one more clause to be countable in one pass.** A row here may be **several paragraphs long**, so *"its status cell"* means *the second-to-last cell of the row's **last cell-bearing line**, excluding table separators and headers* - without the exclusion the count reads a `|---|` as Q95's status, and without the *last line* part it reads the first paragraph and finds no cells at all. Both were hit while counting, in opposite directions, and neither is visible in a rendered table. **This register is read before every work item and checked by nothing**, which five plans have now observed and none has fixed.)* They are **not** equally hard, and treating them as a flat

### The join

| Q4 | *Resolved - see the Resolved table.* | **resolved by ADR 0003** | W4 |
| Q5 | **What does a join do when a branch does not succeed?** **Narrowed by [ADR 0004](decisions/0004-a-non-success-outcome-routes-only-where-an-edge-opts-in-and-user-action-suspends.md), which answered the prior half nobody had asked: whether a non-success outcome routes at all.** It does, but only through an edge that opts in (`on: ["failed"]`); `user_action` routes nowhere and **suspends**. That makes the four cases three region events (`spec/join-policy.md` section 2), and **two of the four turn out to be one**: an optional branch resolving `null` *is* `terminalState: 'failed'` - the same two lines of the same branch at `core/FyflowScheduler.ts:894-897`, and `resolve(null)` is invisible to a listener. **Half of it is answered by [ADR 0003](decisions/0003-a-join-is-satisfied-when-its-region-has-no-live-branches.md):** under `live-token` every one of the three terminal events decrements, so a join **proceeds** rather than failing or waiting - and W4's matrix shows the alternative was not "wait forever" as a policy but as an accident, on six of nine rows. What is still open is whether the choice should be **per-join configuration** rather than one global rule. Premature until [Q24](#live) produces a second policy that does not hang | open - **half answered by ADR 0003** | W4 |
| Q6 | *Resolved - see the Resolved table.* | **resolved 2026-09-02 - ADR 0030, and the answer needed no clause in any policy** | **W22** ([P7](plans/p7-is-a-join-transitive-over-nested-fan-out.md)) |
| Q23 | **May an edge leave a fan-out region for a node that is neither the join nor an `end`?** `FD-V13` currently refuses it, which keeps a routed-away branch from continuing to live outside the region and bounds P1's matrix to one live path per branch. **Against the refusal:** *"score, and if it is junk hand it to the quarantine pipeline"* is an ordinary flow, and it is refused today. Lifting it means a token can be outside a region while the join still waits, which multiplies Q5's rows rather than adding to them | open - refused in P1 | no owner |
| Q24 | *Resolved - see the Resolved table.* | **resolved 2026-08-31 - ADR 0025, and it is two answers rather than one** | W16 |
| Q27 | **May two branches of one fan-out converge on a shared node?** `FD-V19` refuses it. Without the refusal a shared node's `branchPath` is a property of the traversal rather than of the node (`spec/task-id.md` section 1), and two conformant implementations disagree on whether it dispatches once or once per branch - which the join then sees as one arrival or two. **Against the refusal:** *"score both ways, then merge, then join"* is an ordinary flow. Answering it is close to [Q12](#live)'s map-style fan-out, since both turn one node into many dispatches. **[P10](plans/p10-is-fan-out-one-feature-or-two.md) does NOT answer this and says so**: *"may two branch entries meet at a shared node"* is a question about the **static** feature, and P10's is about arity. **But ADR 0036 owes this row a sentence**, because `FD-V19` is the one rule both questions turn on — P10 measured a map's three-item encoding tripping `FD-V19` **three times** alongside `FD-V9`, which is this row's condition reached from the other side. **[ADR 0036](decisions/0036-a-map-is-a-second-node-kind-and-a-branch-path-inside-one-is-a-property-of-the-dispatch.md) ANSWERED *two features*, so this row is UNTOUCHED — and the sentence it owed corrects the framing above rather than merely discharging it.** *"A map is that condition on purpose"* is true of the **repeated-entry ENCODING** of a map and false of a `map` node: a map declares ONE branch, so `FD-V19`'s *"two distinct branch entries of the same fanout"* has **no instance** inside a map region — measured as `0` pairs iterated by [W32](work-items/w32-what-a-map-fanout-is.md), against `n(n-1)/2` for the encoding. **`FD-V19` is not lifted, not weakened and not reached**, and this row is exactly where P1 left it: a question about the STATIC feature, with no owner | open - refused in P1, **and unentangled from [Q12](#live) by ADR 0036** | no owner |
| Q7 | **Can a join time out, and what is the run's state if it does?** Without a timeout, any bug in Q4/Q5 becomes a permanently stuck run - and the scheduler will look **idle** while it happens, because a blocked task is removed from `queued` (`AGENTS.md`, the `idleTimeout: 0` deadlock note). With a timeout, we need a flow-level outcome that is neither `done` nor `failed`. **Detection is answered and it needs no clock** ([ADR 0003](decisions/0003-a-join-is-satisfied-when-its-region-has-no-live-branches.md), *Consequences*): W4 computed a stuck join from the log alone - every dispatched task has an outcome, no branch is parked on a person, and the join has not fired - so a timeout would put a wall-clock guess on top of an answer that is already certain. **What is left is whether a join should time out for some OTHER reason**, and that is a question about a run's patience rather than about its state. Where the predicate should live is [Q42](#live); what a stuck run is CALLED is [Q43](#live) | open - **detection answered, timeout open** | P1 |
| Q91 | *Resolved - see the Resolved table.* | **resolved 2026-08-31 — ADR 0025** | **W16** |
| Q92 | **`Cell.verdict` and `RunState.outcome` are two independent computations of `stuck` and exactly one row compares them.** `verdictOf` in `test/matrix/run.ts` reads the **driver's** public surface — `dispatchedIds`, `snapshot`, `terminalStateOf` — and never calls `fold()`; `spec/run-state.md` section 3's precedence reads the **fold's** input sequence and never sees the driver. **The matrix has printed `stuck` in a column since P1 with the fold never involved**, and [W15](work-items/w15-the-fourth-column-and-the-chain-nothing-runs.md)'s chain test is the first thing to assert the two agree — on `all-exit`, and nowhere else. **Half of this closed when W15 acted on its adequacy reader, and the half that closed is the one that was load-bearing.** The reader measured what *"`fold()` on two of thirty-six cells"* cost: the driver could stop appending region events to `foldInputs()` **for one policy id** and all 361 assertions passed, because `Cell.events` comes from `onRegionEvent` — a different channel — and nothing compared them. `test/matrix/chain.test.ts` now folds **all nine rows under all four columns**, asserting `RN-R6`'s `opened`/`settled`/`suspended` identical and `joined` differing (`all-exit` carries all three of `null`/`true`/`false`), and separately asserts that `dispatchedIds()`, what reached the scheduler, and `foldInputs()`' dispatches **all three agree on every one of the 36 cells**. **What is still open is the original question and only it: should a `Cell` itself carry `RunState.outcome`?** **For:** `verdict` and `outcome` would then be compared on 36 cells rather than beside them. **Against, and it is why W15 put the new suite beside the matrix rather than inside `Cell`:** `runCell` producing a `RunState` makes the L5 rung depend on `src/fold/**`, which is W2's, and W4 kept the matrix on the driver's surface deliberately so a fold defect could not make a policy cell look right | **deferred 2026-08-31 — real, not now.** The half that was load-bearing is closed and what remains is **a preference about where an assertion lives**, not a gap: `RN-R6` is folded across all 36 cells and the three dispatch channels are asserted to agree on every one, both beside the matrix. W4's reason for keeping `Cell` on the driver's surface — *a fold defect must not be able to make a policy cell look right* — is unrefuted, and nothing has been measured that it costs. **What would reopen it:** a defect that a per-cell `RunState` would catch and the two suites beside it would not | no owner |
| Q93 | *Resolved - see the Resolved table.* | **resolved 2026-08-31 — covered at L2, structurally uncoverable at L5, and that is right** | W15 |
| Q94 | *Resolved - see the Resolved table.* | **resolved 2026-08-31 — each ADR carries the table it decided from** | **W16** |
| Q96 | **Should `live-token-requiring-arrival` be what this library tells you to pass, rather than `live-token`?** [ADR 0025](decisions/0025-the-zero-arrival-join-is-reportable-and-arrival-counting-is-not-repaired.md) rule 4 keeps [ADR 0003](decisions/0003-a-join-is-satisfied-when-its-region-has-no-live-branches.md) rule 1 and says so in `AGENTS.md`: `liveToken` is still the recommendation and the newer policy is offered beside it. **For changing it:** on `all-exit` the recommended policy runs the join's successor on work that never happened and carries `null` into it, which ADR 0003 calls a real cost and which no flow author would predict from the words *"the join fired"*. **Against, and it is why the ADR did not:** the two are answers to a question about a **flow** — whether a fan-out whose branches all end elsewhere has finished or has failed — and nothing in a definition says which. Making the stricter one the default converts a completing run into a `stuck` one for everybody who was relying on the other reading, and **a run that stops is the more expensive mistake to make by accident.** **What would settle it: real flows.** This is not answerable from fixtures, and W15's matrix cannot help — every row in it was written to separate policies, not to be representative | open | no owner - **not before a real flow corpus exists** |
| Q97 | **`stuck` still does not distinguish a REPORTED dead join from a silently hung one, and [ADR 0025](decisions/0025-the-zero-arrival-join-is-reportable-and-arrival-counting-is-not-repaired.md) is what makes that reachable.** `withOutcome` maps `joined !== true` to `stuck`, so a run whose policy said `unsatisfiable` and a run whose join is waiting forever with nobody coming are the same `RunState.outcome`. The difference lives in `regions[*].joined` — `false` against `null` — and in whether `onUnsatisfiableJoin` fired, **and that callback is optional**. P5 measured this before any item started and it was theory: **no shipped policy could produce `false`.** One now can, so a consumer can reach the conflation with the recommended API and no test-tree policy. **For a seventh outcome or a new field:** *"the flow declared this join dead"* and *"nobody ever came"* are different operational facts and a dashboard cannot tell them apart. **Against:** `stuck` is one word for *"this run will not finish and no exception was thrown"*, which is true of both, and the distinguishing data is already in `regions` for anyone who looks. `spec/run-state.md` is **W2's** and is not in W16's `Owns`; **ADR 0026 was reserved for exactly this and deliberately not taken** — a run-state change is its own item with its own evidence | open | no owner - **ADR 0026 held for it** |
| Q98 | **The number every Record in this repository calls "assertions" is a count of TEST CASES.** `test/runner.ts:62` prints `passed + failures.length`, and `passed` is incremented once per `test()` in `test/harness.ts` — not once per `equal`, `ok` or `throws`. So *"386 assertions in five runtimes"* is 386 **cases**, and the actual assertion count is several times larger and has never been measured. **Every plan Record, `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md` and `doc/README.md` carries this number**, and four plans have carried the wrong noun for it. It is `doc/workflow.md`'s own *"a count in prose should state the predicate that produces it"* failing on the count those documents quote most often. **For fixing the runner:** an assertion count is the number those documents mean, and it would measure something the case count cannot — a suite gaining coverage inside existing cases. **For fixing the word instead:** it is one noun in every document, the case count is the more stable number, and changing what the runner prints silently rebases every historical figure in `doc/`. **Against doing either now:** nothing has been decided wrongly because of it — the number is used to detect *change*, and it detects change correctly either way. Found by [W16](work-items/w16-the-decision-and-what-it-ships.md) while re-deriving its own gate line rather than chaining P5's | open | no owner |
| Q95 | *Resolved - see the Resolved table.* | **resolved - W18**, with a stub. **Not a feature**: shipping an early-settling policy stays [Q5](#live)/[Q7](#live) | **W18** ([P6](plans/p6-what-may-share-a-scheduler-with-a-driver.md)) |
### Decisions and data

| Q | Question | Status | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q8 | **Does a decision see only its node's output, or accumulated run state?** Node-output-only keeps predicates trivially pure and cheap to test. Accumulated state is what real flows want (*"route to review if any earlier step flagged low confidence"*). **This row said accumulated state *"is what makes [Q19](#live) urgent, because it means the run retains every node's output rather than just the last"*, and that half is FALSE - the run already does.** Measured at [P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md)'s declaration: `src/driver/driver.ts` holds `private readonly log: LogRecord[] = []`, there is exactly **one** `this.log.push` and **no** `splice`, `shift` or reassignment in 1790 lines, `OutcomeRecord.result` is on every settled task, and `Driver.snapshot()` returns the whole of it publicly. An accumulator built over the log with **no new driver state** reproduces every result after the run finishes. **So a run-scoped accumulator is a PROJECTION of state that already exists, and this row's stated cost was paid before the row was written.** **And its mechanism is one field and one call site**: `DecisionContext` in `src/driver/routing.ts` has exactly one member, its own comment names this question by id, and it is constructed at `driver.ts:908` and nowhere else. *The decision is the whole cost.* **ANSWERED by [ADR 0041](decisions/0041-a-decision-sees-its-nodes-outcome-and-whatever-its-own-edge-declares.md): a decision sees its node's effective outcome and whatever its OWN EDGE declares.** `FD-C3`, and an **edge** may carry a `needs` — same grammar as a node's, **minus the `"."` form**, resolved from the same projection with no new state. **This row's fork is the wrong shape**: the answer is *node-output-only by default, and as much accumulated state as the edge names*, so its own argument for the first option — *keeps predicates trivially pure and cheap to test* — survives intact, because a predicate that declares nothing is byte-identical to a P1 predicate. **[P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md) prediction 3 said this would need no ADR and it REVERSED**, on [Q193](#live)'s measurement. **Decided AFTER [Q11](#live) rather than before it** - a predicate and a worker want the same thing, so answering this first would let *what is cheap to hand a predicate* decide what a worker receives | **resolved 2026-09-10 — [ADR 0041](decisions/0041-a-decision-sees-its-nodes-outcome-and-whatever-its-own-edge-declares.md)** | [W42](work-items/w42-what-a-decision-sees.md) ([P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md)) |
| Q9 | **Are decision predicates required to be pure, and how is that enforced rather than requested?** A predicate that calls the network or mutates run state breaks the fold and turns determinism (ladder L3) from a check into a coin flip. "Please write pure functions" is not a mechanism. Depends on Q2. **[P10](plans/p10-is-fan-out-one-feature-or-two.md) DECLINED this with a measurement rather than on scope, and it stays open**, which is the shape [W23](work-items/w23-a-nested-flow-on-real-workers.md) used for [Q90](#live). Three candidates, measured at P10's declaration: **freezing the argument** is already effectively done — the driver hands the predicate `cloneRecord(outcome)` and an assignment to a frozen `ctx` is refused — and it catches mutation of the context and **nothing else**; **evaluating twice and comparing** caught **1 of 4** impurity shapes (a counter differs; a clock, a network call and `Math.random()` all agree) and `Math.random()` agrees about half the time, so the detector is **flaky**, which is the one property `doc/concept.md` section 5 and `CLAUDE.md` both refuse; and **ladder L3, the rung this row cites, structurally cannot reach a predicate at all** — [Q155](#live). **So the honest answer today is that no mechanism worth its cost exists**, and that is recorded here rather than left to be rediscovered a fourth time | open - **declined with a measurement by P10** | no owner |
| Q10 | *Resolved - see the Resolved table.* | **resolved** | W1 |
| Q22 | **Must a node result be structured-cloneable, and is that a constraint on workers or on the driver?** `OR-R2` deep-copies `result` with `structuredClone` because it is the one outcome-record field whose *value* is a reference the producer may still hold - an inline pool returns the worker's own object. `OR-R6` makes a non-cloneable result a run failure. **Against:** it is a hot-path deep copy that [ADR 0007](decisions/0007-the-driver-normalizes-events-into-an-immutable-outcome-record.md) explicitly did not want, and it constrains what a worker may return in order to protect a log. The cheaper alternative - *a worker must not retain what it returns* - is the shape of answer Q9 already rejected as unenforceable. Q19 is what would price it  **[W34](work-items/w34-what-a-branch-receives-and-what-a-join-carries.md) supplies the measured run this row has asked for since P1, and it does not resolve the question.** [ADR 0037](decisions/0037-what-a-branch-receives-is-a-property-of-the-fan-out-and-what-a-join-carries-is-a-property-of-the-region.md) adds a SECOND hot-path deep copy — one per fan-out branch — on `OR-R2`'s own precedent (*a uniform boundary is cheaper to review than a documented exception*). **What it costs, at N = 1000 over a 117 KiB payload**: the shipped single edge-hop clone **1.50 ms**; a clone per branch **2207.94 ms**, a factor of **~1470** and **quadratic**, because a map's arity and its payload grow together; each instance receiving its own item **2.29 ms**, and a clone of that item **7.99 ms** — both **linear**. For a `fanout` over a 230 KiB payload, a clone per branch is **9.3 / 14.2 / 23.0 / 44.3 ms** at arity 2 / 3 / 5 / 10 against **4.5-4.8 ms** for one clone — **linear in a number the DOCUMENT declares**. A join collecting N results with one clone each is **3.47 ms**. **So the answer this row needs is not one number**: the same fix is affordable where the arity is authored and catastrophic where it is data, which is exactly the `fanout`/`map` split [ADR 0036](decisions/0036-a-map-is-a-second-node-kind-and-a-branch-path-inside-one-is-a-property-of-the-dispatch.md) found one layer down. **Still open**, and what it now needs is a decision about the CONSTRAINT rather than a measurement.  **[P15](plans/p15-what-a-run-retains-and-what-a-result-may-be.md)'s declaration OWNS it and corrects it twice.** **(1) The absolutes above do not reproduce, and the reason is W46's method finding applied to the row that quotes them.** Re-taken **one case per process** over 138.6 KiB: the single edge-hop clone **0.64 ms** against 1.50; a clone per map instance at N = 1000 **516.77 ms** against 2207.94; each instance its own item **0.02 ms** against 2.29; a clone of that item **1.54 ms** against 7.99; a `fanout` clone per branch at arity 2/3/5/10 **4.71 / 6.58 / 10.62 / 21.45 ms** against 9.3 / 14.2 / 23.0 / 44.3. **The conclusion is unchanged and the argument is stronger** — still ~**800x**, still quadratic for a map and linear in a document constant for a `fanout` — and every absolute is 2-5x lower, which is what [W46](work-items/w46-what-a-reference-costs-and-whether-anything-says-so.md) measured accumulation in one process to be worth. *A register row quoting an absolute is quoting a method.* **(2) The cloneability boundary is not where this row draws it, and the interesting case is SILENT.** Measured over ten shapes: a function, an object *holding* one, a `Symbol`, a `Promise` and a `WeakMap` are **refused**; a plain object, a `Map`, a `Date` and **a cyclic object** are cloneable — and so is **a class instance, which arrives as a plain `Object` with its prototype and methods gone, `instanceof` false, and NO error and NO report.** `OR-R6` makes the loud case a run failure; nothing anywhere is a rule about the quiet one, and it is the case a worker author actually writes. **And the retention is load-bearing for the library's own public answer**: over both corpora **110 of 124 task nodes (89%)** are named by no `needs`, so *drop a result no reference can name* is decidable from the document — and it is **not free**, because blanking every `result` takes the fold's input from 13984 B to 1612 B **and changes `RunState`**: `nodes[taskId].result` *is* the retained result **ANSWERED by `OR-R11` in `spec/outcome-record.md` v0.9 ([W54](work-items/w54-what-a-result-may-be-and-what-bounding-it-would-cost.md)), and the fork this row draws is the wrong shape.** It is the **DRIVER's** constraint — `OR-R2` unchanged, and the alternative is [Q9](#live)'s rejected shape — but the question an author actually hits is not *may I return this* and it is **what arrives**. **`OR-R11`: a result is a VALUE, not an OBJECT.** Its fields cross the clone; its BEHAVIOUR does not — prototype, methods, accessors and `instanceof` — silently and by design. **And the boundary has THREE answers where this document named two**: REFUSED (a function, an object holding one, a `Symbol`, a `Promise`, a `WeakMap`), cloned FAITHFULLY (a plain object, an array, a `Date`, a `Map`, a `Set`, a `RegExp`, a typed array, a `BigInt`, `undefined`, and a **cyclic** object whose cycle survives), and cloned **LOSSILY** (a class instance with a method, with a getter, frozen, `Object.create(proto)`, and one NESTED inside a plain object). `spec/outcome-record.md` said `structuredClone` *throws on a class instance with behaviour* and that sentence is **measured FALSE** — corrected in place. **Asserted in all five runtimes**, which is prediction 3's own stated way of being wrong checked rather than assumed. **NO ADR**, on `doc/decisions/README.md`'s *a spec rule with a rationale paragraph is better than a spec rule plus an ADR restating it*: ADR 0047 is SKIPPED and not reused. **Prediction 3's CONCLUSION is confirmed and its stated REASON reverses** — see [Q238](#live). **And the re-taken numbers keep their METHOD** ([Q190](#live)'s precedent), one case per process: the single edge-hop clone **0.58 ms**, a clone per map instance at N = 1000 **499.16 ms**, each instance its own item **0.02 ms**, a clone of that item **1.68 ms**, a `fanout` clone per branch **2.61 / 3.71 / 5.91 / 11.79 ms** at arity 2/3/5/10. **The SHAPE and the ratio class reproduce; the ABSOLUTES do not** — this row's own 1.50 / 2207.94 were accumulated in one process, P15 re-took them at 0.64 / 516.77 and this reads 0.58 / 499.16, with the `fanout` row **1.8x** from P15's on the same fixture and the same method. *So [Q179](#live) binds harder than one-process-versus-many: quote the shape and the ratio, never the millisecond.*| **resolved 2026-09-11 — `OR-R11`, `spec/outcome-record.md` v0.9** | [W54](work-items/w54-what-a-result-may-be-and-what-bounding-it-would-cost.md) ([P15](plans/p15-what-a-run-retains-and-what-a-result-may-be.md)) |
| Q11 | **What does a node receive as its payload?** The previous node's output, a run-scoped accumulator, or a declared per-edge mapping. This is the API surface users touch most, so it is the one most expensive to change later. Interacts with Q8 (same retention cost) and with ADR-to-be on redaction if Q21 lands. **[P10](plans/p10-is-fan-out-one-feature-or-two.md) took it NARROWED to the fan-out edge and did NOT settle it in general.** **The fan-out edge is now ANSWERED** by [ADR 0037](decisions/0037-what-a-branch-receives-is-a-property-of-the-fan-out-and-what-a-join-carries-is-a-property-of-the-region.md) and `JP-R11`: a `fanout` branch entry receives the value the fanout node received **as its own copy**, and a `map` instance receives **its own item** as its own copy. `spec/flow-definition.md` section 3 now also states what an **ordinary** edge carries — the source node's effective-outcome `result`, deep-copied — which no document in `spec/` had ever said and which is the first of Q11's three candidates written down at last. **What stays OPEN is the other two candidates**: a run-scoped accumulator and a declared per-edge mapping are both unbuilt, and neither is reachable from a fan-out. See [Q166](#live) for the cost a map instance pays under the answer that was taken **[P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md) TAKES THE GENERAL QUESTION** and starts from three blocked flows rather than from a mechanism - [Q166](#live)'s map that loses its context, [Q165](#live)'s `join -> map`, and [Q8](#live)'s *route to review if any earlier step flagged low confidence*. **Its key problem is measured**: a node id names one output outside a map region and **N inside one**, which is [ADR 0036](decisions/0036-a-map-is-a-second-node-kind-and-a-branch-path-inside-one-is-a-property-of-the-dispatch.md)'s deciding fact for the third time **ANSWERED IN GENERAL by [ADR 0040](decisions/0040-what-a-node-can-reach-is-the-runs-own-outputs-and-a-node-id-names-them-relative-to-a-branch-path.md), and the answer is all three candidates at once — the previous node's output is the DEFAULT, a run-scoped accumulator is the STATE, and a declared per-edge mapping is the ACCESS.** This row presents them as a fork; they are a stack, and only the third can reach a worker at all, because `createTask` takes ONE value. `spec/flow-definition.md` v0.11 section 3 is `needs` — an optional object on a `task` or a `map` mapping payload keys to references, where a reference is `"."`, a node id, or a one-element array holding one. **A node that declares none receives exactly what it received at v0.10**, measured at 574 L1-L5 cases unmoved. The rules are `FD-C1` and `FD-C2`, and what NAMES an output is the pair `(node id, branchPath)` | **resolved 2026-09-09 — [ADR 0040](decisions/0040-what-a-node-can-reach-is-the-runs-own-outputs-and-a-node-id-names-them-relative-to-a-branch-path.md)** | **W41** ([P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md)) |

### Identity, state, failure

| Q | Question | Status | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|

| Q15 | **Is a run resumable across a process restart, and is that in the first set?** Non-goal for now (concept section 7). The question stays live because the *cost of the door closing* is what Q14 is buying, and if Q2 lands on closures the door is already shut. `../fyflow-scheduler/PERSISTENCE_ANALYSIS.md` rates scheduler-side persistence *medium to high*; ours is cheaper only if the fold holds | deferred - first set is in-memory | no owner |
| Q16 | **Flow-level retry and task-level `retryPolicy` - who owns what, and do they compose?** The scheduler retries an *attempt* of a task. Retrying a *node* - possibly routing elsewhere on the second try - is a different thing, and if both exist the effective retry count is a product nobody intended. Also decides whether an attempt is in the task id (Q13). **The TASK half LANDED at [W58](work-items/w58-what-a-node-says-about-its-own-task.md)** (`spec/flow-definition.md` v0.16): a node declares `retryPolicy` in exactly the scheduler's shape, the driver passes it through, the scheduler retries inside the same task id and the outcome record's `attempts` carries it — the first non-zero `attempts` a scheduler has set at ladder L6. **The FLOW half stays open**: nothing re-dispatches a node under a new attempt, the id's fourth segment is untouched ([Q111](#live)), and the composition this row warns about therefore cannot occur yet | open — task half landed at W58 | P1; the flow half has no owner |
| Q17 | *Resolved - see the Resolved table.* | **resolved** | W4 |
| Q25 | *Resolved - see the Resolved table.* | **resolved** | W2 |
| Q61 | **`OR-R6` is reachable from an inline pool and not from a thread pool, and no document says so.** Measured by [P2](plans/p2-the-non-success-half-on-a-real-scheduler.md) before W6 was declared, with a worker returning `{ fn: () =&gt; 1 }`: **inline**, the value reaches the driver intact and *our* `structuredClone` at the listener boundary refuses it - `OR-R6`, `onRunFailure`, run `stopped`; **threaded**, the scheduler refuses it first, because the value cannot cross the worker boundary, and the task ends `user_action` with *"() =&gt; 1 could not be cloned"* with no result ever reaching us. **So one worker bug produces two different failures depending on a pool option the flow author does not control**, and `OR-R6` can be exercised only inline. **Against saying anything:** it is correct behaviour on both sides and neither library is wrong. **Against silence:** `spec/outcome-record.md` presents `OR-R6` as a property of results, and it is a property of results *and the pool that produced them* - a consumer debugging the threaded case will not find `OR-R6` and will not find the scheduler's message documented either | open | no owner |
| Q62 | **Is `regions[*].suspended` cumulative or current, and does `spec/` care?** `RN-R6` names it as one of the three fields `regions` carries and says nothing about its lifetime. The fold clears it on the branch's terminal event, so it is **current** - `{ '1': suspended }` while a person is being waited on, `[]` once they answer. W6 asserts the code's behaviour on two real rows and does not make it normative. **Against specifying it:** nothing has needed the other reading, and a rule with no consumer is a rule that constrains a future feature for no reason. **Against silence:** the array is in the published shape, a consumer will read it, and *"has anyone ever been suspended in this region"* is a question someone will ask of a field that cannot answer it. Found by W6's conformity reader | open | no owner |
| Q63 | **`UncloneableResult`'s message begins with its own class name, so a `${kind}: ${message}` render doubles it.** Observed at ladder L6, which is the first thing in the repository to render one: `UncloneableResult: UncloneableResult: the result of task "..." is not structured-cloneable`, and the same doubled string lands in `RunState.stoppedBy`. `src/driver/listener.ts:85`, and the same shape in `driver.ts`'s other refusals. **Against fixing:** the message is self-describing when logged alone, which is how a caller catching the error sees it. **Against leaving it:** `RunFailure` carries `kind` *and* `message` precisely so a caller can render them together, and every caller that does gets this. Found by W6's conformity reader | open | no owner |
| Q26 | **Does the driver log non-settlement events at all, and in what shape?** `OR-R4` says `task.running` and `task.progress` produce no outcome and no resolution record, and defers the rest. [ADR 0007](decisions/0007-the-driver-normalizes-events-into-an-immutable-outcome-record.md) rule 5 nonetheless requires `task.progress` to get the same listener-boundary treatment, so if it is ever logged the copy rules already apply. **Against logging them:** the log is what run state folds from, and progress is not a state transition - it would grow the log by an unbounded factor for a value only a UI wants (Q19, Q21) | open | W3 |
| Q28 | **What may a resolution do?** `RS-R2` allows exactly one thing: supply an outcome for a dispatch that already happened. The two obvious wants are refused - *"run that node again"* is flow-level retry (Q16) and would have to mint a new task id, and *"send it to node X instead"* is a second routing mechanism competing with the definition, which is the thing [ADR 0001](decisions/0001-routing-executes-in-the-driver-outside-the-scheduler.md) rule 3 exists to prevent. Both are real requests a consuming application will make | open | no owner |
| Q29 | **Is a resolution's `actor` interpreted, and who is allowed to resolve?** Today it is an opaque string the library never reads and never authenticates - which is honest for a library and useless for an audit trail. Authorization belongs to the consuming application; **attribution** arguably belongs in the log, since the log is the only record of why a run resumed. **Against doing more:** any answer richer than a string starts specifying an identity model this library has no business owning | open | no owner |
| Q30 | **May an edge opt in to `user_action`, bypassing suspension?** `FD-V18` refuses it. *"If this node needs a human, route it to the manual-review sub-flow"* is a reasonable thing to want and would be one value in an array. **Against:** it gives one concept two mechanisms - a node in `user_action` would be sometimes parked and sometimes routed, and no reader of the definition could tell which without evaluating the edges | open - refused in P1 | no owner |

| Q44 | **Should `spec/examples/` carry fixtures for runtime behaviour, or only for definition validation?** The corpus cannot express an `optional` branch node with no `on: ["failed"]` edge - the only shape that reaches `branch.lost` **by settling** rather than by a human resolving - nor two sequential fan-outs sharing an `end` - so W4 defines **three** flows in `test/matrix/` instead. That is either correct (the corpus is L4's, and behaviour fixtures belong to whoever exercises them) or a gap that will be re-found by every later item. **~~W5 is evidence for the current answer~~ - that reading was wrong, and W6 made it more wrong.** This row claimed W5's runs *"needed no local flow at all"*. W5 committed `examples/flows/optional-loss.flow.json`, and its own `l6.ts` comment calls it the **second** occurrence after W4's. W6 then added **three** more - `suspend-resume`, `uncloneable` and `exit-last` - because the corpus has no flow whose branch is a non-optional task that fails, none whose task returns a value `structuredClone` refuses, and none where the exiting branch can be made to settle last. **The trigger this row named has now fired four times in three consecutive items**, which is the answer it was waiting for: behaviour fixtures are being re-invented per item, in three different directories. Corrected by W6's conformity reader, which found the register asserting the opposite of a file W5 had committed. **What is still unanswered** is whether a corpus flow should carry the runtime expectations that go with it: W5's six rows live in `examples/l6.ts` and W4's eighteen cells in `test/matrix/`, and neither is reachable from the flow file a reader opens | open - **narrowed by W5** | no owner |
| Q45 | **Two sequential fan-outs sharing one `end` node: should `spec/` refuse the shape, support it explicitly, or leave it as it is?** It is legal today - **and since [W20](work-items/w20-what-a-nested-region-is.md) retired `FD-V15` it is legal outright rather than by omission.** `end` nodes are exempt from `FD-V19` and `FD-V22` from `FD-V13`, `FD-V19` and `FD-V22`, so one `end` can belong to two regions. The driver handles it, by attributing a terminal event to the region the branch was **opened in** rather than the one containing the node - and until W4's test-adequacy pass **that had no test in any of the twelve suites**, while the code carried a comment describing the regression it prevents. Reverting either line produces no throw and no `onRunFailure`: the second join never fires and the run stops. **Against refusing it:** sequential fan-out is an ordinary flow shape and the driver already gets it right. **Against leaving it silent:** a rule nothing states is a rule the next `end`-node exemption will break. **W5 changed what the failure looks like without touching the shape:** the silent hang it produces now folds to `stuck` rather than to `completed` ([ADR 0015](decisions/0015-a-run-whose-join-can-no-longer-fire-is-stuck.md)), so the case is *reportable* even while the definition rule stays unwritten. That lowers the cost of leaving it open; it does not answer it | open - **cost lowered by ADR 0015** | no owner |

| Q36 | *Resolved - see the Resolved table.* | **resolved 2026-09-07 - the arrived branches' results, keyed by `branchPath`** | **W34** ([P10](plans/p10-is-fan-out-one-feature-or-two.md)) |
| Q37 | **Who asks whether a join is stuck, and how often?** `Driver.joinStatusOf(fanoutId)` is the whole of stuck-join detection today (`spec/join-policy.md` section 4), and it is a **poll**: something has to decide to call it. Neither pinned policy ever returns `unsatisfiable`, so a genuinely stuck join is silent until someone asks. **Against a push model:** a driver that notices staleness needs a clock, and a clock in the driver is the thing that makes [Q7](#live)'s timeout question real rather than deferrable. **Measured by [W15](work-items/w15-the-fourth-column-and-the-chain-nothing-runs.md) and deliberately not fixed** (`src/driver/**` is not P5's): on `all-exit` - the one row in this repository where a join demonstrably can never fire - **neither shipped policy calls `onUnsatisfiableJoin` at all**, and `joinStatusOf('split')` under `arrival-count` answers `waiting` forever, which is indistinguishable from a branch that has not finished. The callback is also *optional* in `DriverOptions`, so the whole seam is opt-in. **What narrows the question:** `fold(driver.foldInputs()).outcome` says `stuck` on that same run with **no poll, no callback and no clock** (`RN-R12`), so what Q37 is actually asking is whether the driver owes a signal the fold already supplies - and a consumer who folds incrementally through `onFoldInput` is pushed to, already | open - **narrowed by W15** | W4 |
| Q38 | **Is a run that a refusal stopped resumable, or is it terminal?** `OR-R10` marks a run failed and stops routing it; nothing un-marks it. For `ID-R1` and `FD-R1` that is right — both mean the driver's own bookkeeping is wrong. For `OR-R6` (an uncloneable result) and for a policy that threw, a consuming application might reasonably fix the cause and continue. Interacts with [Q15](#live) (durability) and [Q28](#live) (what a resolution may do) | open | no owner |
| Q39 | **Does the library ship a reference join policy for testing?** W3 ships none deliberately — a default would answer [Q4](#live) from inside the driver. But every consumer, and W5's real run, needs *some* policy to make a fan-out flow complete, and a stub written fresh each time is a stub written wrong each time. **Against shipping one:** whatever ships becomes the de-facto default, which is exactly what `JP-R7` and `JP-R8` guard against. Perhaps only after W4 has answered | open | after W4 |
| Q99 | **The L6 harness's timing arrangement is a load-bearing test input that nothing asserts, and only one of its four knobs has been closed.** `buildScheduler`'s `SETTLES_LAST` set decides which *driver code paths* ladder L6 can reach, not merely how fast it runs: [W6](work-items/w6-the-non-success-rows-at-l6.md) added it because the arriving branch always settled last, so `settleJoin` on the `lost` and `exited` paths was unreachable across twelve rows. **[W17](work-items/w17-the-all-exit-row-at-l6.md) measured what that arrangement was worth to a reader who changes one word**: swap which role gets the long delay and `settleJoin` can be deleted from the **`lost`** path with all 336 assertions passing — W6's original defect restored exactly — while `exit-last` drops from four failures to none against its deletion from the **`exited`** path. **Closed for that knob**: `Expectation.joinSettledBy` now asserts which region event satisfied each arranged row's join, and reversing the set fails exactly the two rows whose arrangement it is. **Still open for the other three, and W17's adequacy reader found the residue is worse than *unasserted*: `delayMs` is INERT.** Emptying `SETTLES_LAST`, or reducing its 4x multiplier to 1x, leaves all 342 assertions green — so the settling order `optional-loss` and `exit-last` depend on is produced by **inline dispatch order**, not by the delay, and three comments attributed it to the delay until W17 corrected them. `joinSettledBy` pins the **effect**, which is right, and cannot tell which mechanism produced it. The delay stops being belt-and-braces the moment either pool becomes threaded. The other two knobs are unasserted in the ordinary way: `maxThreads` is 2 for one pool and 1 for the rest and can be set to 1 with the gate green; `threaded` can be ignored entirely, or flipped on the `all-exit` row, with the gate green; `delayMs: 5` can be 60. `inline` is what makes `OR-R6` reachable at all ([Q61](#live)). **For a general mechanism:** the class is now measured rather than suspected, and it is the shape that produced W6's finding, W17's, and `branchesOverlapped` being reported rather than asserted. **Against one:** the honest assertion for `inline` and `threaded` is a *timing* property the spec does not fix, which `doc/workflow.md` section 5 rule 5 forbids asserting — `joinSettledBy` works only because *which event satisfied the join* is a causal fact rather than an ordering one, and the other knobs may have no such proxy. **And the `delayMs` measurement cuts both ways**: a knob that changes nothing is a knob whose assertion would also assert nothing, so the useful question is narrower than *"assert every knob"* — it is **which knob is currently the sole cause of a reachable code path**, and today the answer is none of the four. Raised by W17, which found it by breaking a rule rather than a fix (`doc/workflow.md` section 5 rule 8) | open — **one knob closed, three open** | no owner . **Re-measured at [W67](work-items/w67-a-flow-from-documents-on-real-workers.md)'s declaration step, now that the delay is in the registry DOCUMENT rather than in a `SETTLES_LAST` set: forcing every un-pinned type INLINE costs ZERO, and flattening the four-times delay costs ZERO** — the knob and the multiplier are both held down by no assertion, which is why `l6.ts` asserts the derivation's BOUND and its DIRECTION instead of its effect ([Q290](#live))|
| Q100 | **Ladder L6's rendered table is a deliverable no assertion reads, which is [W15](work-items/w15-the-fourth-column-and-the-chain-nothing-runs.md)'s renderer finding in a second file and therefore a second occurrence.** W15's four-column matrix render was produced by a script `npm run gate` never executes and survived **seven** mutations, one of which printed a two-column table with the whole gate green; the response was to make the render a function the suite asserts field by field. **`examples/l6.ts`'s table is executed** — it is in the file the gate runs — **and nothing asserts a character of it**, so the two cases differ in *how* the output escapes checking and not in whether it does. **[W24](work-items/w24-the-gates-own-wiring.md) adds a sixth file and then CLOSES its instance, which is the first time an occurrence of this row has been closed rather than recorded.** `scripts/check-source.mjs`'s per-check report was a render nothing read: `c.slice(0, 2)` took the id from a row label, so `S10` and `S11` both read as `S1` and **a failing run printed `ok` on every one of its thirteen rows above four listed failures** — and W24's conformity reader then measured that *reverting the repair cost 0 probes*, and that the whole `checks` array could be emptied for 0. The fix is not an assertion **about** the render: every probe now requires the report's `FAIL` rows and the failure lines to name **the same set**, so the render is a second projection of a fact the suite already checks and disagreeing with it is what fails. **That is available here because the render and the assertion are in the same process**; `examples/l6.ts`'s table and W15's matrix render are printed by a run whose values no assertion re-reads, which is why this closes one instance and not the class. W17 found it the way L7 is supposed to be found, by a person reading the output: a hard-coded 40-column key width silently truncated `p1-routed-away LOW / live-token-requiring-arrival` to `p1-routed-away LOW / live-token-requirin`, so the human artifact named a run that does not exist. **Fixed narrowly** — the width is derived from the keys — **and the class is untouched**: every `console.log` in that file can be deleted, or made to print any value at all, with 341 assertions passing. **For asserting it:** L7 is the only rung a person runs and section 4 says never to spend a person on what an L≤6 assertion could catch. **Against:** the table is explicitly a *by-product* of the values the assertions already use (`examples/l6.ts`'s header), so asserting it re-checks the same numbers through a formatter, and the one thing it would have caught here is a formatting bug rather than a wrong value. **What would settle it:** whether a formatting bug can make the table say something FALSE rather than merely ugly — this one did, by naming the wrong run. **[W19](work-items/w19-a-task-we-did-not-mint-on-a-real-scheduler.md) adds two measured occurrences, and the second is a new sub-case.** Its `Outputs` row (4) asked for L6's counts *"derived rather than typed"*, and after deriving them: un-deriving `l6.ts`'s printed run count back to a literal **survives** with the whole gate green, so the header can read *19 real runs* for a run of 21. And `examples/README.md`'s three counts - assertions, runs, row labels - can be replaced with *"999 assertions over 4 real runs"* and *"3"* row labels, with `test:l6`, `check-docs` and `lint` all green. **A markdown file cannot derive a count at all**, so *"derived rather than typed"* is unachievable there rather than merely unachieved, and W19's `Outputs` table records that half as a gap rather than as a yes **A third and fourth occurrence, measured by [W29](work-items/w29-the-two-self-tests-that-grade-nothing.md) and CLOSED in both.** `check-docs` and `check-scheduler` each print a per-check report block that **no probe read**: deleting a row from the `checks` array, and forcing the verdict column to `ok` for every row, were both invisible with every probe green — `wanted [X2] got [X2] exit 1` and `wanted [D3] got [D3] exit 1`. A mutation to how failures are DISPLAYED changes no failure id, so no set comparison can see one. | Q101 | **`AGENTS.md`'s executed error-class snippet was a closed list compared against a copy of itself, so it omitted an exported class with the whole gate green.** Section 9's ` ```ts run ` block imported **eight** error classes, mapped them to `.name`, joined them, and threw unless the result matched a **string literal in the same snippet**. `InvalidTaskId` is exported from the package root (`index.ts:4` re-exports `src/driver/ids.ts`; measured through the entry point) and was in neither half, so the check agreed with itself and passed. `InvalidRunId`, from the same file, **was** in it — so this is not `ids.ts` being excluded wholesale, it is one name missing from a hand-written list nothing compared to anything. **This is a third form of `doc/workflow.md`'s standing lesson** — *rot is what the gate prevents, absorption is what it cannot see*: a list the gate **executes** is not a list the gate **checks**, and the executed form is more convincing than prose while being no more true. **Fixed by [W18](work-items/w18-the-scheduler-edge-and-what-an-id-we-did-not-mint-means.md)**: the snippet now enumerates the package's own `Error` subclasses with `Object.entries(lib).filter(…Error.isPrototypeOf…)` and compares the documented list to **that**, both ways — a class in `src/` and not in section 9 fails, and a name in section 9 that no longer exists fails. The question W24's Record must answer is whether an owner **closes** this or merely **moves** it. **ANSWERED by [W24](work-items/w24-the-gates-own-wiring.md): an owner MOVES it, NARROWS it and MULTIPLIES it, and it does not close it.** *Moves*: `Owns` is per-item, not standing, so `scripts/**` has an owner for the length of [P8](plans/p8-what-can-be-deleted-with-the-gate-green.md) and for no longer; the next plan that edits the gate chain either edits `scripts/check-source.mjs` without a late exception or reproduces this row with the check in force, and **that is the measurement to take** rather than an argument to have. *Narrows*: `S12` is set equality and fires in both directions on the commit that breaks it, so W24's own obligation cannot go stale the way a floor does — measured by the probe that SWAPS an entry, which leaves the count unmoved and the set different. *Multiplies*: W24 ships **three more floors of exactly this shape** — `MIN_PROBES`, `MIN_META`, `MIN_SELF` in `scripts/check-source-selftest.mjs` — and only the first two are made to **fire**, by a list one level out. `MIN_SELF` lowered together with emptying `SELF` survives, and is recorded in that file's ceiling list rather than guarded. **`RUN_FLOOR` itself is untouched and still in nobody's standing `Owns`.** W24's conformity reader reached the same three-part answer independently before reading the author's **[W29](work-items/w29-the-two-self-tests-that-grade-nothing.md) measured something this row does not say, and it makes the ratchet more load-bearing rather than less.** `RUN_FLOOR` is not only a floor on the reference's snippet count: it is **what actually reports when four OTHER clauses go inert**. | Q18 | **What does cancelling a run actually do?** The scheduler **cannot cancel a queued task** (`AGENTS.md` section 1, not implemented), so cancellation can stop *routing* but not stop already-dispatched work from running. It would not, and the correction is [W12](work-items/w12-the-region-event-a-listener-cannot-corrupt.md)'s conformity reader catching a register entry that was wrong on the day it was written** - the [Q43](#live) failure mode again: `emit` steps with `e` and then appends `Object.freeze({...e})`, a *different* object, and `e` is an unreachable literal after `emit` returns. So a mutating policy corrupts nothing today; what it does is break `JP-R1` silently, and the question is whether that should be loud. One `Object.freeze`, and the real question is whether an impure policy should fail **loudly** or is simply out of contract. Raised rather than done quietly: W12 was declared as a conformity fix with nothing to decide, and this is a decision | open | no owner |

| Q87 | **Nothing enforces that run ids are unique across the drivers sharing one scheduler, and `ID-R4` cannot see the violation.** **Measured by both of W13's readers, independently**: two `Driver`s constructed with the *same* `runId` on one scheduler mint the same task ids, so `dispatched.has()` is true in both, `ID-R4` is not violated, and one settlement produces **two** dispatches of the successor with nothing reported - [Q72](#live)'s corruption verbatim, for the one case ADR 0023 does not close. **Re-measured 2026-08-31 and this row understated it in two ways.** The corruption does not begin at a settlement: the *second* `start()` dispatches `run-1:ingest:-:0` a second time, before any event arrives, and on a real scheduler that is `addTask` overwriting silently. And one settlement of a fan-out's predecessor produced **four** dispatches, not two - each driver routing both branches - with `hasFailed()` false on both and `onRunFailure` never called. **Against fixing in the driver:** no driver can detect it - `SchedulerLike` has two methods and neither asks who else is subscribed ([ADR 0013](decisions/0013-the-scheduler-is-a-development-dependency-reached-from-the-working-tree.md) rule 1 is why). A registry of live run ids would be global state in a library that has none. **Against leaving it:** `MultipleRuns`' message tells a caller to construct a second `Driver`, and the obvious second one is for the same workload. Interacts with [Q20](#live) and [Q34](#live) - if run ids were generated rather than supplied, this would not arise.

**Ruled on by [P5](plans/p5-a-third-and-fourth-policy-or-repairs-of-the-first-two.md) and the ruling splits this row in two, which is why it has one owner and one non-owner.** The **enforcement** half stays a **precondition** and is not being fixed: no driver can detect the violation (`SchedulerLike` is two methods by [ADR 0013](decisions/0013-the-scheduler-is-a-development-dependency-reached-from-the-working-tree.md) rule 1), the alternative is a `WeakMap<SchedulerLike, Set<runId>>` — **the first mutable module-level state in `src/`** — and a consumer can audit it in their own two lines. **The message sentence is a one-line defect and is owned**: `MultipleRuns` reads *"Construct a second Driver; ADR 0023 makes that safe on the same scheduler"* and does **not** say *with a different run id*, so the library's own error recommends the violation. Verified verbatim 2026-08-31 at `src/driver/driver.ts:350`.

**The message half is FIXED by W18 and the row stays open on its enforcement half, which is the point of the split.** `MultipleRuns` now reads *"Construct a second Driver WITH A DIFFERENT RUN ID… Run ids must be unique across the drivers sharing one — nothing enforces that and nothing can see it violated"*, and it is asserted **on the text** rather than on the class: `throws(…, 'MultipleRuns')` matches the name and would have passed with the sentence still wrong, which is how the defect survived two plans. `AGENTS.md` section 9 states the one remaining condition rather than two. **The enforcement half is not fixed and is not being fixed**, on P5's argument, which P6 adopted and W18 did not re-derive | open — **the message is FIXED (W18); the precondition stands** | **W18** ([P6](plans/p6-what-may-share-a-scheduler-with-a-driver.md)) took the message; the enforcement half has **no owner** |
| Q90 | **`ID-R4` tier 2 has no L6 coverage, and the two-driver row cannot give it any.** [W14](work-items/w14-two-runs-two-drivers-one-scheduler-at-l6.md)'s negative test measured it: deleting **tier 1** fails the pair row (7 failures), deleting **tier 2** leaves it **green**, because a foreign settlement returns at tier 1 and never reaches tier 2. Tier 2 needs a settlement carrying *this* run's id for a task the driver never dispatched, and a real scheduler produces one only if something **injects** it - which no L6 row does. It has L2 coverage (three shapes × three terminal states, `test/driver/lifetime.test.ts`) and that is the whole of it. **For closing it:** an injected `FyflowTask` with a crafted id is a few lines, and this repository's own record is that fixtures-versus-reality is where it has been wrong. **Against:** injecting a task the driver did not mint is a scenario no consumer produces on purpose, so the row would be testing the refusal rather than the system. Raised rather than built because W14's declaration scopes it to *one row, two runs*. **THE INSTRUMENT NOW EXISTS, which is what this row asked to be re-read for.** [W19](work-items/w19-a-task-we-did-not-mint-on-a-real-scheduler.md) built `runPair`'s third argument, `ForeignTask[]` (`examples/run-flow.ts`): the `id` reaches a real `FyflowScheduler` **verbatim**, so this row's own stated ground - *"a real scheduler produces one only if something **injects** it, which no L6 row does"* - no longer holds. One L6 row already injects `pc:ingest:x:0`, this run's id with a corrupted `branchPath`; `pc:notanode:-:0` **parses**, reaches tier 2, and is `UndispatchedTask`. **The marginal cost is the string.** W19 re-read this row as its own declaration asked, recorded the re-read, and **did not take it**, because widening a scope the plan settled is not a work item's call (`doc/workflow.md` section 1 rule 3). The *against* half is untouched and is now the whole of the argument: injecting a task the driver did not mint is a scenario no consumer produces on purpose, so the row would test the refusal rather than the system. **For P6's retro to rule on** | open — **and P7's *"the marginal cost is a string"* is measured FALSE**. [W23](work-items/w23-a-nested-flow-on-real-workers.md) declined it on evidence: `ID-R4` **tier 2 REFUSES** — it raises `UndispatchedTask` (`src/driver/driver.ts:625`), which `onEventOrThrow` catches and `OR-R10` **reports** through `onRunFailure` rather than throwing at a caller, which is the verb `ID-R4` uses. Either way it does not drop silently, so a foreign task carrying a run's own run id and a parseable shape **stops that run**. It therefore cannot be a sixth entry in W19's `UNMINTED` list — that row's whole claim is *both runs completing* — and it needs its own row with outcome `stopped`. It also needs `foreign` plumbed into `runFlow`, which today only `runPair` accepts. **A row, an arrangement and a signature change, not a string** | no owner - **the instrument is `ForeignTask` in `examples/run-flow.ts`** |

| Q19 | **Is per-run retained state bounded?** `FLOW_PLANNING.md` targets 1000s-10000s of concurrent workloads. If Q8 or Q11 retains every node output, a run's footprint is proportional to its node count and the total is that times the run count. The scheduler already has this problem and answers it with `maxCompletedTasks` and eviction; we would inherit the answer *and* its sharp edge (eviction is silent). Cross-references `fyflow-react-components` **Q14**, which is the same question one layer up  **A second per-run structure since [W34](work-items/w34-what-a-branch-receives-and-what-a-join-carries.md), found by its conformity reader**: the driver's `arrivals` map retains **every arrived branch's result for the life of the run**, one entry per branch per region, and is never cleared. Nothing reads it once `firedJoins` has latched, so there is no observable defect — but it is new retention that no document recorded, and it is proportional to the fan-out width times the payload rather than to the node count this row already names. **A `map` makes that width a run-time N.** It also means [ADR 0037](decisions/0037-what-a-branch-receives-is-a-property-of-the-fan-out-and-what-a-join-carries-is-a-property-of-the-region.md) rule 1's *a node's payload is reachable from nothing else the run holds* is stated wider than the code holds it: the join's successor payload IS reachable from `arrivals`. Recorded here rather than fixed, because clearing it is a decision about run lifetime and this row is where that lives. **And this row's conditional is not one - [P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md)'s declaration measured it.** *"If [Q8](#live) or [Q11](#live) retains every node output"* describes **today**: the log is append-only, never truncated, carries `result` for every settled task and is exposed by `snapshot()`. So the footprint this row prices is already the run's, it is proportional to the node count as stated, and **no answer to Q8 or Q11 can create it because it is already there.** **What stays open is the question this row is actually about - whether that retention should be BOUNDED** - and P12 declares it OUT: eviction is a run-lifetime decision with the scheduler's own sharp edge (`maxCompletedTasks` evicts silently) and it needs a measured run at the 1000s-10000s `FLOW_PLANNING.md` targets, which this repository does not have.  **THE MEASURED RUN EXISTS SINCE [P15](plans/p15-what-a-run-retains-and-what-a-result-may-be.md)'s DECLARATION, and it corrects this row in three places.** **(1) The footprint is NOT proportional to the node count.** Four dimensions measured one at a time: ~262 B/node, ~469 B/fan-out branch, ~456 B/map instance — *one* variable, how many tasks settle — and **linear in the whole of every result**, which is the multiplier this row and `spec/run-state.md` section 6 both omit. A **4**-node chain returning 256 KiB retains **1025 KiB**; a **128**-node chain returning one key retains **33 KiB**. The footprint is *(tasks settled) x (bytes each returned)*. **(2) `arrivals` is a SECOND COPY, not an alias, and it is HALF of a region-bearing run.** `bytes([inputs, arrivals])` exceeds `bytes(inputs)` by **49.8%** at map arities 4, 16 and 64 over 64 KiB results, and the stored value is neither the log record's `result` object nor the worker's own. Clearing it after the join reads it costs **0 at L1-L5 and 0 at L6** (negative control: clearing it *before* costs **13**), and takes 10 000 concurrent runs from **3174.8 MiB to 1610.9 MiB**. **It does not reach the run that matters most** — a region whose join never fires holds its arrivals for the life of the run. **(3) `maxCompletedTasks` is NOT inheritable**: it caps `scheduler.tasks`, the scheduler's own map, and reaches nothing the driver holds — so there is no answer to inherit, only a shape, and the shape is worse here than *eviction is silent*. `fold()` over a log evicted to its last input reports **`completed` with ZERO nodes and ZERO regions**, no throw and no signal: a plausible wrong answer rather than a silent one. **And this row names TWO structures where the driver has four** — `inputs` is what actually holds every result, is what `Driver.foldInputs()` returns, and is in no row of this register; `log` is its record-kind subsequence **by reference** and costs **16 B** on top of it. **The scale itself: 1 000 runs x arity 4 x 1 KiB = 10.2 MiB; 10 000 x 4 x 1 KiB = 101.8 MiB; 10 000 x 10 x 16 KiB = 3174.8 MiB** — two observers, `v8.serialize` and `heapUsed` after a forced `gc()`, agreeing within 3%. **The target scale RUNS and the margin is a factor of one.** *(And the target's provenance is corrected: `FLOW_PLANNING.md` is not on this machine — the sibling was destroyed by [ADR 0029](decisions/0029-the-scheduler-is-a-registry-dependency-and-nothing-reaches-the-working-tree.md)'s incident and restored from the published package — and `doc/concept.md` says **"thousands at a time"**, not `1000s-10000s`.)* **ANSWERED by [ADR 0046](decisions/0046-a-run-retains-what-its-tasks-returned-and-a-region-releases-its-collection-when-its-join-becomes-terminal.md) ([W53](work-items/w53-what-a-run-retains-that-nothing-reads.md)), and the answer is BOUNDED BY THE RUN, with one second copy removed and one refusal stated.** `spec/run-state.md` is **v0.6**, section 7 is new and **`RN-R13`** is the rule: a run retains *(tasks settled) x (bytes each returned)*, plus one further copy of each result that has arrived at a join that has **not yet become TERMINAL** — and not one byte longer. *(Both this row and `RN-R13` said *whose region is still LIVE* in their first draft, and that is a different predicate and the wrong one: `p1-routed-away` under `arrival-count` has **zero live branches**, both terminal, and still retains. W53's conformity reader.)* **What was taken:** a region's collection is released when its join becomes TERMINAL, at three moments the driver can observe — routed out of (after the read), reported `unsatisfiable` (after the latch), and the run stopped (`OR-R10`), the third being run-level rather than per-region — with nothing recorded outside that window, because a policy may lawfully settle a join with branches still live ([Q95](#live)'s own precondition). Re-measured with it in: the `arrivals` share of a completed region-bearing run goes **49.8% -> 0.0%** at all three arities and 10 000 concurrent runs go **3174.8 MiB -> 1610.9 MiB**. Releasing after the read costs **0** at L1-L5 and **0** at L6; before it, **13** on the pre-W53 suite and **14** with W53's. **What was REFUSED, with the measurement rather than on taste:** nothing may be evicted from `inputs`, because a fold over an incomplete log reports `completed` with **ZERO** nodes and no signal — a plausible wrong answer rather than a silent one — so an eviction needs a way for the fold to say it was handed less, which is a new `FoldInput` kind. **And this row's FOURTH correction, from W53 re-running fact 1 rather than transcribing it:** the driver holds **FIVE** growing structures, not four and not this row's two. `outputsCache` was recorded as `null` in every run P15 measured, which is a property of those fixtures — they declare no `needs`, so `outputs()` is never called. On a flow that does it is **non-null**, and it **ALIASES**: 5/5, 17/17 and 65/65 of its entries are a log record's own `result` object, costing ~25 B an entry. *So the conclusion holds — there is no second 49.8% to find — and the reason is not that the fifth structure is empty.* **What stays open is elsewhere and is named:** bounding `RunState.nodes[*].result` is **89%** of the corpus, is measured **not** free (blanking every result takes a fold's input 13984 B -> 1612 B and changes `RunState`) and is [W54](work-items/w54-what-a-result-may-be-and-what-bounding-it-would-cost.md)'s with [Q22](#live); a region whose join never becomes terminal keeps its collection until the caller releases the driver, which is the BOUND rather than a leak, because `stuck` is decided in the FOLD at a quiescent point (`RN-R10`) and the driver is never told| **resolved 2026-09-11 — [ADR 0046](decisions/0046-a-run-retains-what-its-tasks-returned-and-a-region-releases-its-collection-when-its-join-becomes-terminal.md)** | [W53](work-items/w53-what-a-run-retains-that-nothing-reads.md) ([P15](plans/p15-what-a-run-retains-and-what-a-result-may-be.md)) |

| Q121 | **Dead relative links in `doc/**` and `AGENTS.md` are mechanically checkable and nothing checks them**, while `check-spec`'s **C5** does exactly this for `spec/**` and has since W1. Found by hand during [W22](work-items/w22-what-an-inner-join-does-to-the-outer-one.md), which is not a way to find them: **five dead links in two files**, all pointing at real files under old names or wrong depths — `work-items/w6-the-non-success-half-at-l6.md` and `work-items/w12-the-frozen-region-event.md` in this register (both renamed items), and three `](../doc/open-questions.md)` in `AGENTS.md`, which is at the repo root and needs `](doc/…)`. All five fixed in place. **This is `doc/`-is-checked-by-nothing with a mechanism that is cheap and already written** — C5 is fifteen lines and the only change is which directories it walks — so it is the one sub-case of that class that does **not** need the argument six retros have refused. Against building it inside W22: `scripts/**` and `package.json` are in nobody's `Owns`, and `doc/workflow.md` rule 7 (c) requires a new check to ship with checked-in probes declaring the exact set they trip, which is its own item's worth of work. **The cost of not having it is measured**: a renamed work item breaks every link to it silently, and this repository renames work items. **One implementation detail is already known**: the throwaway checker W22 used flagged two false positives, both `](…)` sequences inside **code spans** written as illustrations of link syntax — so the check must skip backticked spans, which C5 does not have to do because `spec/` prose does not illustrate links. **CORRECTED at [P8](plans/p8-what-can-be-deleted-with-the-gate-green.md)'s declaration, by measuring it — this row was wrong twice and incomplete once, and the third is the one that matters.** (a) Dead file links today: **0**, not five outstanding; W22 fixed all five and none have reappeared, so the file-link half of the check would ship green. (b) The false positives are **4, in three files**, not two — and one of them, `doc/work-items/TEMPLATE.md`'s `../plans/p_-<slug>.md`, is a **placeholder** rather than an illustration of link syntax, so the phenomenon is wider than the row's characterisation while the fix is identical. (c) **The row does not mention anchors, and that is where every live defect is.** `C5` strips `#…` from a target and compares the file path alone, so it is structurally blind to a link whose file exists and whose anchor does not — and there are **14 such links over 9 distinct targets**, being **all nine S-ids** in `doc/scheduler-upstream.md`'s own index. The headings read `## S2 - Cancel a queued task` and slug to `s2---cancel-a-queued-task` (three hyphens, from ` - `); every link says `s2--cancel-a-queued-task`, which is the **em-dash** slug. `git log -S` shows the headings were ASCII-hyphen in the founding commit, so **these were born dead and have never worked**. (d) *"C5 is fifteen lines and the only change is which directories it walks"* understates it in one direction and overstates it in another: **it can be LIFTED rather than copied** — measured, span-blanking leaves `spec/`'s result at 0 → 0 and `spec/` carries zero anchor links — but **`C5`'s id must survive the lift**, because it is cited in seven documents and `doc/workflow.md` section 3 forbids renumbering | open | **W25** — [P8](plans/p8-what-can-be-deleted-with-the-gate-green.md) |

| Q123 | **Nothing in this repository fixes line endings, and a whole-file conversion is invisible to every check.** **THIRD OCCURRENCE at [P15](plans/p15-what-a-run-retains-and-what-a-result-may-be.md)'s declaration, five plans after the second, and this row predicted every detail of it**: `pathlib.Path.write_text` converted **five files entirely to CRLF** — `CLAUDE.md`, `doc/README.md`, `doc/open-questions.md`, `scripts/check-markdown.mjs` and an ADR — **`G2` went red again, by the same layout derivation and with the same misleading message**, and every other check and the whole suite stayed green. **What is new is the PROVENANCE of the mistake**: the session guidance in use says *write multi-line file edits as `.py` scripts and run them with `python3`*, and `CLAUDE.md`'s own hazard paragraph warns about `Get-Content \| Set-Content` and **names no other tool** — so the document that records the hazard recommends the tool that causes it. *The fix that works is writing BYTES* (`read_bytes` / `write_bytes`), which neither this row nor `CLAUDE.md` states. Fixed by normalising the five files in binary; `git ls-files --eol` is **237 of 237 `i/lf`** afterwards. During [W22](work-items/w22-what-an-inner-join-does-to-the-outer-one.md), `python`'s text-mode write — the tool used to make several edits — silently converted **seven files entirely to CRLF** where `HEAD` is LF throughout, turning small edits into whole-file rewrites in the diff. **It reproduced exactly, in [W41](work-items/w41-what-a-node-can-reach-and-what-names-it.md), four plans later** — same tool, same cause (`open(p, "w")` translates `\n` to `os.linesep` on Windows and the matching read translates it back, so a read-modify-write converts the whole file and the script cannot see it), **six files, a 3834-line diff for a 458-line change**. And **this row's *"invisible to every check"* is now HALF WRONG**: `check-wiring`'s `G2` went red, because it derives a dispatch block's end from the LAYOUT — the first `\n}\n` after the dispatch — and there is no such sequence in a CRLF file. **The check that noticed a whole-file line-ending conversion was one deriving a block boundary from layout, and it reported it as a dispatch-block failure** — the right verdict by the wrong route, which is [Q138](#live)'s shape. *A check can be sensitive to a property nobody wrote it for, and its message then sends the reader to the wrong file.* `deno lint`, `tsc`, all five `check-*.mjs` and the whole test suite stayed green; `git diff --stat` was the only signal, and only because the insertion counts were absurd. There is **no `.gitattributes`** and no `core.autocrlf` assumption written down. Two candidate fixes and they are not the same: a `.gitattributes` with `* text=auto eol=lf` normalises on the way into git and hides the problem in the working tree; a check that refuses CRLF in tracked text files makes it visible. **Windows is the primary platform here** (`CLAUDE.md`), so this is not hypothetical — and the same file already warns that `Get-Content \| Set-Content` double-encodes UTF-8, which is the same class one encoding down. **CORRECTED at [P8](plans/p8-what-can-be-deleted-with-the-gate-green.md)'s declaration: this row's premise is FALSE, and correcting it changes what the fix has to decide.** *"`HEAD` is LF throughout"* is not true — `git ls-files --eol` reports **161 LF and one CRLF**, and the CRLF file is **`doc/concept.md`**: CRLF in the index, CRLF in the working tree, and CRLF in **all six commits that ever touched it**, back to the founding commit `2936a72`. So it is **not** W22's `python` writes; it predates them and predates every check here. Confirmed alongside: there is no `.gitattributes` and `core.autocrlf` is `false`. **The consequence is that a check refusing CRLF fails on day one**, on the 221-line document `CLAUDE.md` says to read rather than re-derive — so the item owns a real choice it would not otherwise have had: normalise that file (a whole-file rewrite, visible in a public history forever) or ship the check with a **named** exemption. The two candidate fixes named above are unchanged and still are not the same thing. **P8 declared an item for this and DROPPED it before it started, and one half was discharged outside any item**: `doc/concept.md` is **normalised to LF in P8's declaration commit** — 221 CRLF pairs removed at the byte level, the result **byte-identical to the previous `HEAD` with the CRs stripped**, so no decode and no re-encode and none of the `Get-Content \| Set-Content` hazard `CLAUDE.md` warns about. **The instance is fixed and the mechanism is unguarded exactly as before.** `.gitattributes` was deliberately **not** added: it belongs with the check that would make the problem *visible* rather than with the one that hides it, and no check was built. `core.autocrlf` is `false`, so an LF file stays LF until a tool rewrites it — which is precisely what happened in W22 and would happen again in silence | open | **no owner** — declared as P8's W26 and cut on scope; the id is skipped and never reissued |

So the guard is real and its argument is now a result; what is missing is a row that needs it.
