| Q134 | **`S12` now subsumes `S11`'s membership half, and `S11`'s prefix lookahead is deletable for 0.** Found by [W24](work-items/w24-the-gates-own-wiring.md)'s adequacy reader. `S11` asks whether each `check:*` name appears in the `gate` chain, with `npm run <name>(?![:\w])` — the lookahead stops `npm run check:docs` matching inside `npm run check:docs:selftest`. `S12` now asserts the chain **equals** `GATE_CHAIN`, and every `check:*` name is in `GATE_CHAIN`, so `GATE_CHAIN ⊇ {check:*}` is enforced by the pair and a `check:*` entry cannot leave the chain without `S12` firing. Measured: deleting the lookahead costs **0** on a clean tree; removing only `npm run check:docs && ` from the chain costs **2** failures (`S11` + `S12`) with it and **1** (`S12` alone) without. W24 checked in a probe for exactly that mutation, so the lookahead cannot rot silently — **the question this leaves is whether `S11`'s membership clause should be RETIRED**, leaving `S11` as the *"the entry runs a script that exists, and a `:selftest` entry passes `--self-test`"* check and `S12` as the membership one. **For retiring it:** two checks asserting one fact means every deletion reports twice, which is why W24's probe for it has to declare a cascade of `{S11, S12}` rather than a single check — and `doc/workflow.md` rule 7 (c) is easier to satisfy when clauses do not overlap. **Against:** `S11`'s clause is derived from `package.json`'s **keys** and `S12`'s from a hand-written list, so they are two sources and not one, and `S12`'s list going wrong is precisely [Q102](#live)'s risk. **`doc/workflow.md` section 3 forbids renumbering**, so retiring a clause is not the same as freeing an id | open | no owner |
| Q135 | **A THIRD row of this file renders its cells wrong, by a different mechanism than [Q131](#live)'s two, and it is the mechanism a cell-count check would have to handle first.** Found by [W24](work-items/w24-the-gates-own-wiring.md)'s census parser — the aborting kind, on its third outing. Q131 records `Q119` and `Q71` splitting on **unescaped pipes**; `Q87` is the opposite defect. Its row **spans lines 160-164 with two blank lines inside it**, and its status and owner cells sit on line 164, which does **not begin with `\|`**. GitHub Flavored Markdown ends a table at the first blank line, so line 160 renders as a two-cell row with no status and no owner at all, and lines 162 and 164 render as paragraphs below the table — after which the next `\| Q90 \|` line has no delimiter row above it and is not a table either. **The census is unaffected** and the standing line is correct at 92 (verified: 58 open, 2 deferred, 32 resolved, re-derived by a parser that aborts on any row it cannot classify), because `doc/workflow.md`'s own predicate — *the second-to-last cell of the row's last cell-bearing line* — reads line 164 and gets the right answer. **That predicate and a GFM renderer disagree**, and this row is where. **This matters for the check [Q131](#live) proposes:** *"a row's cell count is arithmetic, not a claim"* is true only if the checker agrees with the renderer about **where a row ends**, and W24's parser needed a rule W18's predicate supplies and GFM does not. **NOT FIXED**, on the same reasoning [P8](plans/p8-what-can-be-deleted-with-the-gate-green.md) gives for leaving Q131's two rows alone: whoever builds the check should have all three shapes to fire on. W24 also records that its own first parser was **wrong in the other direction** — it split on `\|` as well as `\|` and reported five malformed rows where there are three, which is P6's retro's lesson landing on the parser written to honour it | open | no owner |
| Q136 | **`check-markdown` reads ONE link syntax and ONE anchor source, and ten other markdown shapes are invisible to it — one of them in the direction that makes the gate wrong rather than weak.** Built by [W25](work-items/w25-a-link-is-a-link-and-an-anchor-is-an-anchor.md), which matches `](target)` and ATX headings and nothing else. Measured at **zero occurrences** across the 62 documents it scans, which is what makes them gaps rather than live defects: reference-style definitions `[a]: url` and uses `[text][a]`; HTML anchors `<a name=…>` / `<a id=…>`; an unclosed fence; an anchor with uppercase letters or percent-encoded; an empty anchor `](#)`; a query string; an angle-bracket target `](<spaced name.md>)`. **They do not all fail the same way and that is the part to carry.** A reference-style link to a missing file passes **silently** — a false negative, the shape [Q121](#live) exists to refuse, reintroduced by a syntax the checker does not read. An HTML anchor and an unclosed fence would be reported **dead when they are not** — false positives, which are worse, because the gate goes red for a document that is correct and the fix is to edit the document rather than the check. **And a link inside an HTML comment is not invisible at all: it is REPORTED.** W25's own header listed it as *"invisible, 0 occurrences"* and its conformity reader ran it and it fires — so the count of false-positive shapes is three, not two, and one of the ten was miscategorised in the file that declares them. **For closing it:** each is a few lines, and this repository writes markdown constantly. **Against:** every one is a shape nothing here uses, so a clause for each is a clause with no live case and only a probe behind it — which is exactly what W25 says about its own slug rule, one level out. **What would settle it:** the first time any of the ten appears in a document, whichever direction it fails in | open | no owner |
| Q137 | **`C5` did not inherit `M3`, so `spec/`'s link check can still pass vacuously and `doc/`'s cannot.** [W25](work-items/w25-a-link-is-a-link-and-an-anchor-is-an-anchor.md) lifted `C5` into `scripts/check-markdown.mjs` and gave the `doc/` side an `M3` clause — *the walk covers the tree* — after its conformity reader dropped **31 of 62 documents** out of the scan for **0 failures** with the gate at exit 0. The lift returns the counts (`files`, `links`, `anchored`) precisely so a caller can refuse a vacuous pass, and **`check-spec.mjs` reads only `.dead` and `.deadAnchors`** — so the same mutation one directory across (narrowing `walk(SPEC)`, or a `mdFiles` filter that matches nothing) leaves `C5` green on an empty set, which is the defect `M3` was written for. **The argument for `M3` is the argument for a `C5` floor and it was not carried across the lift.** Found by W25's conformity reader. **For fixing:** three lines, the counts are already returned, and `spec/` is seven documents whose count has been stable since W1 so a floor there obliges nobody. **Against:** `check-spec`'s `C1`-`C8` have **no** self-test at all ([Q132](#live)), so a new clause there ships unprobed unless that gap is closed first — which is why W25 declined it rather than adding it under a different id. **This is the general shape worth naming**: a lift moves an implementation and does not move the guards the two sides needed differently | open | no owner |
| Q138 | **A META pass asserts that SOME probe notices a broken check, never WHICH — and that is both a correctness gap and the whole of its cost.** Every self-test here grades its probes the same way: mutate the check, re-run the entire probe suite, and require a non-zero exit. **The verdict is an exit code, so it cannot distinguish *the clause I broke was caught* from *something else went wrong*.** [W25](work-items/w25-a-link-is-a-link-and-an-anchor-is-an-anchor.md) hit both directions in one item. Two of its META entries matched a **comment** rather than the code — the paragraph above the clauses quotes them verbatim — so the check ran unchanged and the entry reported *"the probes passed against a check that does not check"* about a check that was never broken; the diagnosis pointed at the probes and the defect was in the mutation. W24's conformity reader found the mirror image one item earlier: a META entry scored **noticed** because the mutant **crashed**, which is W6's syntax-error-read-as-a-caught-mutation at the grading level, where set equality discriminates one level in and an exit code does not. **And it is why the pass is slow.** `check:markdown:selftest` costs **2m31 to 3m14** of a **4m46 to 6m00** gate — two runs of the same tree each, so the absolutes vary by a quarter and the **ratio does not: roughly half the gate, either way** *(**88% for all five self-tests together — see [Q133](#live), corrected at [P9](plans/p9-when-a-check-stops-checking-what-notices.md)'s declaration**)*, because 37 META entries each re-run up to 37 probes; `check:source:selftest` is 23s for 20 gradings over 24 probes. **The single figure this row first carried was wrong in the way this repository keeps getting counts wrong** — it was one measurement of a quantity that varies, quoted as a fact. **For fixing:** an entry declares the probe it must break, the child runs that probe first, and the grading asserts *that* probe failed — which is both a stronger claim and roughly a 20x cut in spawns, since the child stops after one. It is `doc/workflow.md` rule 7 (c)'s set-equality argument applied to the layer above the probes. **Against:** it adds a second declaration to every META entry, and an entry whose probe is renamed then aborts rather than passing — which is rule 7 (b) and arguably correct. **Not done in W25**: it arrived after both readers reported, and a harness change at that point re-opens a re-run cycle rule 8 has just closed. Interacts with [Q133](#live), which is the same cost from the plan's side. **CONFIRMED and sharpened at [P9](plans/p9-when-a-check-stops-checking-what-notices.md)'s declaration**, over all 56 code-targeted META entries in the two files: every grading is `r.status !== 0`; **which probe gets the credit is an artefact of `PROBES` ORDER** — the child breaks at the first probe the mutant disturbs, so five of `check-markdown`'s six slug-rule mutations are credited to *a link to a file that does not exist* while the two probes written for the slug rule sit lower and never run; only **14 of 24** and **31 of 37** probes are ever the one that notices; and in **7 of 56** the crediting probe declared a non-empty set and reported a **strictly larger** one, so the mutant became **noisy** rather than being detected. **This row's proposed fix is adopted as W28's whole subject** | open | **W28** ([P9](plans/p9-when-a-check-stops-checking-what-notices.md)) |
| Q141 | *Resolved - see the Resolved table.* | **resolved - [W29](work-items/w29-the-two-self-tests-that-grade-nothing.md)** | **W29** ([P9](plans/p9-when-a-check-stops-checking-what-notices.md)) |
| Q142 | **`check-markdown`'s floors are a two-sided window between the corpus and its own probes' fixtures, and adding four ordinary documents closed it — turning `npm run gate` RED one plan after the file's header argued it could not.** `scripts/check-markdown.mjs` says of `M3`/`M4`/`M5`: *"They are a MINIMUM nobody is obliged to raise, which is what keeps them out of [Q102](#live)'s shape … adding a document here can only move these numbers up and away."* **That is right about the CHECK and false about the PROBES**, and the probes are the only thing that makes the check falsifiable. `M3` and `M4` can only be fired by the two `removes` probes, which cut directories out of a copy of the tree — so each floor must sit **above the probe's fixture and below the whole tree**, and every document added outside the removed directories raises the fixture toward the floor. **Measured at [P9](plans/p9-when-a-check-stops-checking-what-notices.md)'s declaration**, which added one plan and three work items: *doc/decisions removed* went from **46 files to 50** against a floor of `< 50`, `M3` fell silent, and the gate exited **1** with `wanted [M1,M3], got [M1]`; *doc/decisions + doc/plans removed* went from **1116 links to 1217** against a floor of `< 1200` and reported `wanted [M1,M3,M4], got [M1,M3]`. **So it IS [Q102](#live)'s shape after all** — an item that adds documents is obliged to retune a constant in a file it does not own — **and it is worse**, because the window is bounded on both sides where `RUN_FLOOR`'s is bounded on one. **The direction is the mitigation and it is the same one W24 recorded for `S12`**: `RUN_FLOOR` went stale *silently, toward weakness*; this failed *loudly, in the gate*, naming both probes and both files. **Retuned to 56 / 1350 / 60 from a measurement** — whole tree 66/1746/86, the two fixtures 50/1548/86 and 40/1217/86 — which restores green and **does not remove the ratchet**. **And retuning it broke two more things, which is the part to carry:** the two META entries that disable those floors are pinned to the literals `if (r.files < 50)` and `if (r.links < 1200)`, so changing the constants made both report *"the mutation matched nothing"* — rule 7 (b) firing correctly rather than passing, and the same class as W25's comment-quoting entries: **a mutation pinned to a magic number is unmutatable the moment the number is right to change.** **For fixing:** derive each bound from the tree rather than declaring it — `S13` already takes two of its bounds from `package.json` and from disk rather than from its own declaration, and that is the shape. **Against:** a derived bound cannot fire on the corpus it derives from, so the probes would have to inject the shrinkage rather than reveal it, which is a different harness. **Owner: [W28](work-items/w28-a-grading-names-the-probe-it-breaks.md)**, which already owns `check-markdown-selftest.mjs` and both broken probes. **[W33](work-items/w33-a-map-at-run-time-arity.md) is a FOURTH instance and it is one this row does not describe: it added NO DOCUMENT.** Every earlier occurrence is *an item that adds documents is obliged to retune a constant in a file it does not own*. W33 added **links** to documents that already existed — a spec rule, a section, register rows, index paragraphs — and the two-directory fixture rose **1507 to 1754**, past `M4`'s floor of **1720**. The probe went silent and the gate reported `wanted [M1,M3,M4], got [M1,M3]`. **So the obligation is on writing PROSE, which no item can avoid, rather than on adding FILES** — and it lands on `M4` alone, because the file and anchored counts move only when documents or anchored links are added (`M3` at 64 was still inside its window and `M5` is fired by a probe no `removes` cut reaches at all). Retuned to **2050** from a fresh three-count measurement — whole tree **73 / 2419 / 119**, one directory removed **56 / 2205 / 119**, both removed **45 / 1754 / 119**. **And the `META` entry pinned to the literal went inert for the SECOND time** — rule 7 (b) firing correctly, as it did at P10's declaration — so W33 made it **anchor-agnostic** (`/if \(r\.links < \d+\) fail\('M4'/`) rather than re-pinning it, which is the fix this row's *"for fixing"* column argues for one level down: **the probe no longer goes stale when the number is right to change**. **[W42](work-items/w42-what-a-decision-sees.md) is the FIFTH retune and the first in which `M3` MOVED TOO**, which is the half three plans of `M4`-only retunes had made look like a ratchet nobody has to pay. [P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md) is the first plan in four to add documents to **`doc/decisions`** — the directory probe 1 REMOVES — so the one-directory fixture rose **56 to 64** against a floor of `< 64`, `64 < 64` is false, and the gate reported `wanted [M1,M3], got [M1]` beside probe 2's `wanted [M1,M3,M4], got [M1,M3]`. **Both floors, one edit**: measured on a scratch copy outside the repository — whole tree **85 / 3328 / 167**, one directory removed **64 / 3014 / 167**, both removed **51 / 2406 / 167** — so `M3`'s window is `(64, 85]` and `M4`'s is `(2406, 3014]`, and the floors are **74** and **2700**. **And `M3`'s `META` entry was still pinned to the literal `< 64`**, because W33 made only `M4`'s agnostic and `M3` had had no live case since: it reported *"the mutation matched nothing"*, rule 7 (b) firing for the THIRD time in this row, and it is agnostic now. *A reason given for one entry was general and was instantiated in one place for three plans, because the other place had nothing to fire on*. **FIRED at [W58](work-items/w58-what-a-node-says-about-its-own-task.md), in the item's FINAL gate, and for the first time NOT predicted**: P16's declaration measured the margin at 388 links and W58's documents — a spec subsection, three register rows, a Record and two index paragraphs, none under a directory a `removes` probe cuts — took the two-directory fixture 3102 → 3532 past `M4`'s 3490. | Q143 | **A mutation whose anchor text also appears in the file doing the mutating — earlier than its target — edits the MUTATOR instead, and two of the four that did so reported BOUNDED for a reason that never happened.** Found by [W28](work-items/w28-a-grading-names-the-probe-it-breaks.md), which wrote four `SELF` entries for its own new grading contract in `scripts/check-source-selftest.mjs`. Each began as *remove the guard AND supply the case the guard catches*, and each quoted the guard's own source line as its `String.replace` anchor — so the first occurrence of that text was **the `SELF` entry's own literal**, hundreds of lines above the guard. Two reported SURVIVED, which is visible; **two reported BOUNDED, which is not** — the run went red for an unrelated reason and the guard it claims to remove was never removed. **This is [W25](work-items/w25-a-link-is-a-link-and-an-anchor-is-an-anchor.md)'s *a comment that quotes the code it explains makes that code unmutatable*, with the comment replaced by the mutation's own argument — and it is worse in one specific way**: a comment produces a no-op that the rule 7 (b) guard catches and reports, while this produces an edit that lands somewhere real, so nothing is vacuous and nothing is reported. **Fixed in W28 by a design change rather than by better anchors**: every one of those entries now edits the **`META` list** and leaves the guard alone, which asks the question the right way round — *with the guard in place, does the run go red?* — and cannot self-match, because `META` sits above `SELF` in the file. **The two-line anchor W28 tried first also works and is the fallback**: a `\n` in a source literal is two characters and the newline it targets is one, so it cannot match itself. **For a check:** every self-test here mutates its own file in the `SELF` pass, so the hazard is structural rather than local, and a guard is cheap — after applying a mutation, assert the changed region is **outside** the mutating list's own line range. **Against:** W28 removed the whole class from its own files by construction, so a check would ship with no live case, which is the second of [P8](plans/p8-what-can-be-deleted-with-the-gate-green.md)'s two open verdicts arguing against itself. **What would settle it:** the next `SELF` entry anyone writes that quotes a guard | open | no owner |

The first repair — sending the report into a discarded array, which leaves the `if`/`else if` chain intact — **was still fatal for this reason**, which is the same entry being wrong twice. **The consequence is a coverage hole with a name**: `P3`'s `names === null` clause has no META entry and cannot have one, because every mutation of it produces a mutant that is inert on the clean tree and throws under its own probe. That entry now takes `P3`'s FLOOR clause instead. **It is also the only known instance of the case the `ran:` guard exists for**, so with it repointed the guard is live in three files and provoked in none — recorded on each file's ceiling list rather than deleted. **For fixing:** one line in `check-policies.mjs` — `names?.join(', ') ?? '(none)'` — after which the clause becomes probeable. **Against:** `check-policies.mjs` is not in W28's `Owns`, and the change is to a check's behaviour rather than to its negative tests, which is the boundary [P9](plans/p9-when-a-check-stops-checking-what-notices.md) draws for every item in it **CORRECTED at [W29](work-items/w29-the-two-self-tests-that-grade-nothing.md): the guard is live in FIVE files, not three** — W29 added the fourth and fifth when it gave `check-docs` and `check-scheduler` a META pass, and this row was not updated in the same pass. Measured: **`ran: no`** with the guard live, and **`ran: yes`, `outcome: disappeared`** with it forced true — the identical crash graded as a detection. **The checked-in form is a `SELF` entry**, and `SELF` is out of W29's scope while [W31](work-items/w31-the-self-list-check-policies-never-had.md) is narrowed to `check-policies`, so no declared item owns it. | open | no owner |
| Q145 | **A META mutation that fails on the repository's own tree is graded `corpus` and PASSES, so global noise is reported rather than refused — which is one half of [Q138](#live) closed and one half deliberately left open.** [W28](work-items/w28-a-grading-names-the-probe-it-breaks.md)'s readers both found that the grading could not tell a detection from a mutant that merely got NOISIER. W28 answered with an inertness precondition — a mutant that does not exit 0 on the untouched tree is classified `corpus`, counted separately, and still passes — plus a `noisy` verdict that **refuses** a mutant which is inert on the clean tree and adds unrelated ids under the probe. **The split is measured**: `check-markdown` reports **37/37 noticed — 31 by the named probe, 6 by the corpus**, and the adequacy reader's own noise edit reproduces as `6/6 noticed — 5 by the named probe, 1 by the corpus` at **exit 0**. **The argument for passing it:** a mutant that fails on the corpus turns the gate red, which is a stronger guarantee than any probe gives, and W25 recorded exactly that as *"caught by the CORPUS rather than by a probe"*. Five of `check-markdown`'s six are slug-rule mutations that **cannot** be made inert — they change what the check does to real anchors — so refusing them would force rewriting correct mutations. **The argument against:** `corpus` does not verify what the entry CLAIMS, and a mislabelled entry is indistinguishable from a legitimately non-inert one, because both are just non-inert mutations. **What would settle it:** whether any of the six can be re-pointed at a probe whose verdict is meaningful under a non-inert mutant, or whether the two cases need different entry kinds | open | no owner |
| Q146 | **A clause can be made inert with every probe green because a SIBLING clause reporting the SAME id fires in its place, and a set-equality grading cannot see the difference.** Found by [W29](work-items/w29-the-two-self-tests-that-grade-nothing.md) on the first run of `check-scheduler`'s new META list, which predicted *disappeared* for both and got **STILL CAUGHT** for both. Measured: `D2`'s unreadable-manifest clause made inert leaves `own` null, so `spec` is undefined and the **devDependency** clause fires — 2 failures under `D2` become 1, both under `D2`. `D5`'s unreadable-declarations clause made inert leaves `expected` empty, so the **empty-parse** clause fires — 1 failure under `D5` becomes 1 failure under `D5`, with a different message. **The `D5` pair is the sharper one because the handover is BY DESIGN**: the empty-parse clause is guarded by `!failures.some(f => f.startsWith('D5'))` precisely so the two do not both report, and that guard is what makes the second step forward when the first is removed. **Neither is a hole in the probes and neither is a mislabelled entry**, which are the only two verdicts [W28](work-items/w28-a-grading-names-the-probe-it-breaks.md)'s contract admits — so W29 added a third: an entry may **declare** the masking, and the assertion becomes *this clause is inert and its named sibling covers the id*, which goes red if the masking ever changes. What these clauses carry is **diagnostic** value rather than detection value: the run fails either way, with a worse message. **Open, because the declaration is a description rather than a fix.** Three candidates, none taken: discriminate below the id on the failure COUNT (the report already prints `(2)` vs `(1)`, so it is available); short-circuit the check so each clause can fire alone (a change to what the check checks, which [P9](plans/p9-when-a-check-stops-checking-what-notices.md) decision 4 puts out of scope); or accept that two clauses under one id are one clause for grading purposes and merge their ids, which section 3 forbids. **This is `doc/workflow.md` rule 7 (c)'s form meeting its limit one layer out** — set equality on ids discriminates a probe that trips the wrong check, and cannot discriminate two clauses that trip the right one **CORRECTED by [W29](work-items/w29-the-two-self-tests-that-grade-nothing.md)'s conformity reader, and the correction is the useful half.** This row was written claiming **two** occurrences and claiming the declaration was falsifiable — *"it goes red if the masking ever changes … if the guard is removed"*. **That sentence was FALSE when it was written**: deleting `D5`'s `!failures.some(f => f.startsWith('D5'))` fallthrough guard left the self-test at **18/18 probes, 21/21 graded, exit 0**, so the guard was deletable for 0 — a **third** instance of this row's own class, inside the mechanism written to describe it. **The fix is the discriminator this row proposed and declined**: a `caught` verdict is no longer terminal, and the entry is re-run against the UNMUTATED check so the failure COUNT can be compared. It costs one extra child, only for entries that reach `caught` — today **one**. **And it immediately separated the two cases, which is why the occurrence count drops from two to one.** `D2`'s unreadable-manifest clause is **CO-FIRING, not masking**: both it and the devDependency clause report under `D2`, so disabling the first moves the count **2 -> 1** and the clause **is** detected — it is now graded normally, by the count, and its declaration was removed. `D5`'s is the real thing: same id **and** same count, because the fallthrough guard exists precisely so the two never both report. **So the class is real and rarer than first measured, and the distinction is worth more than the count**: *two clauses under one id* splits into **co-firing** (detectable, by a projection nobody was reading) and **masking** (genuinely indistinguishable, and only there is a declaration honest). **Still open**, because the discriminator is built for one grading in one file rather than for the contract: the other four self-tests grade on the id set alone, and nothing has measured how many of their clauses share an id with a sibling. | open | **no owner** |

The grading accepts `disappeared`, `disagreed` and `exit0` interchangeably. *(This row first said **four**, naming `fires` as well; W29's conformity reader measured that `fires` exists only in `check-source-selftest.mjs` and `check-markdown-selftest.mjs`, for probes whose declared set is EMPTY, and that every probe in the two files this row was measured on declares a check. | Q148 | **A META entry is TWO check runs, not one, so the cost of a grading is bought per second and the two self-tests are two orders of magnitude apart — and the obvious mitigation is unsafe today, in FOUR places rather than the one this row named.** *([P11](plans/p11-what-should-the-gate-prove.md)'s declaration measured it by moving `node_modules/fyflow-scheduler` aside — never a link — and running each entry, with the rename asserted back and the control re-run after. **Four fail with the package absent**: `check:docs` and `typecheck` on `Cannot find module`, `check:scheduler` on its own `D1`, and `test:l6` on `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`; `lint` and `check:source` are unaffected. **And the stated mechanism is wrong**: the `AGENTS.md` snippet this row names is tagged ` ```ts check `, which `check-docs.mjs`'s own header says is *"typechecked only"* — so the hazard is TYPE RESOLUTION, and `typecheck` fails for the same reason with no snippet involved at all. **The conclusion survives and is stronger; the reason and the scope were both wrong.** The upper bound on parallelising, as arithmetic rather than a measurement: five concurrent plus that one alone is 155.6 s against 404.1, a 2.6x saving, and `check:markdown:selftest`'s 141.7 s is a floor no arrangement gets under without changing what is proved.)* The original row follows. [P9](plans/p9-when-a-check-stops-checking-what-notices.md)'s arithmetic priced ten entries at *"~46 s under this one"* from `4.4 s x 10`; that predates W28's readers adding the **inertness run**, which every entry must do because a `disappeared` verdict cannot be told from a mutant that was already failing. Measured by [W29](work-items/w29-the-two-self-tests-that-grade-nothing.md): a bare `check-docs` run is **3.75 s** and one entry is **7.1 s** (mean over its seven, timed individually), so the plan's figure is optimistic by **2.3x**; a bare `check-scheduler` run is **0.13 s** and one entry is ~0.5 s. **That ratio is why W29's coverage is cost-proportionate** — every clause in `check-scheduler`, one entry per check id plus two report entries in `check-docs`, with the six ungraded clauses named in that file's header rather than omitted. **And the obvious answer to [Q133](#live) — run the self-tests concurrently — does not work today, measured**: `check-scheduler`'s install probes move `node_modules/fyflow-scheduler` aside, and one `AGENTS.md` snippet imports it, so `check:scheduler:selftest` and `check:docs:selftest` contend for a directory outside either one's scratch space. The gate's `&&` chain makes this invisible because it is serial; a parallel lane would find it as a flaky snippet failure with no obvious cause. **For whoever answers Q133**: the saving is not in parallelism and not in the contract, it is in the corpus each probe runs against — **and that last sentence is FALSE, measured**: parallelism is where the saving was, 488.9 s to 290.9, and the corpus was not touched | **RESOLVED** | **[W39](work-items/w39-what-the-gate-proves-and-what-it-costs.md)**, by **[ADR 0039](decisions/0039-the-gate-climbs-the-ladder-and-the-self-tests-run-concurrently.md)** — **and the contention is WIDER than this row or P11's fact 3, in a direction neither looked**. The `node_modules` half is real and is handled by running that entry alone. **The half nobody had found is `check:wiring:selftest`**: `stage()` writes every `scripts/check-*.mjs` flat into `dev-dist/` — the directory every other self-test uses — and runs those copies with their dispatch falsified, so a staged `check-docs.mjs` reaches the real body and `rmSync`s the fixed path `dev-dist/docs`, and a staged `check-spec.mjs` whose override read is gone launches the WHOLE `check-spec` self-test, which deletes `dev-dist/spec-selftest`. Concurrently that reported *an `X4` probe uncaught*, *a probe tripping `[X1,X4]`* and *the mutant did not run: this is a crash, not a detection* — **findings that were facts about a neighbouring process**. Isolated by measurement: green beside `check:wiring`, red beside `check:wiring:selftest`. Both now carry a `SELFTEST-EXCLUSIVE:` marker their own file declares, which the runner reads and `G4` computes a second time and compares. **And their exclusivity is now the gate's binding constraint**: `check:wiring:selftest` runs alone for ~200 s before the concurrent group starts, so the answer to this row costs the answer to [Q133](#live) about 190 s of critical path — [Q182](#live) with a price on it. The residue is [Q181](#live) and [Q182](#live): the marker is bound to nothing, and `dev-dist/` is a shared namespace nothing namespaces |

requiring that file to contain a `--self-test` dispatch is one `readFileSync` and one regex, in the file W30 already owns. **Against:** it is a change to what `S11` checks, which [P9](plans/p9-when-a-check-stops-checking-what-notices.md)'s scope declares out, and a regex over a source file is a weaker guarantee than the manifest comparison beside it — the dispatch could be present and dead. **NOT FIXED** by W30, deliberately: the measurement is the deliverable and the fix is a scope call somebody else makes | **RESOLVED** | **[W38](work-items/w38-the-dispatch-nothing-binds.md)** — and by THREE clauses in a NEW check rather than one in `S11`. `G1` runs each `:selftest` script with the flag and an override pointing its self-test module at a stub, and requires the stub's sentinel; | Q150 | **A `corpus` grading is a PASS, so a harness bug that makes every mutant non-inert reads as full coverage.** [W28](work-items/w28-a-grading-names-the-probe-it-breaks.md) added the inertness run and classified a mutant that fails on the repository's own tree as `corpus` — *noticed by the gate, which is stronger than any probe* — and counted it separately so the number of probe-backed gradings stays visible. That reasoning is right about a **clause** that cannot be made inert ([Q145](#live)) and says nothing about a **harness** that broke. [W30](work-items/w30-the-clauses-nothing-probes.md) measured the difference in its own rule 8 set: dropping the import relocation from `check-spec-selftest.mjs` leaves every META copy unable to resolve `check-markdown.mjs`, so all 21 mutants fail on the clean tree, all 21 grade `corpus`, and the file exits **0** with the summary line reading ***"0 noticed by the named probe, 21 by the corpus"***. True, and nobody reads a summary line in a green run. **Closed in `check-spec-selftest.mjs` alone**, by a declared `MAX_CORPUS = 0`: every entry there is measured inert, so a corpus-backed grading means the harness broke. **Live in the other four.** `check-markdown` legitimately has six that cannot be made inert, so its bound is a number rather than zero; `check-source`, `check-scheduler` and `check-docs` all measure zero today and have no bound at all. **Against fixing it everywhere now:** it is four one-line declarations, but each needs the count measured first, and a bound written from today's run without that measurement is [Q102](#live)'s ratchet in a new place | open | **no owner** — one declared constant per self-test, each preceded by a measurement |

W31's mutants were first written into the scratch directory a child deletes on its first line, so the `META` grandchild could not re-import them: **all twelve entries passed, `12/12 bounded` at exit 0**, while every grading inside every child read *"the mutant did not run: this is a crash, not a detection"*. **The whole pass was measuring the deletion.** That is [Q138](#live)'s own shape — an exit code cannot tell a detection from a crash — **one layer further out than the plan that exists to close it, inside the entries written to close it**, and the file's own green run could not see it. *(It also falsified this item's cost figure: the `check:policies:selftest` "after" number was first measured at **8.5 s** against the broken harness and is **14.0 s** with the children actually running.)* **Closed in `check-policies-selftest.mjs`** by a CONTROL RUN — an unmutated copy, run as a `SELF` child, must RUN and must NOT be graded as bounded — which is W28's inertness check one layer out, routed through the same expression the entries use so the control is that expression's negative case. Reintroducing the defect makes it refuse by name. **Live in `check-source-selftest.mjs` and `check-markdown-selftest.mjs`**, the two other files with a `SELF` list and no control. **Against fixing them here:** neither is in W31's `Owns`, and the control costs a full extra child (**2.1 s of 14.0 s** measured here), which is a real fraction of a 76 s and a 97 s pass | open | **no owner** — one control run per `SELF` list, and the cost measured before it is added |

It was wrong twice over — internally, and about the substance — and W31's conformity reader measured it. The split is: `check-policies`, `check-source`, `check-docs` and `check-markdown` use the message-only form; **`check-spec` and `check-scheduler` do not**, because W29's failure-COUNT discriminator turned that arm into a block that re-runs the unmutated check and can set `ok`. Measured after the list shipped: `if (r.status === 3) why =` -> `if (false)` costs 0; `else if (outcome === 'caught') why =` -> `if (false)` costs 0; `else if (!ran) why =` -> `if (false)` costs **1**, and so does forcing the child's `RAN` producer true. **What the two `SELF` entries for them DO bound is the outcome rather than the branch** — a dangling probe reference and a mislabelled entry each turn the file red — and that is worth having and is not what the item's own table claimed. **For changing it:** `ok` could be computed from an explicit verdict rather than from an accepting arm, at which point both branches become load-bearing in all three files. **Against:** W28 chose this deliberately and its Record says so — *"two verdicts and a message, and the message is the product"* — and the ABORT's message is what turned W29's dangling reference into a ten-second fix. | Q155 | **Ladder L3 folds a log whose routing is already decided, so the rung the register cites for determinism cannot reach a decision predicate.** Raised at [P10](plans/p10-is-fan-out-one-feature-or-two.md)'s declaration while declining [Q9](#live) with a measurement. `doc/workflow.md` section 4 gives L3 as *"fold the same outcome log twice, deep-compare run state"*, catching *"map ordering, `Date.now()` in the fold, hidden state"* — and it does. **But a `FoldInput` carries an outcome record and a region event**, and `selectEdge` is called from the driver, never from the fold, so **which edge a predicate chose is an input to L3 rather than something L3 recomputes.** Two independent folds of the same log agree whatever the predicate did. | Q156 | **May a `map`'s branch itself open a region — a map inside a map, or a map inside a `fanout` branch?** Named in [P10](plans/p10-is-fan-out-one-feature-or-two.md)'s *"not taken"* and **left expressible and undecided** by [W32](work-items/w32-what-a-map-fanout-is.md) rather than answered in either direction. `FD-V25` requires a map's `branch` to be a `task`, exactly as `FD-V9` requires it of a `fanout`'s entries, so nesting goes through that task's edges — the road a nested `fanout` already takes since [W20](work-items/w20-what-a-nested-region-is.md). **W32 measured the outward half and it works**: a map region inside a static one derives the outer prefix (`inner` → `"1"`) and needs an index only for the map's own segment (`merge` → `"1.137"`). **What is NOT measured is a map inside a map**, where `branchPathOf`'s walk would cross two map regions. `branchPathOf` takes its item index **keyed per region** rather than as a single number **precisely so that neither answer costs a signature change** — which is the shape of the decision being deferred rather than the decision. **W32 neither refused it nor blessed it**, which is where P10's *"not taken"* left it. **[W33](work-items/w33-a-map-at-run-time-arity.md) REFUSES the mechanism this rests on and does not answer the question**: a region opener inside a map region is refused at `Driver` construction, because every per-region run-time structure is keyed by the opener's NODE ID and a map enters its branch N times - [Q160](#live), which is where the general finding lives. So a map inside a map was **refused** rather than undecided-and-expressible, and lifting the refusal is what would make this row answerable. **ANSWERED YES by [W84](work-items/w84-what-a-regions-identity-is.md) under [ADR 0062](decisions/0062-a-region-instance-is-its-opener-and-the-openers-own-branch-path.md)**: a map's branch may open a region, the refusal is LIFTED WHOLE rather than narrowed to one of the two kinds, and `FD-V25` is unchanged — the answer is a KEY and not a rule, so a map still reaches a region through its branch TASK's edges exactly as a fanout does. **Measured**: a `fanout` inside a `map` at three items dispatches eleven tasks, opens FOUR region instances (`split@-`, `f2@0`, `f2@1`, `f2@2`), fires three separate joins and completes — where the same document before W84 was refused at `Driver` construction and, before W33's refusal, reported `stuck` with every task `done`. **[Q206](#live) is NOT answered by this**: sibling regions nested inside ONE map region is its shape one level in and P22 declares it out | **resolved** | **[W84](work-items/w84-what-a-regions-identity-is.md)** |
| Q157 | **No [P10](plans/p10-is-fan-out-one-feature-or-two.md) item owns `AGENTS.md`, so the plan as declared ships a node kind the consumer reference never describes.** W32's `Owns` is `src/definition/**` and `spec/**`; W33's is the driver; W34's the matrix; W35's `examples/**`. **The reference is in none of them**, and it is the document `CLAUDE.md` calls *"the consumer reference — what the library looks like from outside, and the only document that does."* W32 hit it as a **late exception**: `index.ts` re-exports `src/definition/regions.ts` with `export *`, so `UnresolvedBranchPath` reached the package root and section 9's executed snippet went red ([Q101](#live)) — **the check working, and the only reason the gap was noticed at all.** W32 made the minimum edit that is not a lie and did **not** document the feature, because no `Driver` dispatches a map yet. **This is [P8](plans/p8-what-can-be-deleted-with-the-gate-green.md)'s *"paths in nobody's `Owns`"* on a product plan rather than a gate one**, and the natural owner is W35, whose exit artifact is a map running on real workers  **P13 IS THE TEST v0.13 ASKED FOR, AND IT IS ANSWERED NO.** v0.13 refused an `Owns` pre-declaration rule for `AGENTS.md`, `spec/README.md` and `scripts/check-docs.mjs` and wrote *if the next product plan repeats it, adopt it*; P13 was declared with no item naming any of the three. **Over three items: `AGENTS.md` yes / yes / NO, `spec/README.md` yes / yes / yes, `scripts/check-docs.mjs` NO / yes / NO.** Only one of the three was taken by all three items, and a rule naming all three would have been wrong twice on each of the other two — so the rule would have traded one recorded exception for two unrecorded assumptions per item. **Late exceptions fell 6 -> 4 -> 1**, the last the lowest of any product item in three plans, with **zero undeclared** throughout. … })` — and eight cases vanish with `all passed` at exit 0. **So counting `*.test.ts` FILES, which is the obvious fix, would not catch it**; the bound has to be on the registered **case** set, which is what `test/runner.ts` prints and nothing reads  **RESOLVED by [W41](work-items/w41-what-a-node-can-reach-and-what-names-it.md), and NOT where this row said the fix would live.** That is why nobody built one for three plans: this row names `test/runner.ts` and `scripts/**`, and both are in nobody`s `Owns`. **The runner and the browser bundle glob the same tree, and a suite that did not load is one `collected()` does not hold** — so the bound is over SUITE NAMES, in `test/definition/l4-corpus.test.ts`, which W41 owns: one assertion, **set equality both ways** for `S12`s reason, in all five runtimes, inside a `test()` body because the runner imports every file before running any case. **Provoked**: renaming W41`s two `.test.ts` files out of the glob went from `21 file(s), 579 assertions, all passed, exit 0` to one named failure. | Q160 | **A region's IDENTITY is its opener's NODE ID, and a `map` makes one opener reachable N times in a run.** [W33](work-items/w33-a-map-at-run-time-arity.md), found by its conformity reader. `joinState`, `firedJoins`, `RegionEvent.regionId` and the fold's `regions` are all keyed by the opener node's id - which was total before v0.8, because every opener was entered at most once. **A map's branch is entered once per item**, so an opener inside a map region is entered N times and instance 2 silently discards instance 1. **Measured on a definition `validateDefinition` accepts with ZERO findings** - `map(split) -> lead -> fanout(f2) -> {p,q} -> join(j2) -> join(collect)` - with all seven tasks settling `done`: the run reported **`stuck`**, `split` branch 1 got no terminal event, and `f2` held all four branch paths of two separate regions under one `joined: true`. **This is [ADR 0036](decisions/0036-a-map-is-a-second-node-kind-and-a-branch-path-inside-one-is-a-property-of-the-dispatch.md)'s own central finding one layer down**: the ADR decided *two features* because `branchOf` is keyed by branch node id and collapses repeated entries. **W33 REFUSES it at construction** with an `InvalidDefinition` naming both nodes, so it is expressible-and-refused rather than expressible-and-silently-wrong. **For lifting it:** *"score each item, then fan out per item"* is an ordinary flow, and [Q156](#live) already asks the narrower *may a map contain a map*. **Against:** the fix is that region identity becomes **(opener node, enclosing path)**, which changes what `RegionEvent.regionId` MEANS, what the fold's `regions` is keyed by, and what `joinStatusOf` and `onUnsatisfiableJoin` name - a decision with its own evidence and its own item. `branchPathOf` already takes its index **per region**, so the id layer is ready and nothing else is. **No corpus flow is refused**: `map-in-fanout` nests a map inside a FANOUT branch, the other direction, where the opener is entered once **[P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md)'s retro gives the verdict its own plan row asked for: keying a per-region structure by node id ALONE is a DEFECT and not a convention.** That plan carried this row with *"fact 3 is its third occurrence and this plan will say whether it is one defect or a convention"*, and [ADR 0040](decisions/0040-what-a-node-can-reach-is-the-runs-own-outputs-and-a-node-id-names-them-relative-to-a-branch-path.md) is the **third** mechanism in three plans to reach for the pair `(node id, branchPath)` — after `branchOf`'s key type ([ADR 0036](decisions/0036-a-map-is-a-second-node-kind-and-a-branch-path-inside-one-is-a-property-of-the-dispatch.md)) and the driver's four run-time maps (this row). **Three independent mechanisms converging on one key is not a coincidence and is what a convention would not do.** **RESOLVED by [W84](work-items/w84-what-a-regions-identity-is.md) under [ADR 0062](decisions/0062-a-region-instance-is-its-opener-and-the-openers-own-branch-path.md): a region INSTANCE is `(opener node id, the opener's OWN branchPath)`, and every per-region structure is keyed by that pair.** `RunState.regions` is two-level — `regions[openerId][openerPath]` — with **no composite string anywhere**, and `RegionEvent` gains `regionPath` on all six variants while `regionId` keeps one meaning AND its value. **The structures were FIVE, not four, and this row said four**: `arrivals` is [W34](work-items/w34-what-a-branch-receives-and-what-a-join-carries.md)'s and postdates W33's conformity reader, so its own doc comment said *like every other per-region structure here* while enumerating four that excluded its own subject — found by W84's declaration step re-taking the list rather than inheriting it, which is what P22's re-take table row 4 exists for ([Q351](#live)). **What was chosen over what is measured rather than argued**: qualifying the OBSERVABLE `regionId` instead costs **twelve silent failure modes** against the two-level road's **sixty NAMED typecheck errors**, so *the cheapest road is the one that fails in silence and the dearest is the one whose whole cost a typechecker prints*. **The corpus could not arbitrate the key**: over both corpora, openers inside a map region are **0 of 50** — which IS the refusal rather than a fact about nesting | **resolved** | **[W84](work-items/w84-what-a-regions-identity-is.md)** |
| Q161 | **At N = 0 the driver must act on a fact NO POLICY CAN SEE, which sharpens the fourth-event question rather than settling it.** [W33](work-items/w33-a-map-at-run-time-arity.md), from its adequacy reader. `spec/join-policy.md` section 5 rejected a fourth region event on the ground that *"both readings - the region finished, and the region failed - reach the same run-level answer"*. **At depth 2 they do not**: a map at N = 0 nested in a fanout branch left that branch **opened with no terminal event at all**, which `JP-R5`'s enumeration does not admit - and `JP-R10`, the mechanism [W22](work-items/w22-what-an-inner-join-does-to-the-outer-one.md) built for exactly that shape, **could not fire**, because it triggers on a policy answering `unsatisfiable` and the ruling is that none can. W33 closed it by having the DRIVER emit `JP-R10`'s consequence directly, on the argument that *a region which opened zero branches can never be satisfied* is **arithmetic over section 2's vocabulary** rather than a judgement: `branch.arrived` is emitted only for a branch that was opened. **So the case for a fourth event is no longer "so a policy can choose between two readings that agree"** - it is that a policy cannot see a fact the driver has to act on alone, which is a different and stronger argument. **Against building it here:** it would answer [Q97](#live) from one case, and ADR 0026 is held for that. **For whoever answers Q97**, with this as the second case | open | no owner - **ADR 0026 held**, see [Q97](#live) |

| Q163 | **A policy's `describe` is observable MID-BURST, and `arrival-count`'s moved on a static region without any verdict moving.** [W33](work-items/w33-a-map-at-run-time-arity.md)'s conformity reader. That item re-sourced `N` from `branch.opened`, and claimed *"no verdict moves on any static region"* - **true, and confirmed**: a `satisfied` answer needs an arrival and `JP-R5` puts every open before one, so the claim cannot break. **But `joinStatusOf` called from an `onRegionEvent` listener** - a pairing `AGENTS.md` section 11 advertises together - now reads `need: 1` on a two-branch fanout's FIRST `branch.opened` where it read `need: 2` before, reaching 2 on the second. `describe` is `JP-R2`'s channel, *"how a UI would ever show a join"*, and `emit` steps the policy **before** notifying the observer precisely so this observation point exists. Recorded in section 3 rather than treated as a defect. **What would make it one:** a consumer that reads `need` as *the region's width* rather than as *what the policy is waiting for* - which is what a UI would naturally do. **Against changing anything:** the alternative is `describe` reporting a number the policy does not yet have, and `JP-R2` exists to expose the policy's own state rather than a tidied version of it | open | no owner |
| Q164 | **`spec/flow-definition.md` has no id prefix for a rule about what a run CARRIES, and `check-spec.mjs`'s `RULE_ID` is a closed list of eight**, so inventing one would make the rule invisible to `C2` (ids declared exactly once) and `C6` (every non-`FD-V` rule names a test owner) — **a rule declared, cited, and checked by nothing.** Found by [W34](work-items/w34-what-a-branch-receives-and-what-a-join-carries.md) trying to place `JP-R11`. That document has `FD-V*` for a **validation** rule and `FD-R*` for a **runtime refusal**, and a payload contract is neither; `FD-R`'s own section is titled *Runtime refusals*, so an `FD-R3` that is not a refusal would break the section's meaning to keep the prefix. **W34's answer was to put both rules in `spec/join-policy.md` section 4**, whose owner table already fixes what the driver does at both ends of a region — which is right for these two and does not generalise: the *ordinary* edge's contract has no region and lives in `flow-definition.md` **as prose with no id at all**. **For a check:** `RULE_ID` is the one place the whole spec's id vocabulary is written down, and it is a literal. **Against:** a prefix per document per rule-kind is a taxonomy nobody asked for, and `scripts/**` is in nobody's `Owns` ([Q102](#live), [Q157](#live)). **What would force it:** a second rule about what a run carries that has no region to hang on. **[P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md) IS that**, by its own declaration | **resolved 2026-09-09 — `FD-C` is the third prefix, and `RULE_PREFIXES` is now bound to `spec/`'s own declaration rows BOTH WAYS** | **W41** ([P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md)) |
| Q165 | **A declared reducer on a `join` node — [Q36](#resolved)'s second candidate, refused by [ADR 0037](decisions/0037-what-a-branch-receives-is-a-property-of-the-fan-out-and-what-a-join-carries-is-a-property-of-the-region.md) rule 5 and refused NARROWLY.** Not because it is wrong: [ADR 0002](decisions/0002-a-flow-definition-is-data-and-a-predicate-is-registered-by-name.md) rule 3's registered-by-name mechanism would carry `reduce: "myReducer"` exactly as it carries `when`, and the shape is additive — a new optional key, a registry lookup, a validator rule, and an `FD-V`-numbered refusal for an unregistered name. It is refused because **a reducer is a function of what the join collects, so it cannot be specified before `JP-R12` is**, and building both at once would let the reducer's convenience decide the collection's shape. **`JP-R12` is what a reducer would receive.** **The concrete forcing case is `join -> map`, and it is HALF expressible — which this row first denied.** It read *"not expressible today ... after ADR 0037 it fails `FD-R2` because the value under `over` is absent"*. **Measured, and false in one direction**: a `branchPath` is a string in `FD-V25`'s `over` charset, so `{"kind": "map", "over": "0"}` after a join names branch 0's entry — `validateDefinition` accepts it with **zero findings**, and with branch 0 settling `['x','y']` the run dispatches `each` twice with `"x"` and `"y"`. **It behaves correctly**, so it is an affordance ADR 0037 created rather than a defect. **What is still not expressible is a map over ALL the collected results**, which is the shape anyone wants: the collection is an object keyed by branch, so no key holds the results as a list, and `over: "1"` fails `FD-R2` the moment branch 1's result is not itself an array. **And the affordance is brittle**: `over: "0"` couples a flow to the fan-out's branch numbering, so inserting a branch silently re-points it. *Score each item, then fan out over the scores* is an ordinary flow, and this is the second construct it is blocked by after [Q160](#live). The workaround is a reshaping `task` between them. **The other candidate answers each invent a shape** — a wrapper key on the join's payload, or an `over` that accepts an object — and a reducer is the one that is already a question. **What `JP-R12` deliberately does not give is a list in item order**, because an array index would be *position in the arrival set* rather than the branch index, and with branch 1 routed away `[r0, r2]`'s index 1 is branch 2's result, silently. **Against:** every reducer anyone has actually wanted so far is `Object.values`, which a consumer writes in the next node. **[P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md) takes the FLOW as a case and not the reducer as a feature**: *score, then fan out over the scores* is one of the three blocked flows [Q11](#live)-in-general is decided from, and its exit artifact runs it on real workers. **The affordance and its limit were both re-measured at that declaration**: `{"kind": "map", "over": "0"}` after a join validates with zero findings and dispatches `r1:each:0:0` and `r1:each:1:0`, and there is still no key holding all the results as a list. **The reducer itself stays refused and unowned** - it is a function of what the join collects, and P12 reopens nothing in `spec/join-policy.md` section 4. **THE FLOW IS NOW EXPRESSIBLE and the reducer is not built** — [ADR 0040](decisions/0040-what-a-node-can-reach-is-the-runs-own-outputs-and-a-node-id-names-them-relative-to-a-branch-path.md): `spec/examples/valid/map-over-region-output.flow.json` runs `ingest -> map -> score -> join -> map(over "scores") -> publish` with `needs: {"scores": ["score"]}` on the second map, **and no `over: "0"` anywhere**, so the brittleness this row names (*insert a branch and it silently re-points*) is gone rather than worked around. `JP-R12` is untouched. **What is still open is the reducer**, and it is now a smaller question: `Object.values` is what a consumer writes in the next node, and `["n"]` is what an author writes instead of reaching for one | open — **the FLOW is closed; the reducer is not** | no owner |
| Q166 | **A `map` instance receives its own item and therefore LOSES the context around it**, and that is a stated cost of [ADR 0037](decisions/0037-what-a-branch-receives-is-a-property-of-the-fan-out-and-what-a-join-carries-is-a-property-of-the-region.md) rule 3 rather than an oversight. Given `{items: [...], config: {...}, runId: '...'}` an instance receives `items[i]` and nothing else — which is what *map* means in every language that has one (`xs.map(f)` calls `f(x_i)`, not `f({...ctx, x: x_i})`), and it is why `over` names a key rather than the whole value. **The workaround is to put the context in the item**, which costs the author a reshaping node and costs the run a copy of the context per item. **Against fixing it here:** every fix is one of [Q11](#live)-in-general's other two candidates — a run-scoped accumulator, or a declared per-edge mapping — so fixing it for a map alone would answer the API question users touch most from **one** construct, which is *a claim written from a mechanism instead of from the cases*. **Measured:** handing each instance its own item costs **2.29 ms** at N = 1000 over a 117 KiB payload against **1.50 ms** for the shipped one-shared-clone, and a clone of that item **7.99 ms**; the alternative of cloning the WHOLE payload per instance is **2207.94 ms** and quadratic, which is what made rule 3 the cheap answer rather than merely the correct one. **[P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md) takes it as a CASE rather than as a question**, which is what this row asks for: it is one of the three blocked flows [Q11](#live)-in-general is decided from, so the fix arrives from the API question rather than from the map. **Reproduced at that plan's declaration**: given `{items, config, runLabel}` the instances receive `"a"` and `"b"` and the other two keys are reachable by **no route at all**. **CLOSED by [ADR 0040](decisions/0040-what-a-node-can-reach-is-the-runs-own-outputs-and-a-node-id-names-them-relative-to-a-branch-path.md)**: `spec/examples/valid/map-with-context.flow.json` declares `needs: {"item": ".", "run": "ingest"}` on the map's branch entry and the instances receive `{"item":"a","run":{items,config,runLabel}}`. The reshaping node this row names as the workaround is not needed, and the copy is one per DECLARED KEY rather than one per item of a restructured payload — though pointing a key at a large value is [Q186](#live) | **resolved 2026-09-09 — [ADR 0040](decisions/0040-what-a-node-can-reach-is-the-runs-own-outputs-and-a-node-id-names-them-relative-to-a-branch-path.md)** | **W41** ([P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md)) |
| Q167 | **Should a driver surface WORKER-lifecycle failures, and it cannot today?** `fyflow-scheduler` **0.5.1** adds `worker.setup.started` / `.completed` / `.failed`, `worker.teardown.*` and `worker.initialization.failed` - a family this library subscribes to **not at all**, by design: `SETTLEMENT_EVENTS` is `task.completed`, `task.failed`, `task.user_action` (`src/driver/driver.ts`) and `spec/outcome-record.md` section 1 fixes the log at two record kinds. Raised by [W36](work-items/w36-the-scheduler-pin-moves-to-0-5-1.md) **because a bump is exactly when a new event family is easiest to adopt by accident**, and adopting one is a decision rather than a consequence of a version number. **What it would buy:** a worker whose `setup()` throws produces tasks that fail for a reason no outcome record can express - the run sees `failed` per task and nothing says *the pool never came up*, which is a different person's bug. **Against:** these are events about a POOL rather than about a run, and `OR-R4` already refuses to log `task.running` and `task.progress` on the ground that they are not settlements - the same argument covers these one layer further out. **And they are dispatched by `WorkerManager` rather than by `FyflowScheduler`**, so a `Driver` holding only a `SchedulerLike` cannot see them without widening that two-method structural interface, which is [ADR 0013](decisions/0013-the-scheduler-is-a-development-dependency-reached-from-the-working-tree.md) rule 1's whole point. **What would force it:** a consumer who cannot tell a failing worker POOL from a flow of failing tasks | open | no owner |
| Q170 | **`CROSS_ROW` counts only the cross-row assertions that run BEFORE the derived-total check, and twelve run after it.** [W35](work-items/w35-a-map-on-real-workers.md). `examples/l6.ts` holds its assertion count down two ways on purpose - a per-row floor derived from each row's own shape, and `DECLARED_TOTAL`, *"a number a human wrote down"*, which exists because W6's adequacy reader measured that both derived halves shared a source. **The two floors do not cover the same assertions.** The derived check runs partway down the file and compares against `rows + CROSS_ROW + PAIR_ROW`, so every assertion after it - W23's two dotted-path clauses, its three printed-table clauses, W19's two foreign-list clauses, - is bounded by `DECLARED_TOTAL` **alone**, and `DECLARED_TOTAL` is the number an author edits when a count moves legitimately. That half is closed by `ROW_KEYS` and by a bound read from the spec corpus; this half is not. It is a floor that an author is *invited* to edit standing alone in front of the seven assertions this file's readers have most often found deletable | open | no owner |

| Q172 | **A rule 8 harness that restores with `git checkout` deletes the work it is measuring, and the repository has no stated procedure for one.** [W35](work-items/w35-a-map-on-real-workers.md), and it cost this item its whole uncommitted `examples/` tree on the harness's first run - five rows, three flows, a worker role, a pool and a provenance fix, recovered from the session transcript. **Rule 8 says what to break and how to grade it and nothing about the harness that applies the break.** Every item since W24 has written one, each from scratch: `check-*-selftest.mjs` run mutants in a scratch copy, and the ad-hoc ones edit the tree in place and restore it - by `git checkout` if the author is thinking about the committed state, by saved bytes if the author is thinking about the working one. **This is [Q151](#live) one layer out**: P9 found a harness writing mutants into a directory its child deletes and grading the deletion; this is a harness whose *restore* is the deletion, and neither is visible in the result - the first reports `12/12 bounded`, the second reports a run of `ABORT`s that read as anchors gone stale. **Candidate answers**: a shared `dev-dist/` harness module; a rule-8 clause requiring the tree to be committed or copied first; or nothing, on the ground that both instances were caught within one run. **W35's own harness now snapshots bytes and asserts the restore**, which fixes one file and not the class | open | no owner |

Deno refuses a JSR version published less than 24 hours ago - a supply-chain control, on by default - so with both pins moved to 0.5.2 `deno run` failed with *"newer than the specified minimum dependency age"* while `check:scheduler` reported **six green rows**, `D3` among them. Its stated premise is *both runtimes reach that same version*; what it binds is that two files agree about a number. **This is the third instance in three items of one shape** - [W35](work-items/w35-a-map-on-real-workers.md) found `D6` binding a command's runtime and resolution but not its FILE, [W36](work-items/w36-the-scheduler-pin-moves-to-0-5-1.md) found `D3` binding its inputs but not its PREMISE, and this is the version bound but not its reachability. **Not silent**: the gate went red on `test:l6:deno`, loudly, which is the only reason the day was not lost. **What a check would need**: resolving the JSR specifier costs a network call, which no check here makes - `D1`-`D7` are all file reads - so this would be the first, and [Q133](#live)'s cost question applies. **The cheaper half**: `check:scheduler` could report `D3` as *not run* rather than `ok` when it has not seen the version resolve, which is `ran.Dx`'s existing vocabulary and W5's own finding | open | no owner |

| Q176 | **Where a check LIVES is a cost decision, and nothing here has ever treated it as one.** [W38](work-items/w38-the-dispatch-nothing-binds.md). Its clause costs **0.53 s**. Built inside `check-source.mjs` exactly as declared, it cost **236 s** — because `check-source-selftest.mjs` re-runs `check-source` for every probe, every `META` grading and every `SELF` entry, **roughly four hundred times**, so a per-run cost is multiplied by four hundred: `check:source` went 0.7 s to 9.7 s and `check:source:selftest` **81.2 s to 317 s**. Moved to its own entry, the same clause costs the gate **+9 s** including its own self-test. *A check's price is not what it costs to run — it is what it costs to run multiplied by how often the thing around it is re-run.* **The multiplier is a property of the file a clause is added to and is written down nowhere**, so the next author to add one to `check-source` or `check-markdown` will pay it again without seeing it. **What would close it**: each self-test knows how many times it re-runs its check and could print it, which turns the multiplier into a number an author reads before choosing a file. Owned by no item; [P11](plans/p11-what-should-the-gate-prove.md)'s **W39** needs it for the cost decision. **W39 paid it again, at a factor of 150 and with a second multiplier this row does not name.** `G4` and `G5` cost `check:wiring` **0.61 s to 1.9 s**; they cost `check:wiring:selftest` **65.5 s to ~200 s** and **502 process starts to 2264**, because that self-test re-runs the check for 34 probes, 14 gradings and 6 SELF entries and each `G4` run now spawns the runner three times. **And the second multiplier is EXCLUSIVITY**: that entry cannot share the tree, so every second of it is on the gate's critical path. *A check's price is what it costs to run, times how often the thing around it is re-run, times whether it can run beside anything else.* | open | no owner |
| Q177 | **L0 does not reach `scripts/**`, so the files that decide whether every other rung is honest are the only ones neither linted nor typechecked.** [W38](work-items/w38-the-dispatch-nothing-binds.md), found by its conformity reader. `lint` is `deno lint src/ test/ examples/ index.ts`; no tsconfig `include` names `scripts/` (`tsconfig.json` is `[index.ts, src/**/*.ts, test/**/*.ts]`, `.node` and `.browser` narrower, `.examples` is `examples/**`). So an item's L0 row can read *pass* while saying nothing whatever about the deliverable — W38's did. **Nine `check-*.mjs` files and six self-tests, about 6 000 lines, are held up by `node --check` at spawn time and by nothing else**, which catches a parse error and no unused binding, no shadowed name, no unreachable arm. It is not a typechecking gap that matters most but a LINT one: three defects in this item alone were a literal matching the wrong occurrence, and none is a lint finding either — so the honest form of this question is *what would a rung over `scripts/**` actually catch*, measured before it is built. **The cost is the objection**: `deno lint` over `scripts/` is free, a `tsc --checkJs` project is not, and [Q133](open-questions.md) is the plan this sits inside | open | no owner |
| Q178 | **A literal that quotes the pattern it searches for is a false match for itself, and it happened twice in one hour in one file.** [W38](work-items/w38-the-dispatch-nothing-binds.md). `check-wiring.mjs` declares `const DISPATCH = "if (process.argv.includes('--self-test')) {";` and then `text.indexOf(DISPATCH)` found **its own declaration** — a string literal — brace-matched from the `{` inside it, and reported all three of `G2`'s clauses against the file that defines them. Fixed by anchoring to a line start. **Then the probe written to test that clause did the same thing**: `t.replace(DISPATCH, 'if (false) {')` rewrote the constant instead of the dispatch, so the probe ran a WORKING check and reported it as SURVIVED — a false negative, which is the direction that does not announce itself. This is [W25](work-items/w25-a-link-is-a-link-and-an-anchor-is-an-anchor.md)'s *a comment that quotes the code it explains makes that code unmutatable* one layer out, and W25's fix (move the quote) does not apply, because here the literal IS the code. **What would close it**: a convention that any literal a check searches for is anchored (line start, word boundary) rather than matched bare — cheap, and it would have caught both. Live in every self-test that anchors a mutation on a string the file also contains | open | no owner |

| Q179 | **Wall time is not a reproducible cost measure for this gate, and every cost claim in the register was taken from one pass.** [W39](work-items/w39-what-the-gate-proves-and-what-it-costs.md)'s declaration. Two consecutive passes over the same tree, same machine, nothing else running: **482.9 s and 545.8 s, +13.0%** at the total, with `check:docs:selftest` at **+43.3%** (119.5 -> 171.1), `typecheck` **+108.8%** and `lint` **+126.2%**, while `check:markdown:selftest` moved **0.0%** and `check:source:selftest` **+0.8%**. **It is not a scaling factor that divides out** - the two cheapest entries more than doubled and the most expensive did not move. So [P11](plans/p11-what-should-the-gate-prove.md)'s stated exit criterion, *the same entries timed in one pass before and after with the change argued from the pair*, cannot distinguish a change from noise below that band; and [Q133](#live)'s numbers, P9's retro's 4.3 s / 347.0 s, P8's retro's 2m31-3m14 and this plan's own 433.7 s are all single readings. **A deterministic proxy does exist**: the seven self-tests spawn **2583 node processes** and that count does not vary - 2583 x 0.174 s reproduces the 449.8 s self-test half to within a second, and it independently confirms W38's 588/461 for `check:source:selftest` at 587/463 three commits later. **What would close it**: state a band or count the work, per claim. **[W39](work-items/w39-what-the-gate-proves-and-what-it-costs.md) did both and the band is WIDER than this row measured**: three BACK-TO-BACK runs of `check:markdown:selftest` alone spread **127.8 to 169.2 s, 32%** — so the variance is not a whole-pass drift that a per-entry method escapes, it is inside one entry. Its cost claims are stated with the side of the band they fall on, per claim: the total (−38.3%) and the self-test half (−40.5%) clear it; the checks (+0.3 s) and L0 (−0.2 s) are inside it and no claim is made. **And the deterministic half is confirmed with one exception**: six of the seven entries reproduce this row's start counts exactly three commits later, and `check:policies:selftest` reads **170 against 222**, twice. *Stable within a tree; not necessarily across trees, for one entry in seven, and unexplained* | open | **W39 — measured further, not closed** |
| Q180 | **A third of the gate is Node booting.** [W39](work-items/w39-what-the-gate-proves-and-what-it-costs.md)'s declaration: `node -e ""` is **59 ms** here (n=20), the seven self-tests spawn **2583** processes, and **2583 x 59 ms = 152 s of the 450 s self-test half is interpreter startup doing no work at all** - about 31% of a 483 s gate. Two of the seven carry it today and **both were found by running the seven and reading the failures**, not by reading the files. **For a check**: a `check-*-selftest.mjs` that names `node_modules` outside a comment, or writes a path under `dev-dist/` that is not derived from `process.pid` or from its own check's name, owes the marker — both are greps and both would have caught these two. **Against**: a heuristic that is wrong in either direction is worse than a stated limitation, and the true predicate is *what does this process write that another reads*, which no grep decides. **Its absence is a flake with no obvious cause**, which is the loudness argument `G1`'s missing negative half turns on, pointing the other way | open | no owner |

| Q186 | **A `needs` reference to a large value inside a map region is [ADR 0037](decisions/0037-what-a-branch-receives-is-a-property-of-the-fan-out-and-what-a-join-carries-is-a-property-of-the-region.md)'s REFUSED quadratic, made available at the author's request.** [W41](work-items/w41-what-a-node-can-reach-and-what-names-it.md), [ADR 0040](decisions/0040-what-a-node-can-reach-is-the-runs-own-outputs-and-a-node-id-names-them-relative-to-a-branch-path.md). That ADR measured *a clone of the whole payload per map instance* at **2207.94 ms against 1.50 ms at N = 1000** and refused it as unaffordable for the feature that needed it; `needs: {"run": "ingest"}` on a map's branch entry **is** that clone, per key. Measured, timing the map's fan-out alone over a 1000-item payload: no `needs` **6.5 ms**, `{"item": "."}` **8.0 ms**, one more key naming a small result **10.6 ms**, the same key naming the 1000-item result **546.6 ms**. **The mechanism is linear; what an author points it at is what costs**, and the same reference outside a map region is one clone. **For acting:** nothing warns, and the difference between a cheap flow and an 85x one is which node id an author typed. **Against:** it is visible in the document, it is the author's own declaration, and a driver-side guard would have to guess how large is too large — which is [Q22](#live)'s constraint question, unresolved. **Why a reference names a NODE and never a path inside its value**: a path language would make *the small part of the big thing* expressible and would move this cost out of the document and into a selector **DECIDED by [W46](work-items/w46-what-a-reference-costs-and-whether-anything-says-so.md): NOTHING WARNS, and the decision is written down** — `spec/flow-definition.md` section 3 v0.14 and no ADR, on [Q190](#live)'s precedent (recorded in that section and asserted at ladder L2). **It stays OPEN and that is the point**: *how large is too large* occurs in exactly one place in this register — **this row** — and NOT in [Q22](#live), which asks whether a result must be structured-CLONEABLE. Resolving this row would delete the only place the threshold question exists, which W46's conformity reader found while checking the deferral. **Three of the four reasons hold and one was the opposite of the truth**: (1) there is no threshold, and it is this row's own; (2) the size-free static predicate — *a reference on a node inside a map region naming one outside it* — is **RIGHT**, and `map-with-context`'s is the CLIFF rather than a false positive (**604.9-643.6 ms as the corpus writes it against 7.9-9.1 ms with the `run` key removed**, ~70x on the shipped document); it is declined because it fires on **3 of 3 intended uses and nothing else**, which restates the feature; (3) a REPORT channel exists — [ADR 0027](decisions/0027-an-inbound-task-id-that-does-not-parse-was-minted-by-no-driver.md)'s *ignore it, but report it* — and is weighed rather than absent, because *resolved once per item* is a fact about the DOCUMENT and `onUnmintedTask` reports a fact about a RUN; and (4) **measuring is NOT the obstacle** — a size measurement is **~29% of ONE clone**, taken once per (node, key) against N clones, so **~0.03%** of what it would warn about at N = 1000. *(This row's own *against* clause makes the guessing the whole of the objection, and it is right — the affordability was never the question.)* **What would force it:** an author reporting a flow whose cost surprised them, a threshold arriving from anywhere, or a reference form whose size is not a property of a whole node. **And decision 5's two boundary checks are PRICED ([W73](work-items/w73-the-wiring-check-a-verdict-per-edge-and-two-boundaries-validated-once.md)'s fact D)**: 0.47-1.28 µs per check on every corpus payload and resolution, 0.35 ms at the walking worst case (a 1000-item array under a schema that walks items), against ~100 ms per real run — once per run and once per human action, ON by default by the maintainer's choice at W73's stop-and-ask 2, which is the run-time slice this row's *nothing warns* leaves per-settlement validation OUT of | **open — decided as NOTHING WARNS, and the decision is written down** | **W46** ([P13](plans/p13-when-is-a-declared-reference-refused.md)) — decided, not closed |

0042](decisions/0042-a-reference-says-whether-it-is-conditional-and-the-bare-form-means-the-target-certainly-ran.md) ([W45](work-items/w45-what-a-reference-must-be-able-to-say.md)), and BOTH halves of precedent | **resolved 2026-09-10 — [ADR 0042](decisions/0042-a-reference-says-whether-it-is-conditional-and-the-bare-form-means-the-target-certainly-ran.md)** | **W45**
