| Q293 | **A `Driver` has no completion promise, so "the run is over" is eight lines a consumer copies.** [W69](work-items/w69-a-getting-started-example-a-consumer-can-copy.md), measured writing the getting-started snippet: `runtime.start` hands back a started `Driver` (P18 decision 10) and the only way to learn the run has ENDED is to append every `onFoldInput` and re-fold until `isTerminal` — `AGENTS.md` §12's pattern, which the getting-started snippet reproduces verbatim and a consumer will copy from the README. The reason it is so is sound and is [ADR 0014](decisions/0014-the-driver-produces-the-folds-ordered-input-sequence.md)'s: a run's state is a pure fold of what the driver reports, and the driver deliberately does not subscribe to itself. **For a promise**: it is the first thing every consumer writes, and a `runtime.start` that returned `{ driver, done: Promise<RunState> }` — the fold kept by the RUNTIME, which already holds the driver — would keep ADR 0014 intact because the runtime is not the fold. **Against**: a promise is a second observer of terminality beside the consumer's own fold, and [Q289](#live) says `start` starts INSIDE the call, so what the promise had seen before the caller held it is the same window. Not P19's; a small item of its own, or never. **[P20](plans/p20-what-the-runtime-owes-a-consumer.md)'s declaration MEASURED both halves of this row and BOTH are wrong, one of them in the direction that decides the question.** *Eight lines* is **THIRTEEN copies and 135 lines** across `AGENTS.md` and `README.md`, in **three shapes that disagree** — ten carry the `queueMicrotask` quiescence guard and **no deadline** (so a run that never becomes terminal hangs for ever, which is the failure W71's own declaration records its author's first spike hitting), two carry a 5 ms poll and a deadline and **no microtask**, and **exactly ONE of the thirteen carries both** — while the reference's own prose at `AGENTS.md:112` calls the microtask *"load-bearing rather than decoration"* (fact 3b, `dev-dist/p20/fact3b.txt`). **And the case AGAINST is measured FALSE on a real `FyflowScheduler`** (fact 4): exactly ONE fold input arrives before `start` returns, reproducing W67's number, so the window is real — and a `.then` attached **fifty macrotask turns after the run was over** still receives `completed`, because **a promise RETAINS its resolution and the window is precisely what a promise is immune to**. [Q289](#live) is about a CLOSURE that needs the `driver` binding, which `done` gives nothing to, and it stays open. What survives of the objection is the *second observer* half alone, and P20 decision 2 answers it by making `done` the SAME expression moved — `fold` and `isTerminal`, one definition with one more caller — rather than a second source. **Owned by P20's W77 and W78** | open | **P20, W77/W78** |
| Q297 | *Resolved - see the Resolved table.* | **resolved by [W76](work-items/w76-what-start-accepts.md)** — forwarded, the two lists bound both ways, and the orphan count of P20 fact 1`s partition taken to ZERO. No ADR | W76 |
| Q300 | **The L4 corpus registry's workers are `corpus://` URLs no check can RUN, so the two corpora now disagree about what a schema can be checked against.** [W72](work-items/w72-the-contract-an-example-beside-every-schema-and-a-worker-run-on-it.md)'s conformance check runs the L6 corpus's fourteen modules and confirms twelve output schemas on a run; `spec/examples/registry.json`'s seven workers have URLs nothing loads, declare no `inputSchema` or `outputSchema`, and the one schema they do declare carries an `examples` that ladder L4 resolves and no worker is ever run on. W73's wiring check needs BOTH schemas on every edge of a corpus flow, and the L4 corpus is where the flow documents are — so W73 either writes schemas on workers it cannot run (declarations the check cannot confirm, fact 6's shape) or points the wiring check at the L6 corpus alone. Named OUT of W72 and left to W73, which owns `spec/examples/**` for its miswire. **RESOLVED at [W73](work-items/w73-the-wiring-check-a-verdict-per-edge-and-two-boundaries-validated-once.md), 2026-09-18, by the maintainer's answer to that item's stop-and-ask 1 — road B: the check's LIVE corpus is the L6 one, whose fourteen workers `check:workers` runs, and the L4 corpus stays UNCHECKED**, every one of its 56 consumers a verdict by name and the check's totality asserted over them at L1 (`test/definition/wiring.test.ts`); road A was priced at 14 invented schemas in 273 lines that nothing could run. The check's positive cases live in a THIRD corpus, `spec/examples/wiring/` — one document per verdict kind, named by the verdict it earns as `invalid/`'s are by rule id, with its own registry of five workers with opposed schemas — so the two corpora disagree by design and a third says what a miswire looks like | resolved | W73 |
| Q301 | **A witness is a floor and the verdict says so, but nothing STOPS a downstream tool from treating a witness verdict as a declared one.** ADR 0056 rule 3 makes `examples` optional with fact 5's witness as the fallback; [W72](work-items/w72-the-contract-an-example-beside-every-schema-and-a-worker-run-on-it.md) measured on the shipped document that the witness is `{}` for thirteen of fourteen corpus input schemas and reaches fewer output paths in five of twelve described workers, and `check-workers` prints which road it took. W73's transformed-edge verdict and W74's sample-and-diff loop inherit the same fallback, and a transform over `{}` is `undefined` — so their verdicts must carry the same word, or an author reads *the sample passed* of a sample nobody wrote. Whether the word is enough, or a witness-based verdict should be a distinct kind (*fits on a witness*), is W73's first design question. **RESOLVED at [W73](work-items/w73-the-wiring-check-a-verdict-per-edge-and-two-boundaries-validated-once.md): the WORD, carried by a REQUIRED field, and a distinct kind refused.** Every transformed verdict carries `sample: { source: 'example' \| 'witness', arity?, value, result }`, `describeVerdict` prints the source (and the arity) on every line, and the suite asserts it on the fixture corpus and on a constructed witness road; a distinct kind would multiply the plan's four verdicts into six for a difference the field already makes, and a downstream tool switches on `kind` and reads `sample.source` beside it — W74's loop inherits exactly that | resolved | W73 |
| Q302 | **A conformance check on ONE example confirms a schema at one value, and a bound the worker's code does not keep over its declared input domain is invisible to it.** [W72](work-items/w72-the-contract-an-example-beside-every-schema-and-a-worker-run-on-it.md)'s conformity reader, deriving from the workers' CODE before reading any run: `split` sets each item's `confidence` to `word.length / 10` over an unbounded `text`, so an eleven-letter word yields 1.1 against the declared `maximum: 1`; `weigh`'s `score` is `round(item.confidence * run.confidence * 100)` with `run.confidence` unbounded by its input schema, so a value of 5 yields 250 against `maximum: 100` — both green under `check:workers`, whose one example never reaches either. Both corrected in the corpus (the bound dropped where the code does not keep it, the input bounded where the code assumes it) and checked in as probes (`c1`/`c2` caught with the example that reaches the bound, `c1b`/`c2b` declared without it). **This is P19 fact 6 in a second direction**: fact 6 was an author's schema wrong AT the example; this is an author's schema right at the example and wrong past it, which one run cannot see and which the plan's prediction 2 already names as the sample-based road's limit (*a witness is not a proof*). **What would close it**: a generated input at the schema's BOUNDS rather than its floor (fact 5's generator sits ON a minimum and never a maximum, and cannot reach an eleven-letter word from `{ type: 'string' }`), or a second declared example chosen to reach a bound — W74's sample-and-diff loop's territory; whether the check should run EVERY declared example rather than the first is the cheap half of it. **Extended at [W73](work-items/w73-the-wiring-check-a-verdict-per-edge-and-two-boundaries-validated-once.md): the WIRING check is a second observer of exactly this shape** — the bound W72 dropped on `split`'s per-item confidence appeared as SEVEN `no` verdicts, one on every consumer that still kept it (`score`'s and `weigh`'s `maximum: 1`, through six map items and one `needs`), which one worker's run could not see and every edge could. The maintainer's choice at W73's stop-and-ask 4 was the OTHER resolution — the code made to keep the bound (`Math.min(1, word.length / 10)` in `examples/workers/shared.ts`) and the schema restored — because every other confidence the corpus declares is a probability. *A bound a producer does not keep is invisible to its own check and visible at every consumer's*. **Extended at [W80](work-items/w80-what-a-run-time-result-check-is.md), which CONSTRUCTED the instance this row no longer has**: both named violations were closed by W73, so `OR-R13`'s case had to be built rather than found — and the run-time check is the OTHER answer to *what would close it*, because the bound is then tested on every real value the worker ever returns, which no number of declared examples can match | open | W80 |
| Q305 | **The wiring relation reads an OPEN producer as never emitting a key the consumer describes but does not require, and that is a stated LENIENCY rather than a soundness clause.** [W73](work-items/w73-the-wiring-check-a-verdict-per-edge-and-two-boundaries-validated-once.md), measured at the build: read strictly — *the producer may put anything there* — every open output schema into a consumer with an optional typed property is `no`; `typed`'s echoed `items[]` (`{ type: object }`) into `rank`'s `properties.confidence` read `no` under the strict reading and `fits` under the lenient one, and P19 fact 3's harness was lenient without saying so. Kept lenient because an author who leaves an output schema open means *there are keys I do not describe*, not *the keys you describe may hold anything*; the cost is a value the relation calls `fits` that a producer may emit wrong-typed under a described key, and it is named in `src/definition/wiring.ts`'s header and in `spec/flow-definition.md`'s verdict table with a control in the suite so the reading is a choice. **What would close it**: a producer-side keyword the subset lacks (`additionalProperties: <schema>`, [Q306](#live)), or the consumer REQUIRING the key — the one road already read strictly | open | no owner |
| Q306 | **A join successor's per-branch shape is the wiring check's own knowledge, and no author can DECLARE it: the subset cannot say `additionalProperties: <schema>`.** [W73](work-items/w73-the-wiring-check-a-verdict-per-edge-and-two-boundaries-validated-once.md)'s declaration, fact B — [P19](plans/p19-what-a-schema-buys-an-author.md) decision 8's first measurement: `schemaProblem` refuses a schema under `additionalProperties` by name, so `JP-R12`'s record over a `map` — *any key, each of shape S* — has no spelling in the subset, and a join successor's `inputSchema` is `{ type: object }` or `{}` at best (the corpus's `publish` declares `{}`). The check carries the shape itself: a fan-out's record EXACTLY (every declared path a property, the certain ones required, no other key) and a map's as an internal `additionalProperties: <schema>` with a `branchKeys` marker that `fits` reads and no author may write — so the verdict is right and the DOCUMENT cannot say what the check knows. P19 prediction 1 named this extension as its own reversal condition and W73 did not take it, because nothing an author writes in either corpus needed it. **What would force it**: a consumer whose join successor must refuse a branch shape from its OWN declaration rather than from the check's derivation | open | no owner |
| Q307 | **A driver refused at `start()` is UNSTARTED and still SUBSCRIBED: the constructor attached its listeners, and nothing detaches them.** [W73](work-items/w73-the-wiring-check-a-verdict-per-edge-and-two-boundaries-validated-once.md), found building ladder L6's refuse-at-start rows. `FD-R4` refuses BEFORE `started` is set, so the same driver may be started again with a corrected payload (measured at L2, `test/driver/boundary.test.ts`) — but a caller who instead builds a second driver leaves the first one listening for ever, and `runtime.start` IS such a caller: it constructs a driver per call and mints a fresh `r<n>`, which is why the L6 harness starts its refused attempt with the run id UNSET rather than share an id between the refused driver and the row's run (two drivers minting one id set is the topology ADR 0023 cannot see). The unstarted driver ignores every settlement (nothing it dispatched — ADR 0023 tier 2) and holds its definition and the registry it was handed, so the cost is a listener per refused start and a consumed minted id, and neither is visible to anything. [P4](plans/p4-the-drivers-lifetime-and-the-handles-it-gives-out.md) fixed a driver's lifetime at construction and never asked what a driver that never started holds — [Q53](#live)'s surface question one step earlier. **For acting**: `createRuntime`'s `start` could hold the payload to the schemas BEFORE constructing a driver (it has the registry, the flow and the walk), or a refused driver could detach. **Against**: one listener that does nothing, on a road a consumer takes by handing a bad payload. **And the refused driver is a value `createRuntime`'s caller never HOLDS** (W73's adequacy reader, f1): `runtime.start` builds a driver and starts it in one call, so on the recommended road the refusal is an exception and the unstarted, subscribed driver is unreachable — which is why ladder L6 observes the refusal and the scheduler's task count while *the same driver then started* is L2's claim alone | open | no owner |
| Q310 | **An EXECUTED snippet's assertions are bound by their PRESENCE and by nothing else: every guard in the reference's exit artifact can be made a tautology with `check:docs` at exit 0.** [W74](work-items/w74-the-assists-and-the-exit-artifact.md)'s adequacy reader (C3/C4), measured on section 15: `X4` refuses a `run` snippet with NO `throw` — confirmed, all seven guards deleted is `FAIL X4` — and that is a PRESENCE test. Replacing each of the seven with `if (arc.kind !== arc.kind) throw …` in both copies, and then ALSO changing the step-3 candidate to the already-correct expression so the section stops demonstrating a `transform-fails` at all, is **`check:docs` EXIT=0, eight checks ok**. `X8` holds README byte-equal to whatever AGENTS says, so the two copies move together. **This generalises past W74**: the reference carries 27 `run` snippets and every claim in every one of them rests on the same shape, which is [P3](plans/p3-a-consumer-reference-the-gate-keeps-true.md)'s *rot is what the gate prevents, absorption is what it cannot see* one layer in — a snippet that still runs and asserts nothing is not rot. **What W74 did instead** is what the rule already prescribes: a SECOND OBSERVER for the claims with none. Five of section 15's seven are re-asserted in `test/definition/assist.test.ts`; the two that were not — *the flow runs to `completed`* and *`{ words: 3 }` comes out of it* — now have the same producer-transform-consumer chain derived at L1, and the snippet's own expected values are read off the registry rather than written beside the run. **What would close it in general**: comparing a `run` snippet's STDOUT to the `//` comments beside it, which is checkable (five of section 15's six output comments are already byte-exact and the sixth is a picture — the conformity reader's finding 13), or a count of `throw` sites per snippet held as a floor. Both are `check-docs`' and neither is W74's. **Against**: a stdout comparison makes every timing, id and ordering in a snippet load-bearing, which is `doc/workflow.md` section 5 rule 5's own refusal | open | no owner |
| Q311 | **`pathsOf` walks four keywords and an object-valued `const` or `enum` is a fifth road it does not take.** [W74](work-items/w74-the-assists-and-the-exit-artifact.md)'s conformity reader, finding 5, measured on schemas `schemaProblem` ACCEPTS: `pathsOf({const: {a: 1}})` and `pathsOf({enum: [{a: 1}, {b: 2}]})` each return `$` alone, because `typeNames` reads those two keywords to decide the TYPE and the walk then finds no `properties` to follow. The types are right and the paths stop at the root, so an author writing a transform over such a schema is shown nothing under it although the schema determines it exactly. **It costs nothing today**: no schema in any of the three corpora has an object-valued `const` or `enum` (the two `const`s in the L6 registry are `true`), so this is a stated bound rather than a measured loss, and the module's header says so. **What would force it**: a consumer whose worker declares an `enum` of object shapes — which is also prediction 3's `oneOf` arriving by another door, since *one of these SHAPES* is what such an `enum` is. **Against**: a fifth road means the walk must MERGE the paths of several values, and two `enum` members that differ in shape give a path that exists in one and not the other — the `required` composition one level out, with no obvious answer | open | no owner |
| Q312 | **What puts a READING in `spec/` is not what W74's criterion said, and the criterion would have kept W73's own check out.** [W74](work-items/w74-the-assists-and-the-exit-artifact.md)'s conformity reader, finding 11. That item declared `Spec: NONE` on the ground that *every assist is a reading; none refuses anything, none changes what a node receives, none is reachable from a run* — and the reader measured all three clauses TRUE of `assist.ts` (zero `throw`s, no importer under `src/driver/**` or `src/scheduler/**`) **and equally true of `checkWiring`**, which sits in `spec/flow-definition.md` section 3 under a heading named for these very functions, with two normative tables and no rule id of its own. So the stated criterion does not distinguish the two. **The reading W74 records instead** is whether a SECOND IMPLEMENTATION would need the text: the wiring relation is a decision about what fits what, with clauses a conformity reader must read a module against; a path walk over a subset the spec already enumerates, a substitution over a relation it already specifies, and a signature no run reads are not. *(`Spec: NONE` is measured correct either way — `check:spec` is 7 / 61 / 107 with C4 at 34 — and this is about the argument.)* **Why it is a question rather than a rule**: `spec/README.md` rule 1 says what a spec document IS and nothing says when a pure function's BEHAVIOUR becomes normative, and the two data points here point opposite ways. **What would force it**: a second implementation of this library, or a consumer asking what `pathsOf` guarantees | open | no owner |
| Q315 | **A binding that must read SOURCE TEXT has no cheap home, and the road taken costs 193 KB of browser bundle.** [W76](work-items/w76-what-start-accepts.md). P20 decision 5 says the `Pick`/spread comparison is *a TEST rather than a `check-source` clause*, because the comparison must know that `joinPolicy` is legitimately spread-less. But a test runs in five runtimes and the browser bundle carries only what `scripts/build-browser-tests.mjs` inlines, so the two `src/**` files had to be added to it — **corpus 386.1 KB -> 579.1 KB, +193.0 KB**, the FIFTH time a suite reading a file outside `spec/examples/` has reached that list. Three roads were priced at the item's declaration and the maintainer chose this one: **(a)** the test plus the inline; **(b)** the two lists as a test and the cross-file partition as a `check-source` clause, which is what `S7`, `S14` and `S16` already are in kind but costs a new check id with its probes, `META` and `SELF` entries, in two files the plan predicted UNNEEDED; **(c)** the test skipped in the browser, which is a rung green on a test that did not run with no `RT-R10` reason behind it. **What the road does not reach**: nothing outside a test reads the two lists, so an item editing `src/scheduler/runtime.ts` learns of the binding only by running `npm test` — where `check-source` would have been in the same chain entry as `lint`. **Against a check clause**: `deno lint`'s and `tsc`'s own output cannot express *this name is in the Pick and in no spread line*, so a clause would re-implement the same two regexes one directory over, and obligation (c)'s LIMIT says two regexes over one text are one observer however many files they live in | open | no owner |
| Q316 | **P20 fact 1's partition table is not a partition, and the harness that produced it cannot see that.** [W76](work-items/w76-what-start-accepts.md), found by the item's own orphan test failing on its FIRST run. `fact1-partition.mjs` derives road (c) from `RuntimeOptions`' whole DECLARATION; four of that interface's six members — `base`, `flows`, `createPool`, `createScheduler` — are the runtime's own and are **not `DriverOptions` members at all**. So the plan's sentence *the runtime supplies two, `RuntimeRegistry` carries six, `RuntimeOptions` carries six, `StartOptions` carries seven — and the partition is clean* is **21 slots over 18 members**. Derived from what `start` actually FORWARDS the road is **two**, and `2 + 6 + 2 + 7 = 17` with `onRegionEvent` the eighteenth. **The conclusion survives and only the arithmetic under it fails**, which is why nobody caught it: the orphan count is 1 under either reading, and the harness prints four road sizes and never adds them up. *A partition claim is checkable by addition and the harness that made it did not add.* Reading the road as the FORWARDING also makes it a real binding rather than a superset — a `DriverOptions` member added to `RuntimeOptions` and never spread would still be an orphan, and reading the declaration would have hidden it. **Also here: `runId` is on TWO roads under the reading fact 1 does not take** — the runtime mints it AND `StartOptions` declares it in its own body, which fact 1's `Pick`-only extraction cannot see, and which [W71](work-items/w71-the-reference-stops-teaching-a-fake-scheduler.md)'s conformity reader had measured from the other end one plan earlier. **What would close it**: a facts harness that asserts its own arithmetic, which is [Q253](#live)'s shape from the side where the harness is not vacuous but wrong about its own scope | open | no owner |
| Q317 | **A dated ceiling whose SITE the expiring item CONSUMES still matches its anchor, so rule 7 (b) cannot abort it and the verdict is right for the wrong reason.** [W76](work-items/w76-what-start-accepts.md), re-running [W71](work-items/w71-the-reference-stops-teaching-a-fake-scheduler.md)'s `f3` and `f3b` as `doc/workflow.md` rule 8's dated-ceiling clause requires — in the item that moves the value, which is what P18's and P19's retros both refused to make a hand-off for. `f3` adds `'onRegionEvent'` to the `Pick`; W76 shipped that name, so the edit now appends a DUPLICATE union member, which TypeScript collapses. The anchor matches, the file changes, no abort fires, and the probe reports **DECLARED (survives)** — under a stated reason that is now FALSE, because the `Pick`'s membership IS bound. `f3b` adds the name AND the forwarding line, expected CAUGHT because §10's `regionEvents !== 0` guard fired; W76 shipped the forwarding and inverted that guard, so its second edit is a duplicate spread line and it came back **SURVIVED against its declaration**. **P18's retro recorded *a ceiling whose SITE no longer exists is neither confirmed nor falsified*; this is a THIRD shape — the site exists, the anchor matches, and the EDIT is a semantic no-op.** rule 7 (b) asks whether the probe changed its INPUT, which it did; the workflow's own answer, *a probe can change the input without changing an observable*, is exactly this case, and no mechanism in the harness reports it. **What would close it**: a ceiling whose edit is asserted to change a NAMED observable, not merely the bytes — which is the reachability half of rule 8's *survivor* definition applied to the declared survivors rather than to the caught ones | open | no owner |

| Q320 | **An always-on completion handle costs one copy of every result per RETAINED HANDLE and one `structuredClone` per settlement, and both are now unconditional.** [W77](work-items/w77-what-start-returns.md), [ADR 0058](decisions/0058-runtime-start-returns-a-completion-handle-the-runtime-keeps.md), and it is the price [P20](plans/p20-what-the-runtime-owes-a-consumer.md)'s Scope Out obliges the item to state. `start` now installs an `onFoldInput` on EVERY run, and `src/driver/driver.ts`'s `append` calls `freezeInput` only when one is present — so a deep `structuredClone` of every result is now made on every settlement of every run, a SECOND unconditional hot-path copy beside `OR-R2`'s, which is what [Q22](#live)'s row calls the thing ADR 0007 *explicitly did not want*. The kept `RunState` then pins those clones by reference (`step` writes `result: r.result`), so a run retains P15's own quantity — (tasks settled) x (bytes each returned) — a second time. **Measured by IDENTITY rather than by bytes**, because at 60 concurrent runs `v8.serialize` read 11.3 MiB of retained state while `heapUsed` after a forced `gc()` read **x0.97**: noise larger than the signal, which is P15's own two-observer difficulty. By identity: **5 of 5 results are different objects from the driver's**, and the kept state is 87.6 KiB of which **99.4% is the results**. **Against the block it replaces it is about the SAME, ~2% less** — 87.6 against ~89.7 — and NOT half, which is what W77's first three statements of it said, by adding two `v8.serialize` roots that share their results ([Q253](#live)'s shape on a measurement rather than on a harness). **What is not priced**: a consumer who wants none of this cannot opt out, and the LAZY road — wire the machinery only once `done` is touched — was rejected because the BREAKING road hands `done` to every caller by destructuring, so a getter would fire for essentially everyone. **What would close it**: a scale run at [P15](plans/p15-what-a-run-retains-and-what-a-result-may-be.md)'s own 10 000 concurrent runs, where ADR 0046 released exactly one such copy to take 3174.8 MiB to 1610.9. **RESOLVED by [W81](work-items/w81-what-a-settlement-costs.md): the cost is EXACT and the opt-out EXISTS.** See the Resolved table | **resolved - W81** | W81 |
| Q321 | **A promise a library creates for EVERY call cannot be bounded by a test helper, because most callers never attach to it.** [W77](work-items/w77-what-start-returns.md). `runtime.start` now builds a `done` on every run; `test/scheduler/runtime.test.ts` has 25 `.start(` sites and most (W66's, W76's) never touch the handle. So a `done` made to REJECT — rule 8's `b2`, and stop-and-ask (c)'s refused road — reaches them as an UNHANDLED REJECTION and kills the runner mid-file with **exit 1 and ZERO `FAIL` lines**, which `doc/workflow.md` section 4 says is a crash rather than a detection. The item's own `w77Settlement` helper turns an unsettled or rejecting `done` into a NAMED failure **at the handles its own cases hold**, and can reach no others; its first docstring claimed the general case. **Measured from both ends independently**: the author's rule 8 pass graded it a DECLARED CRASH discriminated by its message (the harness learned to, rather than swallowing it), and W77's adequacy reader reached the same structural fact by mutation. *This is why ADR 0058 rule 3 resolving rather than rejecting is load-bearing and not a preference*: the reject road also killed a CHILD PROCESS at exit 1 on a class-II refusal, where `start` reports AND rethrows so the caller never receives the handle at all. **What would close it**: a `done` nobody has attached to being collectable without a rejection, which needs either a lazy handle or a library-installed no-op catch — both of which hide a real rejection from a consumer who attaches late | open | no owner |
| Q322 | **Nothing binds this package's VERSION to the shape of its API, and the two manifests can DISAGREE about it for zero.** [W77](work-items/w77-what-start-returns.md), which moved `0.2.0` -> `0.3.0` in `package.json` and `deno.json` under [ADR 0058](decisions/0058-runtime-start-returns-a-completion-handle-the-runtime-keeps.md) rule 1 and [Q279](#live)'s procedure. Taken as declared exceptions, and the item's own Scope In 5 and Scope Out contradicted each other as written — **nobody could have satisfied both**. **And the reach is wider than the five typecheck lanes**: `AGENTS.md`'s executed snippets carry `RegionEvent` and `FoldInput` LITERALS, which no typecheck lane reads and `check:docs`'s `X3`/`X5` do — **five sites in two snippets**, on a rung this item's `Gate` line does not name, so **W84 as declared could have shipped a gate entry red with its own gate green**. A third site in that file is PROSE and is reached by nothing at all. *P22 prediction 4's scope column names `AGENTS.md` as uncovered; what nobody wrote down is that the `Gate` line was drawn from the same list.* **For a rule:** this is the ninth-plus instance of an `Owns` block drawn from a title rather than from a reach, and `doc/workflow.md` section 7 rule 2 already says to write it from the plan's index row — which is what produced this one. **Against:** the exceptions were declared and cost nothing; an `Owns` pre-declaration rule has been refused for nine plans. **What would force it:** an item whose `Gate` line omits the rung that would have caught a real defect | open | no owner |
| Q349 | **A per-region structure keyed by the INSTANCE is observable only while TWO instances are LIVE AT ONCE, and an eager RELEASE is what makes that window rare.** [W84](work-items/w84-what-a-regions-identity-is.md), found by rule 8's own set. Two probes survived its author pass — one re-sourcing a join's collection read to *the opener's FIRST instance*, one making `releaseArrivals` drop the opener's whole entry — and both survived for one reason: **every test settled instances SEQUENTIALLY**, so [ADR 0046](decisions/0046-a-run-retains-what-its-tasks-returned-and-a-region-releases-its-collection-when-its-join-becomes-terminal.md) rule 3 had already dropped instance k's entry before instance k+1's branches arrived, and *read the instance being settled* and *read the opener's first instance* were the same expression. Closing it needs an INTERLEAVING — and **the order inside the interleaving is itself an assertion**: closing the FIRST instance first leaves the read probe surviving, because instance 0 is also `values()[0]`. The NOT-first instance has to fire while the first is still held. *A lifetime rule that releases eagerly narrows the window in which the key it is keyed by can be shown to be right.* **For a rule:** it is [Q195](#live)'s *a sensor cannot corroborate itself* with the sensor removed by a correctness feature rather than by a probe. **Against:** one item, and the interleaving is checked in. **What would force it:** a second structure whose lifetime hides its own key | open | no owner |
| Q350 | **`RN-R6`'s cross-policy claim cannot be widened to nested regions, because under nesting its own PREMISE fails — and a reader finds that out from a red rung rather than from the rule.** [W84](work-items/w84-what-a-regions-identity-is.md). `RN-R6` says *two policies folded over THE SAME region events produce the same `opened`, `settled` and `suspended`*. `test/matrix/chain.test.ts` asserts it over `SCENARIOS`, every one of which has ONE region opening at TOP LEVEL — so the walk can be narrowed from region INSTANCES back to openers for **zero**, and rule 8's set said so. **The obvious repair was BUILT and is RED, and the red is right**: running the walk over `NESTED_SCENARIOS` fails on `nested-inner-lost/outer@-`, where `live-token` folds `{"0":"arrived","1":"arrived"}` and `arrival-count` folds `{"0":"arrived"}` — because the two policies disagree about the INNER join and `JP-R10` turns that into a `branch.lost` on the OUTER branch, so the two runs do **not** see the same outer events. That is [ADR 0030](decisions/0030-a-join-is-transitive-and-a-dead-join-ends-the-branch-it-sits-in.md) rule 4's content and not a defect. The claim keeps its scope and the instance keying is bound against the DOCUMENT instead. **For a rule:** `RN-R6`'s premise is stated and the scope in which it HOLDS is not, and the same shape would arise for any rule whose premise a later feature can break. **Against:** the premise IS written down; what is missing is a note, and `spec/join-policy.md` v0.17 now carries one. **What would force it:** a second rule widened into a red rung for a reason its own text already implies | open | no owner |
| Q351 | **An enumeration written by a READER goes stale the moment a later item adds a member, and nothing binds it.** [W84](work-items/w84-what-a-regions-identity-is.md). [W33](work-items/w33-a-map-at-run-time-arity.md)'s conformity reader enumerated the driver's per-region run-time structures — `joinState`, `firedJoins`, `RegionEvent.regionId`, the fold's `regions`. **Four.** [W34](work-items/w34-what-a-branch-receives-and-what-a-join-carries.md) then added `arrivals`, and that field's own doc comment read *"Keyed by the opener's node id, like every other per-region structure here"* while listing the four that **exclude its own subject** — a list asserting its own completeness from inside the member it omits. Two documents and one source comment carried the four. **W84's declaration step re-took the list and found the fifth**, which [P22](plans/p22-what-a-regions-identity-is.md)'s re-take table row 4 exists for and which decision 2's whole cost depends on; `arrivals` is also the member whose mis-keying is INVISIBLE, because `JP-R12`'s collection is a `Record<string, unknown>` whichever instance filled it. *[Q217](#live)'s regress one layer out*: a membership list written in PROSE has no `CROSS_ROW_SUBJECTS` and no both-ways binding, and the only thing that found it was an item three plans later RE-RUNNING the list rather than inheriting it — which is P15 decision 5 reaching a reader's finding. **For a rule:** the rule that would catch it is *a list is a claim about another list* (v0.13, obligation (e)'s third shape), which is written for lists in CODE. **Against:** prose cannot be bound both ways without a parser that reads English, which is the standing `doc/` refusal at twenty. **What would force it:** a prose enumeration going stale in a direction a measurement depends on | open | no owner |

| Q352 | **A class of churn enumerated by reading the DIFF is not the class a probe measures, and it can be wrong in BOTH directions at once.** [W85](work-items/w85-what-a-nested-region-carries-and-how-it-ends.md). [P22](plans/p22-what-a-regions-identity-is.md) fact 5 named the gap [W84](work-items/w84-what-a-regions-identity-is.md) left as *`branch.lost`, `branch.suspended` and `branch.resumed` — the design edits them and the spike drove none of them*, derived by reading which lines the change touches. **Re-measured one site at a time: `branch.lost` was ALREADY BOUND at 7** (the L5 `nested-inner-lost` row, whose regions are `fanout`-nested at path `1`), the other two were at **0**, and **four more sites the enumeration does not name were also at 0** — `firedAdd` at both of `settleJoin`'s exits, `releaseArrivals` at the `unsatisfiable` one, and `JP-R10`'s emission at N = 0. **And a SIXTH event the class excludes by construction**: `branch.exited` is `spec/join-policy.md` section 2's fourth branch-ending event, W84 edited its `regionPath` for the same reason, and *three* is what the sentence says. *The class was right and its membership was wrong in both directions, because a diff shows which lines MOVED and a probe shows which lines are OBSERVED, and those are different questions.* **For a rule:** an item's declaration re-takes the gap it inherits site by site rather than inheriting its enumeration. **Against:** [W84](work-items/w84-what-a-regions-identity-is.md) and W85 both did exactly that and the re-take is what found this, so the practice is already working and a rule would restate it; and v0.17's enumeration clause covers a rule's OWN values, not a plan's prose. **What would force it:** a second plan whose fact names a class an item then measures with a different membership | open | no owner |
| Q353 | **A key is observable only where two things it distinguishes are alive AT ONCE — and `values()[0]` is where the wrong answer and the right one are the SAME STRING.** [W85](work-items/w85-what-a-nested-region-carries-and-how-it-ends.md)'s adequacy reader, finding 1, and it is [Q349](#live)'s shape *in the item written to discharge Q349*. W85 drove `branch.suspended` and `branch.resumed` under a region nested in a `map` and asserted the instance — but parked the task in **instance 0**, so filing the event under the opener's FIRST OPENED instance cost **ZERO** at both sites while forcing `'-'` was caught. `branch.lost` escaped only because its loss happens to be in instance 1. **The `'-'` direction is the easy half and is the one an author reaches for**, because `'-'` is the value the type suggests and the value every pre-nesting flow carries; *the wrong INSTANCE is the mis-keying that matters and it is a well-formed path.* Closed here by parking instance 1 and settling it first, with both reader edits checked in as probes. **For a rule:** an assertion about a per-instance key names an instance that is not the opener's first. **Against:** Q349 already says *interleave, and the ORDER is itself a claim*, and this is that clause applied to an emission rather than to a release — one clause, two sites. **What would force it:** a third item asserting a per-instance key at instance 0 | open | no owner |
| Q354 | **`JP-R12`'s `{}` PROPAGATES: an empty collection is an ARRIVAL and not an absence, and at depth 1 there is nothing above a region for it to propagate into.** [W85](work-items/w85-what-a-nested-region-carries-and-how-it-ends.md). The rule says *a join nothing arrived at carries `{}`*, which reads as the end of the story. Under nesting it is not: a `map` whose every instance opens nothing has every branch ended by [`JP-R10`](../spec/join-policy.md), so its own join is SATISFIED under `live-token` (*no live branches*), routes out carrying `{}`, and **that `{}` is what the ENCLOSING branch carries in** — the outer join's collection reads `{"0": …, "1": {}}` and branch 1 has a KEY. The author of W85 derived `{"0": …}` on paper and the run corrected it. *`JP-R12` enumerates three ways to have NO key — routed away, lost, still suspended — and *carried nothing* is not one of them.* **For a rule:** `JP-R12`'s text distinguishes *absent* from *present with `{}`* the way it already distinguishes *absent* from *present with `null`*. **Against:** it follows from the two rules as written and needs no clause, and the fixture that shows it is checked in. **What would force it:** a consumer reading an empty collection as a missing branch | open | no owner |
| Q355 | **A rule's own ERROR MESSAGE reads like its condition and computes something else, and both the author and a shipped comment reasoned from the message.** [W85](work-items/w85-what-a-nested-region-carries-and-how-it-ends.md)'s conformity reader, finding 8. `FD-V28`'s condition is structural and reads no path at all — *`"n"` where this node is outside a **`map`** region containing `n`* — while its message says *"it produces exactly one output on this node's branch path; write `"n"`"*, which reads as a COUNT over paths. W85 wrote the LIST form for a reader past a `fanout` nested inside a `map`, reasoning *the target is inside a region the reader is outside of*; the rule refused it, because the only `map` region containing the target contains the reader too. **So a region nested inside a `map` does not make a reference across it a list: the outer map multiplies the READERS and not the visible outputs.** The verdict was right and the derivation was not, and *an implementer writing `FD-V28` from that sentence writes a different rule.* **For a rule:** a refusal's message states the condition rather than its consequence. **Against:** a good message names what the author should DO, which is what this one does, and [Q102](#live)'s neighbour — *a helpful error message is a worse discriminator than a terse one* ([W32](work-items/w32-what-a-map-fanout-is.md)) — is the same trade seen from the probe side. **What would force it:** a second rule whose message and condition disagree about what they range over | open | no owner |
| Q356 | **A membership over SUITE names is satisfied by an EMPTY SUITE, so a whole file's cases can be deleted at exit 0 with the census green.** [W85](work-items/w85-what-a-nested-region-carries-and-how-it-ends.md)'s adequacy reader, finding 3. `test/definition/l4-corpus.test.ts`'s census closed [Q158](#live) by binding suite NAMES both ways, and says so in its own header; W85 shipped a file of five suites and thirteen cases, and **eleven of the thirteen were deletable at `npm test` EXIT 0, zero FAIL lines** — taking four of the item's six declared Outputs with them — because a suite that registers no case is still a suite `collected()` holds. The only residue was the assertion count, which nothing asserts. **And the census's own control is defeated by a one-token re-source**: `declared = [...names]` makes both set differences empty by construction while `ok(names.length >= SUITES.length)` stays exactly as true as before, which is v0.21's clause failing v0.22's test. Closed by a case-level membership in the file itself and by a COUNT beside the census's differences. *It is [Q217](#live)'s regress with a NAMED limit: re-source plus a DELETION is caught by the literal count; re-source plus a RENAME is not, and no expression inside one process can check that a list of names was written down before the run.* **For a rule:** a test file that is an item's main output declares its CASES, not only its suites. **Against:** `retention.test.ts` has had `DECLARED_CASES` since W53 for exactly this reason and nobody generalised it; two occurrences, and the second was found by a reader rather than by a rung. **What would force it:** a third file shipping a whole item's output with no case-level membership | open | no owner |
| Q357 | **The same mis-keyed latch is SILENT or LOUD depending on what the join's successor is, and the nested corpus is entirely the silent kind.** [W85](work-items/w85-what-a-nested-region-carries-and-how-it-ends.md)'s conformity reader, finding 2. [Q95](#resolved)'s precondition at instance granularity — a policy settling a join before every branch is terminal — makes `settleJoin` route out of ONE instance twice when the latch is keyed by the opener. **What that costs depends on the successor**: a TASK is minted at the same id twice and `ID-R1` stops the run naming it, while another JOIN simply records a second arrival and nothing is reported at all. Every nested fixture in the tree routes an inner join into an outer join, so the item claimed a `DuplicateTaskId` its own document could not produce — measured at **zero occurrences of the string** — and it took a second document with a task after the inner join to make the cost observable. *W18 reached this at depth 1 only because that flow's join successor happened to be a task.* **For a rule:** an item asserting the COST of a defect names the construct that makes it observable. **Against:** rule 8 already asks for the provocation to FIRE FOR THE REASON IT NAMES, and reading the failure lines is what found this — the clause exists and was not applied to a sentence in an item's own `Verification` block. **What would force it:** a second claim about a cost whose fixture cannot produce it | open | no owner |

## Resolved

| Q160 | What is a region's IDENTITY, given that a `map` makes one opener reachable N times in a run? | **`(opener node id, the opener's OWN branchPath)`, and every per-region structure is keyed by that pair** — [ADR 0062](decisions/0062-a-region-instance-is-its-opener-and-the-openers-own-branch-path.md), [W84](work-items/w84-what-a-regions-identity-is.md). `RunState.regions` is two-level with **no composite string anywhere**, `RegionEvent` gains `regionPath` on all six variants and `regionId` keeps its meaning AND its value. **The structures were FIVE and this row said four** — `arrivals` is W34's and postdates W33's reader ([Q351](#live)). Qualifying the observable id instead was measured at **twelve SILENT failure modes** against the two-level road's **sixty NAMED typecheck errors** |
| Q156 | May a `map`'s branch itself open a region? | **YES, and the refusal is LIFTED WHOLE rather than narrowed to one of the two kinds** — [ADR 0062](decisions/0062-a-region-instance-is-its-opener-and-the-openers-own-branch-path.md), [W84](work-items/w84-what-a-regions-identity-is.md). `FD-V25` is unchanged: a map still reaches a region through its branch TASK's edges, exactly as a fanout does, so the answer is a KEY and not a rule. Measured — a `fanout` inside a `map` at three items opens FOUR region instances, fires three separate joins and completes |
| Q332 | Can a control written under v0.21's clause be ENTAILED by its own claim, and can the clause see that? | **Yes it can, and no it cannot — so the clause loses its prescription and gains a TEST.** [P21](plans/p21-what-a-run-time-schema-check-buys-and-what-it-costs.md)'s retro, `doc/workflow.md` [v0.22](workflow.md). v0.21 named IDENTITY as the answer; **all three of P21's items instantiated the clause and all three produced a control identity passes** — W80's operands are freshly built, so the two sides are always different values AND always equal; W81's were two zeros; W82's were different objects with one source. *Identity answers **are these two sides ONE VALUE**; it does not answer **can these two sides EVER DIFFER**, which is the property a control needs.* The requirement is unchanged and identity survives as one instance of it, correct for the `OBSERVED equals DECLARED` shape P20 measured — the only one where the two questions coincide. **W80 wrote the replacement down and did not generalise it**: *what tree makes this fail while its claim passes? There is none.* |
| Q320 | What does the always-on completion handle cost, and can a consumer decline it? | **+1 `structuredClone` per SETTLED TASK — one more copy of everything `OR-R2` already copies, a ratio of 1.00x by construction — and the opt-out is `new Driver(flow, { ...runtime.registry, … })`** — [W81](work-items/w81-what-a-settlement-costs.md), 2026-09-22, `examples/settlement-cost.ts` and `test/driver/result-schema-cost.test.ts`. **The COUNT is exact at every shape**: 46 to 52, 94 to 106, 414 to 466 through `src/`, and 28 against 22, 58 against 46, 28 against 22 on the two public roads — one per settled task, every time, including at **24.0 MiB of settled results**, which `map-alias` reaches through its DATA with no new worker. **The TIME does not resolve at any shape this corpus can reach**: three back-to-back passes of one row read 1.05x, 1.26x and 1.17x, a spread as large as the effect ([Q179](#live)), which is why the answer is stated as a count. **The opt-out is real and it is [P18](plans/p18-a-flow-and-its-runtime-from-documents-alone.md) decision 14's own sentence executed** — `runtime.registry` IS the object a runtime hands its own drivers, and a driver built that way installs no `onFoldInput`, so `append` never calls `freezeInput`. **Its price is named rather than hidden**: that consumer has no `done` and no fold, and every road back to one either installs an `onFoldInput` (which reinstates the per-settlement clone) or calls `foldInputs()` (which clones the WHOLE sequence on every call) — *so the opt-out is for a consumer who does not ask the driver what happened.* **What was NOT done, and where it goes**: the 10 000-concurrent-run scale this row named as its closing condition is `examples/retention.ts`'s mode and its super-linearity is the TOPOLOGY's ([Q244](#live)), not the handle's — the handle's share is one copy per settlement at any N |


| Q279 | Does anything signal that a grammar change is BREAKING for every flow document? | **The PACKAGE's semver — `0.1.0` to `0.2.0` in both manifests, with the change named in `README.md`** — [W67](work-items/w67-a-flow-from-documents-on-real-workers.md), 2026-09-16, the maintainer's answer to that item's stop-and-ask 2. The document's own `version: 1` is untouched until [Q249](#live) answers the TYPE half. Measured: a `0.1.0`-era task node is refused by `FD-V23` and `FD-V17` at construction, by name, with the key it carries |
| Q71 | Is `SchedulerLike` satisfied by a real `FyflowScheduler` under the DOM lib? | resolved 2026-09-11 — **YES, and the fix was the SEAM's listener parameter, not an import** — [ADR 0048](decisions/0048-the-scheduler-is-a-declared-dependency-reached-by-one-bare-specifier.md) rule 6, [W57](work-items/w57-the-scheduler-is-a-dependency.md). `SchedulerLike.addEventListener`'s listener takes `object`: TypeScript compares a callback parameter's own parameter covariantly, so the seam had to accept whatever `Event` the DOM lib says a listener receives, and `{ detail: unknown }` is not a supertype of it. Measured over seven candidate shapes under `ES2022`, `ES2022+DOM`, the browser config and `deno check` — `object` and `unknown` are the two the real class AND the L2-L5 fakes both satisfy; a union with the named class needed a cast at the boundary. **P16 fact 3's stated mechanism — *an `import type { FyflowScheduler }` closes it by construction* — was FALSE**: the import alone changes nothing about assignability, and it is kept because it makes Q71 an L0 assertion (`FyflowScheduler extends SchedulerLike`) under the browser and Deno typechecks. `AGENTS.md` section 12's cast paragraph is deleted; `tsconfig.docs.json` carries `DOM`, so every snippet is typechecked under the consumer's default; `check-docs` `X5`'s one-snippet allowance is zero. *The against-case Q71 recorded — widening lets anything with `addEventListener` through — does not apply: the two-method shape is the seam, and an `EventTarget` without `addTask` still fails it* |
| Q8 | Does a decision see only its node`s output, or accumulated run state? | **Its node`s effective outcome, and whatever its OWN EDGE declares** — [ADR 0041](decisions/0041-a-decision-sees-its-nodes-outcome-and-whatever-its-own-edge-declares.md), [W42](work-items/w42-what-a-decision-sees.md). `FD-C3`, and an **edge** may carry a `needs`: same grammar as a node`s, **minus the `"."` form**, because *what a predicate would have received anyway* is `ctx.outcome` and it already has it. **This row`s fork is the wrong shape** — the answer is *node-output-only by default, and as much accumulated state as the edge names* — so its own argument for the first option survives intact: a predicate that declares nothing is byte-identical to a P1 predicate. **On the EDGE and not the node, for two measured reasons**: a node`s `needs` rewrites what its WORKER receives, and *what the node received* is underivable at routing time, because the ENTRY node`s payload is in no record and re-deriving any other means re-implementing the whole payload contract at a second site. **[P12](plans/p12-what-a-node-receives-and-what-a-decision-sees.md) prediction 3 said this would need no ADR and it REVERSED**, and the ADR`s first stated forcing fact was itself wrong — [Q193](#live) |
| Q11 | What does a node receive as its payload? | **All three candidates at once, in a stack rather than a fork** — [ADR 0040](decisions/0040-what-a-node-can-reach-is-the-runs-own-outputs-and-a-node-id-names-them-relative-to-a-branch-path.md), [W41](work-items/w41-what-a-node-can-reach-and-what-names-it.md). The previous node's output is the **default** and is unchanged; a run-scoped accumulator is the **state**, and it is a PROJECTION of the log that adds no retention; a declared per-edge mapping is the **access**, and it is the only one that can reach a worker at all, because `createTask` takes ONE value. `spec/flow-definition.md` v0.11 section 3 is `needs`, `FD-C1` and `FD-C2` are its rules, and **what NAMES an output is the pair `(node id, branchPath)`** — a node id names one outside a map region and N inside one, which is [ADR 0036](decisions/0036-a-map-is-a-second-node-kind-and-a-branch-path-inside-one-is-a-property-of-the-dispatch.md)'s deciding fact for the third time |
| Q22 | Must a node result be structured-cloneable, and is that a constraint on workers or on the driver? | **On the DRIVER, unchanged — and the fork is the wrong shape** — `OR-R11`, `spec/outcome-record.md` **v0.9**, [W54](work-items/w54-what-a-result-may-be-and-what-bounding-it-would-cost.md). `OR-R2` and `OR-R6` stand; the alternative (*a worker must not retain what it returns*) is [Q9](#live)'s rejected shape. **The question an author actually hits is not *may I return this* — it is WHAT ARRIVES: a result is a VALUE, not an OBJECT.** Fields cross the clone, behaviour does not, silently and by design. **The boundary has THREE answers where this document named two** — refused, cloned faithfully, and cloned **LOSSILY** — and `spec/outcome-record.md`'s own sentence that `structuredClone` *throws on a class instance with behaviour* is **measured FALSE** and corrected in place. Asserted in **all five runtimes**, which is P15 prediction 3's stated way of being wrong checked rather than assumed. **NO ADR** — 0047 skipped and not reused — on `doc/decisions/README.md`'s own *a spec rule with a rationale paragraph is better than a spec rule plus an ADR restating it*. **Prediction 3's conclusion is CONFIRMED and its stated reason REVERSES**: a driver CAN tell, at 0.04-0.52% of a clone and right on 14 of 14 shapes — it is blind to the NESTED case instead, and the walk that sees it costs 27-55% ([Q238](#live)) |
| Q19 | Is per-run retained state bounded? | **Bounded by the RUN and by nothing else, and the one second copy is released** — [ADR 0046](decisions/0046-a-run-retains-what-its-tasks-returned-and-a-region-releases-its-collection-when-its-join-becomes-terminal.md), [W53](work-items/w53-what-a-run-retains-that-nothing-reads.md). `spec/run-state.md` v0.6 section 7 and **`RN-R13`**: a run retains *(tasks settled) x (bytes each returned)*, plus one further copy of each result that has arrived at a join that has **not yet become TERMINAL** — *not* one per LIVE region, which is a different predicate and the wrong one. **This row's own premise was wrong in both halves it stated.** The footprint is not proportional to the node count — that is one factor and *the whole of every result* is the other, so a 4-node chain returning 256 KiB retains **1025 KiB** where a 128-node chain returning one key retains **33 KiB** — and `maxCompletedTasks` is not an answer to inherit, because it caps `scheduler.tasks` and reaches nothing this layer holds. **What the decision takes:** a region's collection lives from its first arrival until its join becomes terminal and is released there, which is **49.8% -> 0.0%** of a completed region-bearing run and **3174.8 -> 1610.9 MiB** at 10 000 concurrent runs, for **0** failures at L1-L5 and **0** at L6. **What it refuses:** any eviction from `inputs`, because a fold over an incomplete log reports `completed` with zero nodes and no signal. **What it does not decide** is bounding what a CONSUMER reads — 89% of corpus task nodes are named by no `needs`, and it is measured NOT free — which is [Q22](#live) and [W54](work-items/w54-what-a-result-may-be-and-what-bounding-it-would-cost.md)'s |
| Q164 | Where does a rule about what a run CARRIES live? | **`FD-C` is the third prefix in `spec/flow-definition.md`, beside `FD-V` for what a document may say and `FD-R` for what a run refuses** — [W41](work-items/w41-what-a-node-can-reach-and-what-names-it.md). Its stated forcing condition was *a second rule about what a run carries that has no region to hang on*, and `FD-C1` is exactly that. **Measured live before the prefix was taken**: a rule declared as `FD-C1` and cited one line below it made `check-spec` print *8 checks ok, 80 rules*, exit 0. The vocabulary was written down **twice** in `scripts/check-spec.mjs` and both patterns are derived from one constant now — **and that constant is bound to `spec/`'s own declaration rows both ways**, after rule 8 measured that dropping `FD-C` from it cost zero |
| Q187 | When is a declared reference refused — is reachability enough? | **No, and the two candidates were one mechanism** — [ADR 0042](decisions/0042-a-reference-says-whether-it-is-conditional-and-the-bare-form-means-the-target-certainly-ran.md), [W45](work-items/w45-what-a-reference-must-be-able-to-say.md). `FD-V29` refuses an UNCONDITIONAL reference a run can reach the declaring node without, by CERTAINTY rather than by dominance — a region's branches are conjunctive (`JP-R5`) and a task's edges are not — and a `?` modifier is what says *if it ran*, with `FD-C4` leaving the key absent. **Measured: of the 127 references the corpora admit, dominance refuses 30 and `FD-V29` refuses 8**, so 22 are references a dominance rule would have cost. **`FD-R3` is not replaced**, and its two remaining cases are written into its own row |
| Q206 | Is there any form a reference can take that reaches a SIBLING region's outputs? | **An output is visible when the two branch paths AGREE ON THE REGIONS THEIR TWO CHAINS SHARE, and *one path is a prefix of the other* is that rule's special case** — [ADR 0044](decisions/0044-a-reference-is-visible-when-two-paths-agree-on-the-regions-that-contain-both-of-them.md), [W49](work-items/w49-what-a-reference-across-sibling-regions-means.md) under [P14](plans/p14-what-a-reference-means-when-two-regions-arities-are-independent.md); `spec/flow-definition.md` v0.15, `FD-C1` amended, no rule id added or retired. **The row's framing was corrected rather than merely discharged**: it is neither a `map` question nor an arity question — every sibling pair in both corpora is `map`/`map`, and constructed, a `fanout`-to-`fanout` sibling reference validates with zero findings and STOPS THE RUN. Of the three candidates the row named, the predicate change costs **6** L1-L5 failures and removes no corpus flow where a fourth refusal costs **11** and removes one, the 6 a strict subset of the 11. `FD-R3` NARROWS to the join-policy road ([Q218](#live)). *Its live row said `resolved` and it had no row here, alone among 47 such rows, until [P18](plans/p18-a-flow-and-its-runtime-from-documents-alone.md)'s retro's census found the gap; this row is written from that live row and adds no claim to it.* |
| Q166 | What does a `map` instance lose by receiving its own item? | **Nothing it cannot ask for** — [ADR 0040](decisions/0040-what-a-node-can-reach-is-the-runs-own-outputs-and-a-node-id-names-them-relative-to-a-branch-path.md), [W41](work-items/w41-what-a-node-can-reach-and-what-names-it.md). `needs: {"item": ".", "run": "ingest"}` on the map's branch entry, and the instance receives its own item AND the value the map node received. [ADR 0037](decisions/0037-what-a-branch-receives-is-a-property-of-the-fan-out-and-what-a-join-carries-is-a-property-of-the-region.md) rule 3 is **untouched** — *map* still means `f(x_i)` — and what changed is that the context is now declarable instead of packable. `spec/examples/valid/map-with-context.flow.json`. **The cost it makes reachable is [Q186](#live)** |
| Q158 | What notices when a whole `*.test.ts` file leaves the runner's glob? | **A suite census in a test file, not a check in `scripts/**`** — [W41](work-items/w41-what-a-node-can-reach-and-what-names-it.md). `test/definition/l4-corpus.test.ts` compares the declared suite names to `collected()`, **set equality both ways**, in all five runtimes. Three plans passed without a fix because the row named `test/runner.ts` and `scripts/**` as where one would live and both are in nobody's `Owns`; the runner and the browser bundle glob the same tree, so a suite that did not load is one `collected()` does not hold. **Provoked**: W41's two files renamed out of the glob went from `21 file(s), 579 assertions, all passed, exit 0` to one named failure. The residue is [Q188](#live) |
| Q133 | What should `npm run gate` prove, and when? | **Everything it proved before, in ladder ORDER, with the seven `:selftest` entries run CONCURRENTLY** - [ADR 0039](decisions/0039-the-gate-climbs-the-ladder-and-the-self-tests-run-concurrently.md), [W39](work-items/w39-what-the-gate-proves-and-what-it-costs.md). **516.1 s to 393.1 s, -23.8% against a +-13% band, with no clause deleted and no bound lowered.** Two of the five candidates were refused on measurements rather than on taste: *move them off the gate* has no *something else* to move them to, because there is no CI, no workflow and no git hook; and *stop paying for a process per probe* is 137 s of real interpreter boot that `NODE_COMPILE_CACHE` does not touch and that costs seven self-tests rewritten to remove. **The two properties nobody had ever chosen were ORDER and CONCURRENCY**, and the order was the ladder's inverse - 488.9 s of self-test ahead of `lint`, in a chain whose own `S12` asserts order because `&&` is the only implementation the ladder has |
| Q148 | Can the self-tests run concurrently? | **Five of the seven can; two cannot, and the second one was found by running them** - [ADR 0039](decisions/0039-the-gate-climbs-the-ladder-and-the-self-tests-run-concurrently.md), [W39](work-items/w39-what-the-gate-proves-and-what-it-costs.md). `check-scheduler-selftest.mjs` deletes `node_modules/fyflow-scheduler`; **`check-wiring-selftest.mjs` stages every `scripts/check-*.mjs` flat into `dev-dist/` and runs those copies with their dispatch falsified**, so one of them reaches the real `check-docs` body and `rmSync`s `dev-dist/docs` and another launches the whole `check-spec` self-test. Each declares itself with a `SELFTEST-EXCLUSIVE:` marker in its own file; `scripts/run-selftests.mjs` reads it and `G4` derives the same set independently and compares both ways. **This row's last sentence - *the saving is not in parallelism* - was false, and the measurement is 488.9 s to 365.0.** The residue is [Q181](#live), [Q182](#live) and [Q183](#live) |
| Q149 | What notices when a check's `--self-test` dispatch stops routing? | **Three clauses in a new check, `scripts/check-wiring.mjs`** - [W38](work-items/w38-the-dispatch-nothing-binds.md). **`G1`** runs each `:selftest` script with the flag and `CHECK_<NAME>_SELFTEST` pointing at a stub that returns a sentinel, and requires that exit code - so it binds BEHAVIOUR, not the presence of the string `--self-test`, which is in a comment in every one of them. **`G2`** reads the DEFAULT the dispatch loads when nothing sets that override, **which is every run the gate makes and the one configuration `G1` structurally cannot observe**, by capturing both names out of `const mod = process.env.<NAME> ?? join(ROOT, 'scripts', '<file>');` and comparing them - a presence test there was broken in one edit by a reader, leaving a stale value beside the live one, which is W37's `D7` a third time. **`G3`** derives the manifest's two entries per check from the `scripts/` listing, both ways, because both new entries were otherwise repointable and deletable for zero. **The one clause in `S11` this question proposed was built exactly as proposed and cost the gate 236 s** - [Q176](#live). Seven checks, including the new one, which covers itself; 16 probes, 8 META gradings and 6 SELF entries |
| Q1 | Where does routing execute? | **The driver, outside the scheduler** - [ADR 0001](decisions/0001-routing-executes-in-the-driver-outside-the-scheduler.md). The driver is the only thing that touches the scheduler; a worker computes and does not route; routing is a pure function of `(definition, run state, outcome)`. Decided by two library facts rather than by preference: spawn is lineage and cannot join, and a worker that routes owns part of the definition. **The cost is accepted, not dodged** - a serialization point on a hot path, and the driver holding the only copy of run truth until Q15 |
| Q2 | Is a flow definition data, code, or data with registered predicates? | **Data, with predicates registered by name** - [ADR 0002](decisions/0002-a-flow-definition-is-data-and-a-predicate-is-registered-by-name.md). The graph is JSON-serializable with no functions in it; an edge names a predicate; an unknown name fails **at instantiation**, mirroring `addTask` throwing on an unknown `workerType`. Keeps Q15 and Q21 open, which is most of what it was protecting. Rule 5 carries the honest half-truth: the *definition* is portable, the *deployment* is not |
| Q3 | Which `fyflow-scheduler` version do we pin? | **0.5.2** since 2026-09-08 ([W37](work-items/w37-the-scheduler-pin-moves-to-0-5-2.md)), which is the fix for [S9](scheduler-upstream.md) - the one item this repository has ever raised upstream; **0.5.1** from 2026-09-07 ([W36](work-items/w36-the-scheduler-pin-moves-to-0-5-1.md)); **0.3.0**, and since 2026-08-31 **from the registries** - npm `fyflow-scheduler@0.3.0` and JSR `@fyflow/scheduler@0.3.0` ([ADR 0029](decisions/0029-the-scheduler-is-a-registry-dependency-and-nothing-reaches-the-working-tree.md)). It is pinned **twice**, once per registry, and `check-scheduler.mjs` D3 is what stops the two drifting. *(It landed 2026-08-30 on no registry, which is what had forced ADR 0013's working-tree link.)* Was 0.2.1; bumped the day the resource-events work landed, which is the coordinated-rather-than-discovered bump the original answer predicted. `fyflow-react-components` pins 0.2.1 and will need the same move. No ADR - this is a note, not an architecture. **There is no changelog**, so a version bump here means re-reading `AGENTS.md` §6 rather than a diff **AMENDED, not reopened, by [W36](work-items/w36-the-scheduler-pin-moves-to-0-5-1.md): the DECISION is unchanged and only the number moved.** Pin one exact version, install it from a registry, and pin the Deno half to the same version on JSR with `check:scheduler` `D3` comparing the two — all of that is [ADR 0029](decisions/0029-the-scheduler-is-a-registry-dependency-and-nothing-reaches-the-working-tree.md) and none of it changed. **What was measured before the bump**: both registries carry 0.5.1 and it is `latest` on both; the reference grows 858 to 887 lines and every added line is about `worker.setup.*` / `worker.teardown.*`, a family this library subscribes to not at all; the only public type change is two internal worker-message variants; **every cited source line is byte-identical** — `857`, `889`, `907`, `909`, `1139`, `1141`, `894`, `1130`, `212`, `1468-1473` and `workerInterface.ts:50` all say at 0.5.1 exactly what `spec/` cites them for at 0.3.0; the `user_action` event pair holds, measured by running it; and **L6 passes 489 assertions / 25 real runs in BOTH runtimes**, unmoved. **What did NOT move: [S9](scheduler-upstream.md)**, which is unfixed at 0.5.1 in both halves and on the same lines |
| Q4 | What satisfies a join - expected arrivals, or live tokens? | **Live tokens** - [ADR 0003](decisions/0003-a-join-is-satisfied-when-its-region-has-no-live-branches.md), decided by W4's matrix and not by the argument this row records. **The result is bigger than the question:** P1 predicted arrival-counting breaks on the routed-away case; the matrix says it breaks on **six of nine rows**, and only one of those is that case. An ordinary `optional` task failing is enough. Fixed-`N` is wrong whenever *"how many will arrive"* differs from *"how many opened"*, and every non-success outcome makes it differ - so the sharp edge is the common case, not the exotic one. **The cost is recorded rather than hidden:** on the all-exit row `live-token` fires a join no branch reached, which is [Q24](#live) |
| Q10 | What happens when no decision edge matches? | **A mandatory default edge, refused at instantiation; and a named run failure if routing ever reaches a node with no matching edge** - `spec/flow-definition.md` section 3, `FD-V7` and `FD-R1`. Two rules at two times: `FD-V7` is the mechanism, `FD-R1` is the assertion that it held. **The third candidate - "the node is terminal for that branch" - was rejected on P1's validity rather than on taste**: a flow that silently stops halfway inside a fan-out produces a join that never fires with nothing queued and nothing running, which is character-for-character the signature [P1](plans/p1-what-satisfies-a-join.md) exists to attribute to a join policy. No ADR: a spec rule with its rationale stated in place, per `doc/decisions/README.md` |
| Q13 | What is the scheduler task id scheme, and what stops a collision corrupting a run? | **`<runId>:<nodeId>:<branchPath>:<attempt>`, four segments always, with the driver refusing a duplicate before `addTask` sees it** - `spec/task-id.md`, `ID-R1`. Reading 0.3.0's source made the case stronger than `AGENTS.md`'s one line: `addTask` sets the map entry *and* pushes to the ready queue (`core/FyflowScheduler.ts:444-445`), so **both tasks run**, and the success path settles each object on its own outcome (`:723-727`) - so a collision writes **two outcome records under one key**, one of them carrying the other execution's `executionTime`, plus a `task.running` for a task that never started. **W1's first version claimed the opposite** (one record, second execution vanished); the spec-conformity pass caught it. `branchPath` being a property of the node rests on `FD-V19`, which refuses convergent branches ([Q27](#live)) |
| Q14 | Is run state a fold of an event log, and whose log? | **Yes, and of OUR log** - `spec/run-state.md` section 1. Not the scheduler's task events: they cannot see flow structure, because the library has no dependency graph, so *"branch taken"* and *"join satisfied"* do not exist at that level. Not ours wrapping theirs: an event `detail` is a live object ([ADR 0007](decisions/0007-the-driver-normalizes-events-into-an-immutable-outcome-record.md)), so a log retaining them would be a bag of aliases, and once the normalization at the listener boundary exists there is nothing left to wrap. **What makes this an answer rather than a restated leaning** is that the fold is now the only thing producing run state, and two rungs check it - `RN-R1`'s purity and ladder L3. The last of `doc/concept.md` section 6's four leanings to land |
| Q17 | How does `user_action` surface at flow level? | **Four answers from four places, and the last one is W4's.** *Detection*: key on terminal state, not event name ([ADR 0007](decisions/0007-the-driver-normalizes-events-into-an-immutable-outcome-record.md) rule 2). *Mechanism*: the branch suspends and an external resolution record resumes it (ADR 0004 rules 3-6). *Run-level outcome*: `awaiting_action`, and it outranks `running` (`spec/run-state.md` section 3, `RN-R7`). *What a join does*: **it keeps waiting, under both policies** - W4's `suspended` row is one of only three where the two agree, and both are right. A run waiting on a person is never reported as stuck, which is the whole reason `branch.suspended` exists |
| Q24 | Are the two one-line refinements of the join policies a third and fourth policy, rather than repairs of the first two? | **Two answers, not one** — [ADR 0025](decisions/0025-the-zero-arrival-join-is-reportable-and-arrival-counting-is-not-repaired.md), from [W16](work-items/w16-the-decision-and-what-it-ships.md). `JP-R8`'s is **adopted** as `live-token-requiring-arrival` under its own id, so a join reached by nothing folds to `stuck` rather than `completed`. `JP-R7`'s is **refused**: over the nine matrix rows it is `live-token` weakened and never strengthened, and on the third row it repairs it fires a join zero branches reached ([Q91](#live)). `live-token` and `arrival-count` are byte-identical after it, and **not one of the 36 matrix cells changed**. The refused variant stays a matrix column, because a refusal nothing executes is a refusal nobody can falsify |
| Q25 | Does the flow-level outcome vocabulary belong in the definition or in run state? | **Run state's, and closed** - `spec/run-state.md` section 3. Five outcomes: `running`, `completed`, `failed`, `awaiting_action`, `stopped`. `end.outcome` stays an author-defined string the driver never interprets: **an author labels *why* a path ended; run state says *what the run is*.** Letting a definition supply run-level outcomes would let a flow invent a state no UI could render and no fold could reason about |
| Q31 | Can the driver retain a scheduler event's `detail`? | **No, and one event lies about its own name** - [ADR 0007](decisions/0007-the-driver-normalizes-events-into-an-immutable-outcome-record.md). Verified in the source: `detail` **is** the live `FyflowTask`. *(This row was written at 0.2.1 and cited `core/FyflowScheduler.ts:727, 775, 777, 974`; at **0.3.0** those are `:857, 907, 909, 1427` plus a second failure path at `:1139, 1141`, and **`scheduler.completed` now emits a copy** - `{...this.stats, timestamp}` at `:401` - so the half of this row about `stats` being mutated in place is no longer true. ADR 0007's version table carries both readings; trust it over this cell.)* Separately, a non-optional task out of retries sets `state = 'user_action'` and then dispatches **both** `task.failed` and `task.user_action` for the same object, so a driver reading event names double-counts and routes a human-intervention case down an error edge. The driver copies declared fields at the listener boundary and keys the outcome record on terminal state. Upstream declined to fix the first half in code ([S8](scheduler-upstream.md)), which is what made it ours |
| Q32 | How does P1 tell a stuck join apart from a silently dropped task? | **The question dissolved - 0.3.0 fixed the drop** ([S7](scheduler-upstream.md)). `_failOrphanedTask` now fails an orphaned waiter terminally with `task.failed`, `task.user_action`, a rejected promise and a `stats.failed` increment, and sweeps it out of every blocked queue with an `orphaned` exit each. A dropped task no longer produces silence, so a P1 hang can no longer be a lost task masquerading as a stuck join. W5's every-node-settles assertion stays as **cheap insurance** rather than a validity gate - it now checks a property the library asserts rather than one it violated |
| Q33 | Must ladder L6 run in all three runtimes, and does P1's W5 do it? | **L6 runs in Node 24 and Deno; the browser is [Q52](#live)** - `spec/runtime-targets.md` `RT-R10`, and no ADR: a gate-scope rule with its rationale stated in place. **The leaning was *Node only for P1* and it lost its own argument.** That argument was *"a real worker file per runtime - `.ts` for Deno, `.js` for Node"*, and a typed `.ts` worker runs under Node 24 in both `inline` and threaded pools ([S9](scheduler-upstream.md)). So one worker file serves both and the second runtime costs a two-line import map, measured at ~90 ms per run. **The browser is the genuinely expensive one** - Playwright, the worker emitted as a separate bundle asset, a page to serve it from - and its exclusion is **structural rather than a note**: L6 lives in `examples/**` and `scripts/build-browser-tests.mjs` globs `test/**/*.test.ts`, so nothing that imports a scheduler can reach the browser bundle by accident |
| Q35 | Is the *published* Node floor 24, or is 24 only the development pin? | **The published floor is `>=22`; the pin stays 24** — W3. `package.json` carries `engines.node: ">=22"`, matching `fyflow-scheduler`; `.node-version` pins 24 for the machine that runs the gate. Nothing this layer needs is newer than 22 (`structuredClone` is global from 17), and publishing `>=24` would lock out a consumer for no reason the repo can name. **Node 24 is a TOOLCHAIN requirement** — it runs TypeScript without a build step, which is a fact about the gate rather than about the package |
| Q41 | Who composes the fold's input sequence, and where does its order come from? | **The driver, and it is the log with the routing facts interleaved** - [ADR 0014](decisions/0014-the-driver-produces-the-folds-ordered-input-sequence.md). `Driver.foldInputs()` returns it, `onFoldInput` pushes it, `snapshot()` is now *defined* as its record-kind subsequence, and append order is fixed at each site rather than emerging from callback registration. **Both objections in the original row survive and are answered rather than dodged:** the driver holds no second copy of run truth (it holds the log plus facts no record carries, and computes no `RunState`), and the fold stays pure - `RN-R1` is untouched and the composition belongs to the caller. **The row was filed as an ergonomics question and was a capability gap:** two of the six kinds were not observable from outside at all, so no caller could have built the sequence however carefully it tried, and `pathEnded` had no producer anywhere |
| Q42 | Where does the stuck-join predicate live? | **In the fold, as a run-state outcome** - [ADR 0015](decisions/0015-a-run-whose-join-can-no-longer-fire-is-stuck.md) rule 5. Not on `Driver`, which would grow the copy of run truth W3's scope caps; not re-derived per consumer; not left in a test, which is where W4 had to leave it. `RN-R12`'s `isTerminal(state)` is the one-line form a caller wants. **The register's objection to the fold was *"it does not know what dispatched means without Q41 wired"*, and Q41 is now wired** - which is why these two were one change. What is NOT answered is [Q37](#live): who watches a *live* run, and how often |
| Q43 | Run state has no outcome for a stuck run | **`stuck` is the sixth outcome** - [ADR 0015](decisions/0015-a-run-whose-join-can-no-longer-fire-is-stuck.md), `spec/run-state.md` v0.3 `RN-R10`. **And this row's own reasoning was wrong, which is most of what it taught.** It said *"`regions` already records `{0: arrived, 1: exited}`, so the information is present"*. It is not: on `p1-routed-away` **both policies produce that identical value** - every branch settles under each - which is exactly what `RN-R6` guarantees. The difference is whether the **join fired**, and nothing carried it; the other candidate, inferring it from `publish` being absent from `nodes`, needs the definition, which `RN-R4` forbids. So the fix cost a new fact (`joined`, `RN-R11`) rather than a precedence tweak. **Found by W5 trying to act on the row**, and asserted on two real runs that differ in nothing else |
| Q51 | How do the three runtimes reach `fyflow-scheduler` (0.3.0 when asked; **0.5.2** since [W37](work-items/w37-the-scheduler-pin-moves-to-0-5-2.md))? | **A `devDependency` from a REGISTRY, and never a runtime dependency** - [ADR 0029](decisions/0029-the-scheduler-is-a-registry-dependency-and-nothing-reaches-the-working-tree.md), which supersedes [ADR 0013](decisions/0013-the-scheduler-is-a-development-dependency-reached-from-the-working-tree.md) rules 3 and 5. Node installs `fyflow-scheduler@0.5.2` from npm; Deno resolves the same specifier to `jsr:@fyflow/scheduler@0.5.2`; the browser not at all. **And the SECOND premise expired on 2026-09-07**: point (2) below says Node cannot import the sibling's TypeScript source, which 0.5.2 fixed ([S9](scheduler-upstream.md), the one item this register ever raised).
