Node can; the split stays because `dist/` is what an npm consumer gets. *(The row's header was re-dated to 0.5.2 and this answer was left at 0.5.1 - W37's conformity reader, and the register is read before every work item.)* **The answer below was right for six weeks and its first premise expired on 2026-08-31**, when both registries began carrying 0.3.0 - which is this register's own recurring lesson, one layer out: a row is only as current as the measurement under it. The published npm and JSR packages depend on the scheduler **not at all**, because no published file imports it; `S7` in `scripts/check-source.mjs` is what turns that from a property into a rule. **Three things were measured rather than assumed, and each changed the answer.** (1) `@fyflow/scheduler` **is** on JSR and `fyflow-scheduler` **is** on npm - both at **0.2.1**, so it is only 0.3.0 that is unpublished, and a version range is not available. (2) Node 24 **cannot** import the sibling's TypeScript source - `core/threadWrapper.ts:5` value-imports an `interface`, which type stripping cannot erase - while Deno can, so the two runtimes take different roads to one library ([S9](scheduler-upstream.md)). (3) `AGENTS.md` §3's *"the file must be JavaScript"* is **false at Node 24**, so one `.ts` worker file serves Node and Deno and [Q33](#live)'s leaning lost the premise it rested on |
| Q53 | `Driver`'s public surface says it serves many runs and `ID-R3` says it serves one — should the surface narrow? | **Yes** — [ADR 0022](decisions/0022-a-driver-takes-its-run-at-construction.md), from [W13](work-items/w13-one-driver-one-run-fixed-at-construction.md). A driver takes its run id at **construction**, so `start(payload)`, `hasFailed()` and a single `failed` flag replace the three shapes that only made sense for many runs. It also moves `ID-R2`'s charset refusal to the constructor, beside `MissingJoinPolicy`, which is where a caller can act on it |
| Q72 | Two `Driver`s sharing one scheduler cross-route, and nothing refuses it | **Refused at the listener, in two tiers** — [ADR 0023](decisions/0023-an-inbound-settlement-is-this-runs-only-if-this-driver-dispatched-it.md)'s `ID-R4`, from [W13](work-items/w13-one-driver-one-run-fixed-at-construction.md). Another run's settlement is **dropped in silence**, because the driver that owns it will report it; this run's id for a task never dispatched is `UndispatchedTask`. Before it, each driver received the other's settlements and dispatched its successors — one task id under two outcomes, with nothing reported. **[W19](work-items/w19-a-task-we-did-not-mint-on-a-real-scheduler.md) runs the blessed topology on real workers** |
| Q73 | `onRegionEvent` hands out the driver's own live object while every other outbound path is frozen | **Frozen** — one `Object.freeze` in `emit()`, by [W12](work-items/w12-the-region-event-a-listener-cannot-corrupt.md). Found by W9's adequacy reader: a listener writing `e.branchPath` corrupted both `foldInputs()` and the folded `regions`. The asymmetry was invisible from outside, and `OR-R1`'s whole point is that a consumer should not have to know which handles are live. A conformity fix with nothing to decide, so no ADR |
| Q84 | Should `AGENTS.md` section 9 name every refusal class it lists? | **Yes, and the list is now checked against the PACKAGE rather than against itself** — [W13](work-items/w13-one-driver-one-run-fixed-at-construction.md) added `UndispatchedTask`, and [W18](work-items/w18-the-scheduler-edge-and-what-an-id-we-did-not-mint-means.md) found the deeper defect ([Q101](#live)): the snippet was a closed list compared against a **copy of itself**, so it omitted `InvalidTaskId` — exported from the package root the whole time — with the gate green. A class the reference does not mention is the omission P3's conformity reader called *"a claim by omission it does not defend"* |
| Q86 | Should a task id that does not PARSE be treated as "not ours" rather than as a defect? | **Neither: report it and keep routing** — [ADR 0027](decisions/0027-an-inbound-task-id-that-does-not-parse-was-minted-by-no-driver.md)'s `ID-R5`, from [W18](work-items/w18-the-scheduler-edge-and-what-an-id-we-did-not-mint-means.md), decided at a new **tier 0** ahead of `ID-R4`'s two, because both of those compare a run id and an unparseable id has none. **What decided it was asking who PRODUCES one**: a foreign task and a worker's own `spawnTask` child can never be ours and are the common case on the blessed topology; a `detail.id` that stopped being what we dispatched could be ours and is rare. Stopping is right for one and catastrophic for the other, so *stop* is wrong in one direction, *ignore* in the other, and only *report* is wrong in neither. Supersedes ADR 0023 rule 2 |
| Q91 | `JP-R7`'s variant fires a join with ZERO arrivals — the exact cost `JP-R8` exists to remove | **Recorded as the reason `JP-R7`'s refinement was refused** — [ADR 0025](decisions/0025-the-zero-arrival-join-is-reportable-and-arrival-counting-is-not-repaired.md). Measured by [W15](work-items/w15-the-fourth-column-and-the-chain-nothing-runs.md)'s fourth column: on `all-exit`, decrementing `N` reaches `need: 0` with nothing arrived, so it fires a join no branch reached. **The two refinements point in opposite directions**, which is why one shipped and one did not |
| Q93 | Two of the six region events reach no policy, so ladder L5 cannot see the driver dropping them | **Covered at L2, structurally uncoverable at L5, and that is right** — [W15](work-items/w15-the-fourth-column-and-the-chain-nothing-runs.md). `branch.suspended` and `branch.resumed` decrement nothing, so no policy can distinguish a run in which they were dropped; the assertion that they reach `policy.step` belongs where the driver's calls are observed rather than where their effect is |
| Q94 | [ADR 0003](decisions/0003-a-join-is-satisfied-when-its-region-has-no-live-branches.md) carries a hand-copied two-column matrix and the renderer now emits four | **Each ADR carries the table it decided from** — [W16](work-items/w16-the-decision-and-what-it-ships.md). ADR 0003's two columns are the evidence *it* was taken on and are left as history; [ADR 0025](decisions/0025-the-zero-arrival-join-is-reportable-and-arrival-counting-is-not-repaired.md) carries the four-column table, verified against `runCell` once, mechanically, all 36 cells |
| Q6 | Is a join transitive over nested fan-out? If a branch itself fans out, does the outer join wait for the grandchildren? | **Yes, for every policy, and it needed no mechanism** — [ADR 0030](decisions/0030-a-join-is-transitive-and-a-dead-join-ends-the-branch-it-sits-in.md), from [W22](work-items/w22-what-an-inner-join-does-to-the-outer-one.md) under [P7](plans/p7-is-a-join-transitive-over-nested-fan-out.md). Measured over four depth-2 rows × the four matrix columns: **each region answers exactly as the depth-1 row of the same shape does**, statuses and `describe`s alike, with only the branch paths renamed. An outer region's `branches` is unchanged by what nests inside it, and an inner join firing enters the outer join at the path its own `fanout` sits at — which **is** the outer branch's arrival, built by [W21](work-items/w21-the-branch-path-is-a-path.md) for [Q114](#resolved) and not for this. **The row's own leaning was half right and half wrong**: *"under Q4's token reading this is nearly free"* — yes; *"under arrival-counting it needs a separate mechanism"* — **no**, and [P7](plans/p7-is-a-join-transitive-over-nested-fan-out.md) built a whole refusal on that half before measuring it. `N` is per-region and an inner branch never needed counting by the outer join. **Transitivity is a property of a REGION, not of a row**: on the all-exit row the inner join is `unsatisfiable` and the outer one is `satisfied`, because `JP-R10` ends the branch the dead region sat in. **What was NOT free** is that exit: at depth 1 an `unsatisfiable` join emitted nothing, which was unobservable because a top-level fanout is in no region, and at depth 2 it left a branch with **no terminal event** in a quiescent run — a state `JP-R5`'s own enumeration does not admit |
| Q107 | `ID-R5` at L6 is reached from `task.completed` and nothing else | **Closed by [W23](work-items/w23-a-nested-flow-on-real-workers.md), and it cost one boolean.** `ForeignTask` gains `optional`, and a sixth injected id runs `ReviewWorker` — whose pool serves the `fail` role — so it **throws**, and `optional: true` is what makes the scheduler settle it `failed` rather than end it `user_action` (`core/FyflowScheduler.ts:894-897`). Same refusal as the first entry (one segment where four are required), reaching tier 0 under a different event, so the `event` field stops being a constant with five copies. **Its side effect is the finding**: the sixth task runs a DIFFERENT POOL, and one assertion had been comparing `settlementIds` (settlement order) against `row.foreign` (declaration order) — passing only because all five previous foreign tasks shared one pool and the two orders coincided. That is a dependency on an order `spec/` does not fix, which `doc/workflow.md` section 5 rule 5 forbids; **the claim was always about the set** and is now written as one, sorted on both sides with one comparator. Found by adding a row, not by reading |
| Q117 | The L6 harness mints a silently wrong task id on a nested flow | **Closed by [W23](work-items/w23-a-nested-flow-on-real-workers.md), and this row was wrong about the failure twice.** `examples/run-flow.ts`'s inbound `resolve()` step read `buildTaskId(runId, at, Number(branchPath), 0)`. [W21](work-items/w21-the-branch-path-is-a-path.md)'s declaration said that yields `NaN`; **it does not** — `Number('1.0')` is **1**, so at depth 2 it minted `r*:node:1:0`, a **well-formed id for a different task**, and `resolve()` refused it as not-suspended (`RS-R1`) — a real failure reported under a false cause. At depth 3 `Number('1.1.0')` **is** `NaN` and `buildTaskId` throws since W21's `INDEX` test, from a call site **outside** the surrounding `try`, inside a `queueMicrotask` — an unhandled rejection rather than a harness failure. Fixed with `branchIndices(branchPath)`, which is `buildTaskId`'s exact inverse and needs no new export, and the call moved **inside** the `try`. **The register described the same defect incorrectly in two successive items**, which is `doc/`-is-checked-by-nothing in the file that is read before every one of them |
| Q95 | The `at most once` latch on the driver's `unsatisfiable` path is unreachable, and deleting it fails nothing | **Closed with a stub** — [W18](work-items/w18-the-scheduler-edge-and-what-an-id-we-did-not-mint-means.md), and **the register was wrong about which latch**. `settleJoin` has **two** `firedJoins.add` calls: the *satisfied* one is already covered, and its absence produces the `DuplicateTaskId` the row recorded; the *unsatisfiable* one is [W15](work-items/w15-the-fourth-column-and-the-chain-nothing-runs.md)'s actual survivor and deleting it cost **0 of 386**. Its precondition is any policy that settles a join **before every branch is terminal**, and what its absence produces is a second `joinSettled(fired: false)` — or, worse, a join routed out of after being declared dead, taking `RunState.outcome` from `stuck` to `completed` in silence |
| Q113 | `Driver.regionOf`'s call sites - and whether a JOIN POLICY needs innermost resolution at all | **Yes it does, and two events are the whole of why.** Measured 2026-09-02 at W22's declaration step, which is where [W20](work-items/w20-what-a-nested-region-is.md) asked for it, and measured rather than argued: `emit` steps the state machine keyed by `e.regionId` (`src/driver/driver.ts:885`), so the question is *which events does each policy act on* crossed with *how is each event's region found*. **(1) Blindness** - `step(s, e) !== s` for all six event types, from `open` and from a two-branches-open state: `arrival-count` acts on **`branch.arrived` and nothing else**; `live-token` and `live-token-requiring-arrival` act on `opened`, `arrived`, `exited` and `lost`; **all three ignore `suspended` and `resumed`**. Cross that with the driver: `opened` is keyed on `fanoutId` and `arrived` on `joinId`, both unambiguous, so **the only `regionOf`-sourced events any shipped policy acts on are `branch.exited` and `branch.lost`** - and they are acted on by exactly the two policies [P7](plans/p7-is-a-join-transitive-over-nested-fan-out.md) says support nesting. **(2) Consequence** - a nested flow through a real `Driver`, with `Driver.regionOf` reverted to the pre-W20 `.find` and the region maths left alone so id minting is not confounded. On an inner branch **lost**, `live-token` goes from *inner `satisfied`, outer `satisfied`* to inner **`waiting {live:1}`** - hung forever on a branch that died - and outer `satisfied` off one arrival plus **a decrement stolen from a region it never opened**, with the outer branch's real `arrived` never emitted at all. `live-token-requiring-arrival` shows the theft in `describe`: `arrived: 2` becomes `arrived: 1`. **`arrival-count`'s two statuses and both `describe`s are byte-identical**, and its blindness is by **construction rather than by sample** - it steps only on `branch.arrived`, whose region comes from a `joinId`-keyed lookup `regionOf` never touches. Same shape with an inner branch **exited**. **So nesting is NOT invisible to a policy**, and the one policy it is invisible to is the one P7 refuses nesting for - which means the refusal is not doing double duty. **Three things W22 gets from this, which is why the row was answered before its declaration.** (a) ADR 0030's *"`live-token` is transitive for free"* is true **given** innermost resolution - which token a terminal event spends is a string `regionOf` computes - so the ADR names the dependency rather than inheriting it from the region maths. (b) **A depth-2 matrix row built only from arrivals tests none of this** and runs green against the old `.find`, so W22's depth-2 rows must put the exit and the loss on an **inner** branch; anything else is W20's survivor reproduced one rung up. (c) `runCell` renders the stream as `type(regionId:branchPath)`, so **the `events` field discriminates under all three policies including the blind one** - if `arrival-count`'s depth-2 cells become *"refused"*, that discriminator leaves with them, which is an argument for a refused cell that records why rather than a blank one. **And this row's own census was wrong for the second time, in both directions — and W22 then made it wrong a THIRD time, which is the occurrence to carry.** *(Corrected 2026-09-02 at W22's Record, by its conformity reader: `regionOf` now has **six** call sites, because [ADR 0030](decisions/0030-a-join-is-transitive-and-a-dead-join-ends-the-branch-it-sits-in.md) rule 4 added one at the `unsatisfiable` exit, and the site this row names at `driver.ts:1022` moved to `:1069`. **The line number was stale before the item that wrote it had finished** — a count-in-prose written one step earlier in the same work item, falsified by that item's own code. The predicate, which does not go stale, is `grep -c "this\.regionOf(" src/driver/driver.ts`. **The new site is NOT in the gap this row describes**: at depth 3 it has two candidate regions and the checked-in cascade test discriminates them.)* The state of the row when it was written: `regionOf` was read at **five** identity-using sites, not four: [W21](work-items/w21-the-branch-path-is-a-path.md) added `driver.ts:1022` - the `fromRegion` a join's successor is entered with - **after this row was written**; and the truthiness-only fifth site the row named is **gone**, because W21 replaced `branchPathFor` with `pathOf`, which reaches `innermostRegionOf` through the region maths and never through `Driver.regionOf`. **Site :1022 is the one nothing tests.** Reverting `Driver.regionOf` wholesale costs **4 failures** in `npm test` and all four are W20's four site tests, because at depth 2 that site's argument is a `fanout` node with exactly one candidate region. **At depth 3 it discriminates**, measured on an inner join routing to an `end` inside the enclosing region: `exited(mid:1.1)` becomes `exited(outer:1.1)`, `mid` hangs at `{live:1}` and `outer` fires having been reached by one branch. That is P7's *"an item that claims depth 3 works owes a row"* with an address, and it is **W23's or a later plan's, not W22's** - P7 scopes the matrix and L6 at depth 2. **Resolved 2026-09-02 by measurement rather than by decision, at W22's declaration step; ADR 0030 is still W22's.** |
| Q114 | `settleJoin` routes out of a join with no branch path at all | **Closed by [W21](work-items/w21-the-branch-path-is-a-path.md), and it took two fixes.** Harmless at depth 1, where a join's successor is outside every region; inside a nested region the outer region recorded a branch's arrival as `-`. The first fix carried the path and passed `undefined` for the **region**, and the `end` arm attributes a `branch.exited` by the region alone — so an inner join routing to an `end` inside the enclosing region emitted **no terminal event for the outer branch** and the outer join sat at `live: 1` forever: **a valid definition that hangs**, found by the conformity reader. The second carried both. The fix's own assertion was then found blind at depth 2, where truncation is the identity; a depth-3 sibling shape collapses two arrivals into one. **Derived rather than threaded, so it needed no new state** |
| Q126 | `npm run gate`'s own wiring is checked for `check:*` and for nothing else, so a runtime can be dropped from it silently | **Closed by [W24](work-items/w24-the-gates-own-wiring.md), and the hole was wider than the row said.** `scripts/check-source.mjs:290` was the only expression in the repository reading `scripts.gate`, and its loop opened `if (!name.startsWith('check:')) continue` — so **8 of 15 entries were guarded and 7 were not**, and one level down nothing reached `typecheck`'s five sub-typechecks, `test:browser`'s `build:browser-tests`, or `lint`'s **argument list**. **`S12`** declares the chain as a named list and compares it to `scripts.gate` **both ways and in order**: a missing entry, an added entry, a **swapped** entry (count unmoved, set different), a duplicate, a reordering and an unparseable segment are each refused, and an unclassifiable segment stops the comparison rather than producing a mismatch that is true of the parse. **`S13`** takes the three composed lists the same way, with **two clauses derived from sources that are not the declaration** — every gate entry whose command runs another npm script must be declared in `COMPOSED` (from `package.json`), and every tree this script walks must be a `deno lint` target (from disk), so emptying `COMPOSED` fails rather than passing. All of it is negative-tested by `scripts/check-source-selftest.mjs`, which is the first self-test `check-source` has ever had — **24 probes, 20 META gradings, 3 SELF gradings**, each probe declaring the exact set of checks it must trip. **The gate chain is now 16 of 16 guarded**, and `check:source:selftest` is itself an entry in it. What this does **not** close is [Q102](#live), which W24 answers rather than resolves, and [Q132](#live), which is the nine `check-source` clauses still without a probe |
| Q12 | Is fan-out one feature or two? | **Two features over ONE region** — [ADR 0036](decisions/0036-a-map-is-a-second-node-kind-and-a-branch-path-inside-one-is-a-property-of-the-dispatch.md), decided by [W32](work-items/w32-what-a-map-fanout-is.md) and **open since P1**. `map` is a fifth node kind: it declares **one** branch and a run opens N instances of it, where a `fanout` declares N branches known at authoring time. **What the two share is the REGION; what differs is the whole of the fan-out** — every piece measured as carrying over (`reaches`, `branchOf`, `region(F)`, `after(F)`, `FD-V12`, `FD-V13`, `FD-V20`, `FD-V22`, the join, the id grammar) is region machinery, and every piece measured as breaking (how many branches, what an index means, whether `FD-V19` has an instance, whether the arity is in the document) is fan-out machinery. **The finding that decided it had not been measured before**: `branchOf` is keyed by branch NODE ID, so `branches: ["x","x","x"]` collapses to **one** entry and the repeated-entry encoding does not express N branches even to the layer that would honour it — while `FD-V19` fires `n(n-1)/2` times on it (1, 3, 6, 10, 15, 45 at N = 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10) and **0** times on a one-branch region. **`spec/task-id.md`'s grammar is unchanged**, and its section 1 heading is corrected: a branch index is a property of the NODE inside a `fanout` region and of the **DISPATCH** inside a `map` one, with `ID-R6` the refusal that keeps a derivation from guessing. **[Q27](#live) is untouched** and the ADR says so rather than leaving it to be inferred |
| Q141 | Can `check-scheduler`'s `D5` empty-parse clause be made inert with every probe still green? | **Yes, it could, and [W29](work-items/w29-the-two-self-tests-that-grade-nothing.md) closed it** — reproduced exactly before the fix (`if (expected.length === 0 && ...)` -> `if (false)`: exit **0** on the clean tree, **12/12 probes caught**, self-test exit 0). **The cause was that no probe could reach the clause**, not that the probes were weak: the clause fires only when the declaration parse yields nothing, and every probe read the installed package's own `.d.ts`, which always parses. The fix is an input override — `CHECK_SCHEDULER_DTS`, on the pattern of `CHECK_SOURCE_PKG` — plus a **hand-written** fixture declaring only `export type` lines, written from [ADR 0029](decisions/0029-the-scheduler-is-a-registry-dependency-and-nothing-reaches-the-working-tree.md)'s surface claim rather than captured from `node_modules` (`doc/workflow.md` section 5 rule 4). **The override closed three clauses, not one**: `D5`'s unreadable, empty-parse and missing-value-export clauses were all unreachable for the same reason, so taking Q141 alone would have built the mechanism and declined to use it. **The provenance probe confirms the fixture is load-bearing**: reading it from `node_modules` instead costs **1** failure. `check-scheduler-selftest.mjs` went from 12 probes and no grading to **18 probes and 21 gradings**. **P8's retro asked for a counter-case and this is it** — a check written for a defect that **was** live, against [W30](work-items/w30-the-clauses-nothing-probes.md)'s sixteen that are not |
| Q159 | Can a join policy tell a `map` region from a `fanout` region — and should it? | **NO, and the question turned out not to be about the KIND** — [W33](work-items/w33-a-map-at-run-time-arity.md), `spec/join-policy.md` v0.12 section 1. Exactly one shipped policy read `Region.branches` (`arrival-count`, for its `need`), and what it wanted was never *which kind opened this region* but **how many branches this region opened** — which the event stream already carries, one `branch.opened` per branch, all of them before any other event (`JP-R5`). So `N` is **counted** and the shape is unchanged. **Two alternatives were measured and rejected**: a fifth field carrying the arity would be a SECOND source for a number the events already give, with nothing able to see the two disagree; and handing a map region `branches` of length N would give `arrival-count` `need: 0` at N = 0 and **fire a join no branch ever reached**, which is character for character what [ADR 0025](decisions/0025-the-zero-arrival-join-is-reportable-and-arrival-counting-is-not-repaired.md) refused `JP-R7`'s variant for. **The sharpest form of the answer is that after the re-sourcing NOT ONE shipped policy reads its `Region` at all** — measured as `deno lint` reporting the `Region` import unused — so the interface need not say which kind opened a region because nothing reads the region |
| Q36 | What does a satisfied join carry downstream? | **The arrived branches' results, keyed by `branchPath`** — [W34](work-items/w34-what-a-branch-receives-and-what-a-join-carries.md), [ADR 0037](decisions/0037-what-a-branch-receives-is-a-property-of-the-fan-out-and-what-a-join-carries-is-a-property-of-the-region.md) rule 4, `JP-R12` in `spec/join-policy.md` section 4. **One entry per branch that emitted `branch.arrived`**, valued by what that branch carried in, keys in ascending segment-wise-numeric order. A branch routed away, lost or suspended has **no key**; a join nothing arrived at carries `{}`, which is live because `live-token` fires over an all-exited region. **The argument the `null` rested on is unchanged and is what the answer satisfies**: which terminal event satisfies a join is decided by fan-out completion order, so forwarding *whichever finished last* makes a run's data order-dependent — and carrying **all** of them keyed by branch is not picking one. **`settleJoin` still takes no payload parameter**, the guard that kept this a question rather than a convention; the driver records arrivals as they happen and reads the whole map at the exit. **The order-dependence did not vanish, it MOVED**: integer-like object keys are re-ordered ascending by the language, so a depth-1 join is deterministic for free, while dotted keys keep insertion order — which for a join is settlement order — so the hazard is **invisible at depth 1 and live at depth 2**, and `collectArrivals` pins the order. **A declared reducer is [Q165](#live)**, refused narrowly because a reducer is a function of what the join collects and cannot be specified before this rule was |
| Q154 | Does a fan-out hand ONE payload object to every branch, so a branch mutating it mutates its siblings' input? | **Yes it did, and it is closed in TWO different ways for the two features** — [W34](work-items/w34-what-a-branch-receives-and-what-a-join-carries.md), [ADR 0037](decisions/0037-what-a-branch-receives-is-a-property-of-the-fan-out-and-what-a-join-carries-is-a-property-of-the-region.md) rules 1-3, `JP-R11`. **The invariant is *no two nodes ever receive the same object*.** A `fanout` branch now gets its **own copy** of the value the fanout node received; a `map` instance gets **its own item**, as its own copy, so its instances no longer receive one value at all. **The asymmetry is the finding**: measured at N = 1000 over a 117 KiB payload, a clone per branch is **2207.94 ms** against **1.50 ms** for the shipped one-shared-clone — a factor of ~1470, and **quadratic**, because a map's arity and its payload grow together — while for a `fanout` the same fix is **linear in a number the DOCUMENT declares**: 9.3 / 14.2 / 23.0 / 44.3 ms at arity 2 / 3 / 5 / 10 over a 230 KiB payload against 4.5-4.8 ms for one clone. **The fix that is catastrophic for the feature that needed it is unnecessary for that feature and affordable for the other.** **The unmeasured half is measured**: on a real `FyflowScheduler`, **inline**, branch B's worker saw A's write in its own input *before it ran* and A's view gained B's write *after A returned*; **threaded (`maxThreads: 2`)**, each saw only its own. **`a.payload === b.payload` was `true` in BOTH** — the sharing is a driver-level fact and only its OBSERVABILITY is a pool option the flow author does not control, which is [Q61](#live)'s asymmetry in the opposite direction and the second instance of that class. That is why the rule is not conditioned on the pool: the driver cannot see it, which is `OR-R2`'s own reason for cloning *including where it is redundant*. **And *its own item* was not enough**: `structuredClone` preserves aliasing WITHIN one clone, so `{items: [x, x]}` survives the edge hop as an array holding one object twice — constructed and measured — which is why the rule says *its own copy* |
| Q300 | Do the two corpora disagree about what a schema can be checked against, and what does the wiring check do with the L4 corpus's `corpus://` workers? | **They disagree by DESIGN, and the check's live corpus is the L6 one** — [W73](work-items/w73-the-wiring-check-a-verdict-per-edge-and-two-boundaries-validated-once.md), 2026-09-18, the maintainer's answer to that item's stop-and-ask 1 (road B, against 14 invented schemas in 273 lines nothing could run). The L4 corpus stays UNCHECKED with totality asserted over its 56 consumers; the positive cases are a third corpus, `spec/examples/wiring/`, one document per verdict with its own registry of opposed schemas |
| Q301 | Is *the verdict says which* enough, or must a witness-based verdict be a distinct kind? | **The word, carried by a REQUIRED `sample` field, and a distinct kind refused** — [W73](work-items/w73-the-wiring-check-a-verdict-per-edge-and-two-boundaries-validated-once.md): every transformed verdict carries the sample's source, arity, value and result, `describeVerdict` prints the source on every line, and the suite asserts it on the fixture corpus and a constructed witness road |
| Q297 | `onRegionEvent` is a `Driver` option the RUNTIME does not forward, so a consumer on the recommended road cannot subscribe to region events at all | **Forwarded** — [W76](work-items/w76-what-start-accepts.md), and **no ADR**: P20 decision 4 had already settled that it is one name on `StartOptions`' `Pick` and one conditional spread, touching `RuntimeRegistry` not at all. **What made it a defect rather than a judgement is that it was the UNIQUE residue of a partition that is otherwise total** — and W76 re-ran that partition and found the plan's own table wrong: `2 / 6 / 6 / 7` is 21 slots over 18 members, because road (c) was read off `RuntimeOptions`' DECLARATION and four of its six are not `DriverOptions` members ([Q316](#live)). Derived from what `start` forwards it is `2 / 6 / 2 / 7 = 17`, with `onRegionEvent` the eighteenth. **The trap P20 fact 2 measured is closed in BOTH directions**: widening the `Pick` without the forwarding line was green on all five typecheckers, `npm test`, `check:source` AND `check:docs`, and it now fails a test that reads the shipped source and compares the `Pick` against `start`'s conditional spreads both ways — with the `??` road DERIVED rather than exempted by name, which is how `runId` turned out to be on it too. **And the forwarding is bound at every SHAPE rather than at one**: W76's adequacy reader narrowed it three ways for zero — four of six event variants dropped, every nested region's events dropped, every `map` region's events dropped — because both observers ran a two-branch `fanout` at depth 1. The answer is an EQUALITY rather than more topologies: what `onRegionEvent` receives IS the region subsequence of what `onFoldInput` receives, element for element, which is total over variant, kind and depth. *(Value equality, not identity: `freezeInput` gives `onFoldInput` its own frozen copy, so the two outbound paths carry equal values and not the same object — the first version of that assertion went red on its own first run.)* `AGENTS.md` section 10 is INVERTED rather than deleted and keeps a guard that fires if the answer changes back |

## Notes

**2026-08-30 - three conformity passes on W3, and each found a different shape of gap.** 85, then
146, then 100 mutations. The arc is worth recording because it is not the arc anyone predicts:

- **Pass 1** found an assertion that could not fail (`JP-R5`'s ordering test compared a multiset of
  event types), a wrongly-typed edge target that slipped all 23 refusals, and `RT-R4`'s mandated
  grep simply absent.
- **Pass 2** found that **the join could never fire.** There was no policy seam at all — and all 132
  assertions passed, because every test asserted on the event stream and none asserted that a run
  *completes*. The largest thing in the item was missing and the gate was green.
- **Pass 3** found the other half of that seam untested: both `settleJoin` calls on the non-arrival
  paths survived deletion, and **deleting one makes `live-token` give `arrival-count`'s answer on
  P1's own flow** — the false result `JP-R7` and `JP-R8` exist to forbid, reachable by a one-line
  regression nothing would report. Every seam test had used an arrival-counting stub, so every one
  of them asked only the question `arrival-count` can answer.

**The through-line: a suite cannot answer a question nothing in it asks.** Three times the code was
correct and the gap was in what the tests were shaped to look at. Pass 3 also found that *"the run
terminates"* — said by `FD-R1`, `ID-R1` and `OR-R6` — was implemented as a `throw` from a scheduler
listener, which `EventTarget.dispatchEvent` **discards**; that is `OR-R10`, and it was the third
appearance in this layer of the silent-stop signature `spec/flow-definition.md` section 3 refuses on
P1's own validity grounds.

**2026-08-30 - W3's conformity pass ran 85 mutations and 37 survived, which is a different kind of
result from the three that came before.** Those read documents and argued; this one broke the code
and watched. What it produced:

- **The four mutations that would have mattered most were caught.** `{...e.detail}` for the field
  copy, a missing `structuredClone`, keying on the event name instead of `detail.state`, and
  removing the duplicate-id refusal all fail the suite.
- **`JP-R5`'s ordering clause turned out to be unfalsifiable**, and not because the test was lazy:
  `FD-V9` forces every branch entry to be a `task`, and entering a task dispatches without emitting,
  so a conforming and a non-conforming driver are behaviourally identical today. Recorded in
  `spec/join-policy.md` rather than papered over with a passing assertion.
- **The corpus tests one case per *rule id* and the rules have clauses.** `FD-V7`'s two hardest
  clauses shadowed each other — its corpus case trips both, so deleting either was invisible.
  Clause-level unit tests now live in `test/definition/`, deliberately not in `spec/examples/`,
  which is W1's normative fixture.
- **A wrongly-typed edge target slipped every refusal** and produced a run that stopped mid-flow with
  no error and no `end` node — the exact silent stop `spec/flow-definition.md` section 3 refuses on
  P1's own validity grounds. The rule existed; nothing checked the type.
- **`FD-R1`'s prescribed test was impossible to construct**, which W3 annotated in a code comment
  instead of reporting as a spec conflict (`spec/README.md` rule 2). Now corrected in the spec.

**2026-08-30, later still again (3) - the three-runtime requirement was surfaced by the maintainer,
not by the documents.** `spec/runtime-targets.md` records it: Deno via JSR, Node and the browser via
npm, one source tree, mirroring `fyflow-scheduler` exactly - **because a consumer who can run the
scheduler in the browser and not the layer above it has been handed a library that does not compose
with the one it wraps.** Three things fell out of writing it down:

- **The ladder gained a second axis.** A rung is not green until it is green where it is required to
  run, and `doc/workflow.md` section 4 now says which rungs those are. **A rung reported green
  without naming its runtimes ran in one.**
- **`OR-R2` was portable by luck until it was checked.** `structuredClone` is global in all three,
  which is the single load-bearing entry in `RT-R3`'s API table - had it not been, the outcome
  record's copy rule would have needed a per-runtime implementation.
- **The obvious way to make a run id is refused.** `crypto.randomUUID()` is secure-context-only in
  browsers, so it works on `localhost` and fails on a plain-HTTP page - an environmental failure that
  no fixture test can reach (Q34).

**2026-08-30, later still again (2) - W1's spec-conformity pass found four blocking ambiguities and
one of them became [ADR 0004](decisions/0004-a-non-success-outcome-routes-only-where-an-edge-opts-in-and-user-action-suspends.md).**
The pass was run by a reader that had not written the spec, spec first and W1's own declaration
last. What it found is the argument for the pass existing:

- **The largest finding was a question nobody had asked.** `spec/` said in one document that every
  settlement routes and in another that a failure does not, and **both readings conformed**. Not an
  error in either document - a hole in the joint between them, which is exactly what
  `doc/workflow.md` section 7 rule 1 says the spec exists to close. It is now ADR 0004: routing is
  opt-in per terminal state, and `user_action` **suspends** rather than routes.
- **Q5's four cases were three, and two of them were one.** Reading the source rather than the
  register: `state = 'failed'` is assigned only inside `if (task.optional)`, so an optional branch
  resolving `null` and a branch failing are the same event, and a non-optional failure is
  `user_action`.
- **The most uncomfortable finding was symmetric to a guard W1 wrote itself.** `JP-R7` forbids
  repairing `arrival-count` with one line because it would answer Q4 by accident - and W1's own
  `live-token` carried exactly such a refinement, unremarked. Both are now pinned to Q4's wording
  and both refinements are Q24. **A rule that catches everyone except its author is worth noticing.**
- **One factual claim was wrong**, and it had already propagated into Q13's resolution above. See
  the Q13 row: the corruption is real, the observable is not what W1 wrote down.

**2026-08-30, later still again - W1 wrote `spec/` and the two questions it was sent to answer both
came back with the argument drawn from P1's own validity rather than from taste.** Q10's losing
candidate - *"the node is terminal for that branch"* - fails because a flow that stops halfway
inside a fan-out is indistinguishable from a join that never fires, which is the observation P1 is
built to make. Q13's answer got sharper by being read in the source rather than in `AGENTS.md`: a
reused id does not lose a task, it runs two and attributes one's outcome to the other.

**The finding worth carrying forward is what W1 nearly wrote.** *"Every fan-out branch must reach
its join"* is the first well-formedness rule anyone writes for a fan-out region, it reads as
obviously correct, and **it makes P1's branch-routed-away flow invalid input** - the plan would have
discovered its own case was unauthorable somewhere around W4. It is recorded as `FD-V14`, a held id
with no rule behind it, because the useful artifact is the argument for its absence.
**Q24 is the same hazard one layer down**: decrementing the arrival counter on `branch.exited` is
one line, makes arrival-counting survive P1's case, and would have returned *"the two policies are
equivalent"* as a passing test.

**2026-08-30, later still - `fyflow-scheduler` 0.3.0 landed and the register was re-verified against
the code, not the plan.** That distinction paid twice. **Q32 dissolved**: the plan proposed making
the orphaned-task drop *observable* and deferred the fix, but `634e23a` fixed it properly - so the
confounder that threatened P1's validity is gone, and W5's assertion drops from a gate to cheap
insurance. **[S1](scheduler-upstream.md) did not** - the dead `parents` field survived the whole
pass - **and was then answered directly the same day**: it stays dead and will eventually be
removed. The upstream register now has nothing left to raise, which is a fine place for it to sit
while P1 runs.

**ADR 0007 needed no rule changed, which is the outcome to be slightly suspicious of.** Every line
reference moved and one new dispatch site appeared, but all four rules held. The one substantive
shift went *against* comfort: timestamps arrived as `task.timestamp`, **a mutable field on the live
task**, so rule 1 protects more than it did at 0.2.1 rather than less. A retained detail now
misreports its own timestamp.

**2026-08-30, later - reading `../fyflow-scheduler/RESOURCE_EVENTS_PLAN.md` cost us an ADR and
raised two questions.** The plan is a counter-proposal to `fyflow-react-components`' upstream spec,
so most of it is about resource gauges we do not consume. Two paragraphs in it were ours:

- **§11, four lines long, is the one that mattered.** Event details are live references, and
  upstream has declined to fix that in code. Our whole architecture is a fold of a log, and a log of
  aliases is not a log — so this became
  [ADR 0007](decisions/0007-the-driver-normalizes-events-into-an-immutable-outcome-record.md)
  (Q31). Checking it in the source then turned up the sharper half, which the plan does not mention:
  a non-optional task out of retries dispatches **`task.failed` and `task.user_action` for the same
  object**, in that order, with `state` already set to `user_action` before either. That is
  [Q17](#live) answered by accident, and it would have been found by W3 debugging a double-count
  instead.
- **§4.4's exit (c)** — a blocked task dropped with no event — produces exactly the signature P1 is
  trying to attribute to a join policy. Q32.

**The general lesson, worth a rule if it recurs:** the useful part of an upstream document written
for a *different* consumer was not the part addressed to a consumer at all. Reading only the sections
that name our concerns would have missed both.

**2026-08-30 - the three gating questions are cleared, two of them by deciding.** Q1 and Q2 became
[ADR 0001](decisions/0001-routing-executes-in-the-driver-outside-the-scheduler.md) and
[ADR 0002](decisions/0002-a-flow-definition-is-data-and-a-predicate-is-registered-by-name.md).
Both went to the leaning, and in both cases the argument that carried it was **from the library's
own behaviour** rather than from taste - spawn cannot join, and `workerType` already resolves by
name against a registry.

**Q4 went the other way, and that is the more interesting decision.** The argument for live-token
accounting is strong, and it was refused as *sufficient*. [P1](plans/p1-what-satisfies-a-join.md)
builds both policies behind one interface and settles it with the branch-routed-away case as an
executable test. The cost is one round trip before fan-out work starts; what it buys is that the
answer cannot be a plausible-sounding mistake, which is the failure mode a strong argument produces
and nothing else catches. **Arrival-counting winning is a real possible outcome**, and would make
the simpler mechanism the right one.

**2026-08-29 - the register opens with nothing resolved, deliberately.** Q1-Q21 were written
alongside `doc/concept.md`. Only Q3 is answered, and it is a note rather than a decision.

The sibling repo `fyflow-react-components` resolved eleven of thirteen on day one, and that was
right there: its questions were about a **wire format for events that already exist**, so the
library's behaviour decided most of them. Ours are about **control flow the library does not have
at all** (concept section 2), so there is no existing behaviour to read the answer off. Copying the
sibling's cadence would mean deciding Q4 by preference and discovering the branch-routed-away case
during implementation.

**The finding that reframed the repo before it started.** `FLOW_PLANNING.md` - the input document -
is written against `parents`, `dependsOn` and a "DAG scheduler", none of which exist. That is not a
detail: it means this repo builds the ordering primitives rather than wrapping them, which is a
different project of a different size. Found by checking the plan against the library rather than
by starting to build, which is the cheap way to find it.

**`SpawnTaskConfig.parents` is the trap that made it plausible.** The field is declared
(`core/workerInterface.ts:50`) and read nowhere; the spawn path drops it silently. Anyone writing
`spawnTask({ ..., parents: [...] })` gets a clean typecheck and no dependency.
**Confirmed vestigial by the maintainer on 2026-08-30** - it stays dead and will eventually be
removed - so the finding is now a statement rather than an inference. [S1](scheduler-upstream.md).

**Three questions are shared with `fyflow-react-components` rather than merely similar** - Q17 with
their Q16 (`user_action` is missing from every design), Q19 with their Q14 (unbounded retention),
Q20 with their Q15 (one collector, one scheduler). Answers should be coordinated; two repos
answering `user_action` differently is a bug with two homes.
